f_{*}(S,\pi,B)=(S,f\circ\pi,B),\qquad(f_{*}(\varphi))_{s}^{s^{\prime}}=\varphi_{s}^{s^{\prime}}.

We have
\supp_{G}f_{*}(\varphi)=\supp_{G}\varphi
and
\supp_{1}f_{*}(\mathbf{B})=f(\supp_{1}\mathbf{B})
.
If
f
is
G
-equivariant, then
\supp_{2}f_{*}(\varphi)=f^{\times 2}(\supp_{2}\varphi)
.
If
f
is not
G
-equivariant, then there is no general formula that expresses

\supp_{2}f_{*}(\varphi)
directly in terms of
\supp_{2}\varphi
, but we have

(4.7)

\supp_{2}f_{*}(\varphi)\subseteq f^{\times 2}(\supp_{2}\varphi)\circ\Big\{{\begin{pmatrix}{f(gx)}\\
{gf(x)}\end{pmatrix}}\;\Big|\;x\in\supp_{1}\mathbf{B},g\in\supp_{G}\varphi\Big\}.

Thus to estimate
\supp_{2}f_{*}(\varphi)
we need to estimate the failure of equivariance
of
f
.

4.
b
.
G
-control structures

Definition 4.8
.

Let
G
be a group and
X
be a

G
-set. A
G
-control structure on
X
is a triple
\mathfrak{E}=(\mathfrak{E}_{1},\mathfrak{E}_{2},\mathfrak{E}_{G})

where

\bullet

\mathfrak{E}_{1}
is a collection of subsets of
X
that is closed under finite unions and
taking subsets;

\bullet

\mathfrak{E}_{2}
is a collection of subsets of
X\times X
that is closed under finite
unions, taking subsets, opposites, and composition;

\bullet

\mathfrak{E}_{G}
is a collection of subsets of
G
that is closed under finite unions,
taking subsets, elementwise inverses, and products.

We require in
addition that for
M\in\mathfrak{E}_{G}
,
E\in\mathfrak{E}_{2}
the product

M\cdot E:=\big\{{\begin{pmatrix}{gx^{\prime}}\\
{gx}\end{pmatrix}}\;\big|\;g\in M,{\begin{pmatrix}{x^{\prime}}\\
{x}\end{pmatrix}}\in E\big\}

belongs to
\mathfrak{E}_{2}
.

One might wonder if the condition that
\mathfrak{E}_{G}
is closed under elementwise inverses is
really necessary. We use this condition in the proof of
Lemma
4.21
.

Remark 4.9
.

In our examples
X
will always be a topological space and the elements of
\mathfrak{E}_{1}
will
always have finite intersections with compact subsets of
X
.

Remark 4.10
.

In almost all our examples
\mathfrak{E}_{G}
will be the collection of relatively compact subsets of
G
.
The only other example for
\mathfrak{E}_{G}
that we use is the
collection of all subsets of
G
. It will only be used in Section
14

for the proof of the Reduction Theorem
14.1
.

Example 4.11

(Trivial control structure)
.

Let
X
be
a
G
-set. We obtain a
G
-control structure
\mathfrak{E}
on
X
, where
\mathfrak{E}_{1}
is the
collection of all finite subsets,
\mathfrak{E}_{2}
is the collection of all subsets of the
diagonal in
X\times X
and
\mathfrak{E}_{G}
is the collection of all relatively compact subsets of
G
.

4.
c
.
The category
\mathcal{B}_{G}(\mathfrak{E})

Definition 4.12
.

Let
X
be a
G
-set,
\mathfrak{E}=(\mathfrak{E}_{1},\mathfrak{E}_{2},\mathfrak{E}_{G})
be a
G
-control structure on
X
,
and
\mathcal{B}
be a category with
G
-support. The additive category

\mathcal{B}_{G}(\mathfrak{E})
is the following subcategory of

\mathcal{B}_{G}(X)
.

(4.12a)

An object
\mathbf{B}=(S,\pi,B)

from
\mathcal{B}_{G}(X)
belongs to

\mathcal{B}_{G}(\mathfrak{E})
, iff it is finite over each point of

X
26
26

26

I.e.,
\pi\colon S\to X
is finite-to-one
,
\supp_{1}\mathbf{B}\in\mathfrak{E}_{1}
,

\supp_{2}\mathbf{B}\in\mathfrak{E}_{2}
and
\supp_{G}\mathbf{B}\in\mathfrak{E}_{G}
;

(4.12b)

A morphism
\varphi
in

\mathcal{B}_{G}(X)
between objects from
\mathcal{B}_{G}(\mathfrak{E})

belongs to
\mathcal{B}_{G}(\mathfrak{E})
iff

\supp_{2}\varphi\in\mathfrak{E}_{2}
,
\supp_{G}\varphi\in\mathfrak{E}_{G}
and
\varphi
is row
finite
27
27

27

As morphisms in
\mathcal{B}_{G}(X)
are already required to be column
finite, this means that
\varphi
is column and row finite, i.e., for fixed
s
there
are only finitely many
s^{\prime}
with
\varphi_{s}^{s^{\prime}}\neq 0
and for fixed
s^{\prime}
there are
only finitely many
s
with
\varphi_{s}^{s^{\prime}}\neq 0
.
.

Remark 4.13
.

For a smooth
G
-set
X
and the
G
-control structure
\mathfrak{E}
from
Example
4.11
the category
\mathcal{B}_{G}(\mathfrak{E})
is equivalent to

(\mathcal{B}[X])_{\oplus}
.

Remark 4.14

(Summands)
.

Let
\mathbf{B}=(S,\pi,B)\in\mathcal{B}_{G}(\mathfrak{E})
.
For
S_{0}\subseteq S
set
\mathbf{B}|_{S_{0}}:=(S_{0},\pi|_{S_{0}},B|_{S_{0}})
. Consider

\mathbf{B}|_{S_{0}}\xrightarrow{i}\mathbf{B}\xrightarrow{r}\mathbf{B}|_{S_{0}}\quad\text{with}\quad i_{s_{0}}^{s}=r_{s}^{s_{0}}=\begin{cases}\id_{B(s)}&s=s_{0};\\
0&s\neq s_{0}.\end{cases}

Then
\id_{\mathbf{B}|_{S_{0}}}=r\circ i
,
\supp_{1}\mathbf{B}|_{S_{0}}\subseteq\supp_{1}\mathbf{B}
,

\supp_{2}i=\supp_{2}r\subseteq\supp_{2}\mathbf{B}
. Altogether
\mathbf{B}|_{S_{0}}
is a direct
summand of
\mathbf{B}
in
\mathcal{B}_{G}(\mathfrak{E})
.

For
Y\subseteq X
we abbreviate
\mathbf{B}|_{Y}:=\mathbf{B}|_{\pi^{-1}(Y)}
.

Remark 4.15

(Corners)
.

Let
\varphi\colon\mathbf{B}=(S,\pi,B)\to\mathbf{B}^{\prime}=(S^{\prime},\pi^{\prime},B^{\prime})
.
For
Y,Y^{\prime}\subseteq X
we obtain summands

\mathbf{B}|_{Y}\xrightarrow{i_{Y}}\mathbf{B}\xrightarrow{r^{Y}}\mathbf{B}|_{Y}
and

\mathbf{B}^{\prime}|_{Y^{\prime}}\xrightarrow{i_{Y^{\prime}}}\mathbf{B}^{\prime}\xrightarrow{r^{Y^{\prime}}}\mathbf{B}|_{Y^{\prime}}
as in Remark
4.14
.
We define
\varphi_{Y}^{Y^{\prime}}
as the composition

\mathbf{B}|_{Y}\xrightarrow{i_{Y}}\mathbf{B}\xrightarrow{\varphi}\mathbf{B}^{\prime}\xrightarrow{r^{Y^{\prime}}}\mathbf{B}^{\prime}|_{Y^{\prime}}
.
Then

(\varphi_{Y}^{Y^{\prime}})_{s}^{s^{\prime}}=\begin{cases}\varphi_{s}^{s^{\prime}}&\pi(s)\in Y,\pi^{\prime}(s^{\prime})\in Y^{\prime};\\
0&\text{else}.\end{cases}

If
{\begin{pmatrix}{x}\\
{x^{\prime}}\end{pmatrix}}\in\supp_{2}\varphi_{Y}^{Y^{\prime}}
, then
x=\pi(s)
,
x^{\prime}=g\pi^{\prime}(s^{\prime})
for

\pi(s)\in Y
,
\pi^{\prime}(s^{\prime})\in Y^{\prime}
,
g\in\supp_{G}\varphi
and

g\in\supp\varphi_{s}^{s^{\prime}}
. Thus

(4.16)

\supp_{2}\varphi_{Y}^{Y^{\prime}}\subseteq Y^{\prime}\times(\supp_{G}\varphi)\cdot Y.

Lemma 4.17
.

Consider the situation of of Remark
4.15
. Suppose that for all

{\begin{pmatrix}{x^{\prime}}\\
{x}\end{pmatrix}}\in\supp_{2}\varphi
with
x\in(\supp_{G}\varphi)\cdot Y
we have

x^{\prime}\in Y^{\prime}
. Then

i_{Y^{\prime}}\circ\varphi_{Y}^{Y^{\prime}}=\varphi\circ i_{Y}\colon\mathbf{B}|_{Y}\to\mathbf{B}^{\prime}.

Proof.

We need to check that
\varphi_{s}^{s^{\prime}}\neq 0
with
s\in\pi^{-1}(Y)
implies

\pi^{\prime}(s^{\prime})\in Y^{\prime}
. If
\varphi_{s}^{s^{\prime}}\neq 0
, then there is

g\in\supp_{G}\varphi_{s}^{s^{\prime}}\subseteq\supp_{G}\varphi
and so

{\begin{pmatrix}{\pi^{\prime}(s^{\prime})}\\
{g\pi(s)}\end{pmatrix}}\in\supp_{2}\varphi
. Hence
\pi^{\prime}(s^{\prime})\in Y^{\prime}
by assumption.
∎

Remark 4.18

(Shifted copy)
.

Let
\mathbf{B}=(S,\pi,B)\in\mathcal{B}_{G}(\mathfrak{E})
.
Let
\sigma\colon S\to X
be a finite-to-one map.
Assume that
\sigma(S)\in\mathfrak{E}_{1}
and that
\pi
and
\sigma
are

\mathfrak{E}_{2}
-equivalent in the sense that

E:=\Big\{{\begin{pmatrix}{\pi(s)}\\
{\sigma(s)}\end{pmatrix}}\;\Big|\;s\in S\Big\}\in\mathfrak{E}_{2}.

Consider
\mathbf{B}_{\sigma}:=(S,\sigma,B)
and

\mathbf{B}_{\sigma}\xrightarrow{\varphi}\mathbf{B}\xrightarrow{\psi}\mathbf{B}_{\sigma}\quad\text{with}\quad\varphi_{s}^{s^{\prime}}=\psi_{s}^{s^{\prime}}=\begin{cases}\id_{B(s)}&s=s^{\prime};\\
0&s\neq s^{\prime}.\end{cases}

Then
\supp_{1}\mathbf{B}_{\sigma}=\sigma(S_{0})\in\mathfrak{E}_{1}
,

\id_{\mathbf{B}_{\sigma}}=\psi\circ\varphi
,
\id_{\mathbf{B}}=\varphi\circ\psi
, and

\displaystyle\supp_{2}\varphi

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ \Big\{{\begin{pmatrix}{\pi(s)}\\
{g\sigma(s)}\end{pmatrix}}\;\Big|\;\mid s\in S,g\in\supp_{G}B(s)\Big\}\subseteq\supp_{2}\mathbf{B}\circ(\supp_{G}\mathbf{B})\cdot E\in\mathfrak{E}_{2};

\displaystyle\supp_{2}\psi

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Big\{{\begin{pmatrix}{\sigma(s)}\\
{g\pi(s)}\end{pmatrix}}\;\Big|\;\mid s\in S,g\in\supp_{G}B(s)\Big\}\subseteq E^{{\mathrm{op}}}\circ\supp_{2}\mathbf{B}\in\mathfrak{E}_{2}.

Altogether,
\mathbf{B}_{\sigma}
and
\mathbf{B}
are canonically isomorphic in
\mathcal{B}_{G}(\mathfrak{E})
.
We call
\mathbf{B}_{\sigma}

a shifted copy of
\mathbf{B}
.

4.
d
.
Quotients

Definition 4.19
.

Let
\mathfrak{E}=(\mathfrak{E}_{1},\mathfrak{E}_{2},\mathfrak{E}_{G})
be a
G
-control
structure on
X
. Let
\mathcal{Y}
be a collection of subsets
Y
of
X
that is closed
under finite unions and taking subsets. Suppose also that for
M\in\mathfrak{E}_{G}
and

Y\in\mathcal{Y}
we have
M\cdot Y\in\mathcal{Y}
. We obtain a
G
-control structure

\mathfrak{E}|_{\mathcal{Y}}:=(\mathfrak{E}_{1}|_{\mathcal{Y}},\mathfrak{E}_{2},\mathfrak{E}_{G})
on
X
where

\mathfrak{E}_{1}|_{\mathcal{Y}}:=\{F\cap Y\mid F\in\mathfrak{E}_{1},Y\in\mathcal{Y}\}
.

Definition 4.20
.

In the situation of Definition
4.19
the
category
\mathcal{B}_{G}(\mathfrak{E}|_{\mathcal{Y}})
is a full subcategory of

\mathcal{B}_{G}(\mathfrak{E})
and we define

\mathcal{B}_{G}(\mathfrak{E},\mathcal{Y}):=\mathcal{B}_{G}(\mathfrak{E})\;\big/\;\mathcal{B}_{G}(\mathfrak{E}|_{\mathcal{Y}}).

Lemma 4.21
.

In the situation of
Definition
4.19
the category
\mathcal{B}_{G}(\mathfrak{E})
is

\mathcal{B}_{G}(\mathfrak{E}|_{\mathcal{Y}})
-filtered,
see Definition
2.1
.

Proof.

Let
\mathbf{U}_{-}\xrightarrow{\varphi_{-}}\mathbf{B}\xrightarrow{\varphi_{+}}\mathbf{U}_{+}
be morphisms in

\mathcal{B}_{G}(\mathfrak{E})

with
\mathbf{U}_{-},\mathbf{U}_{+}\in\mathcal{B}_{G}(\mathfrak{E}|_{\mathcal{Y}})
.
Write

\mathbf{U}_{\pm}=(S_{\pm},\pi_{\pm},U_{\pm})
and
\mathbf{B}=(T,\rho,B)
. Let
T_{-}\subset T

consist of all
t
, for which there is
s\in S_{-}
with
(\varphi_{-})_{s}^{t}\neq 0
, and let

T_{+}\subset T
consist of all
t
, for which there is
s_{+}\in S_{+}
with

(\varphi_{+})^{s}_{t}\neq 0
. Set
T_{0}:=T_{-}\cup T_{+}
. We obtain the summand

\mathbf{B}|_{T_{0}}
of
\mathbf{B}
as in Remark
4.14
. Clearly
\varphi_{\pm}
factors
over
\mathbf{B}|_{T_{0}}
. It suffices now to check that
\mathbf{B}|_{T_{0}}
is isomorphic to an
object in
\mathcal{B}_{G}(\mathfrak{E}|_{\mathcal{Y}})
. We now use a shifted copy of
\mathbf{B}|_{T_{0}}
. Choose

\sigma\colon T_{0}\to X
such that for every
t\in T_{0}
there are either
s\in S_{-}
,

g\in G
with
(\varphi_{-})_{s}^{t}(g)\neq 0
and
\sigma(t)=g\pi_{-}(s)
or there are

s\in S_{+}
,
g\in G
with
(\varphi_{+})^{s}_{t}(g)\neq 0
and

\sigma(t)=(g)^{-1}\pi_{+}(s)
. Then
\sigma
is finite-to-one because
\varphi
is
column finite and
\varphi^{\prime}
is row finite. Our choice of
\sigma
implies

E:=\Big\{{\begin{pmatrix}{\rho(t)}\\
{\sigma(t)}\end{pmatrix}}\;\Big|\;t\in T_{0}\Big\}\subseteq\supp_{2}\varphi_{-}\cup(\supp_{G}\varphi_{+})^{-1}\cdot(\supp_{2}\varphi_{+})^{{\mathrm{op}}}.

Thus
28
28

28

Here we use in particular that
\mathfrak{E}_{G}
is closed under pointwise
inverses.

E\in\mathfrak{E}_{2}
and the shifted copy
(\mathbf{B}|_{T_{0}})_{\sigma}
of

\mathbf{B}|_{T_{0}}
is isomorphic to
\mathbf{B}
, see Remark
4.18
. By
construction

\supp_{1}(\mathbf{B}|_{T_{0}})_{\sigma}=\sigma(T_{0})\subseteq\\
(\supp_{G}\varphi_{-})\cdot\supp_{1}(\varphi_{-})\cup(\supp_{G}\varphi_{+})^{-1}\cdot\supp_{1}(\varphi_{+})\in\mathcal{Y}

and
(\mathbf{B}|_{T_{0}})_{\sigma}\in\mathcal{B}_{G}(\mathfrak{E}|_{\mathcal{Y}})
as required.
∎

Combining Lemma
4.21
with
Theorem
2.2
we obtain a fibration sequence

(4.22)

\mathbf{K}(\mathcal{B}_{G}(\mathfrak{E}|_{\mathcal{Y}}))\to\mathbf{K}(\mathcal{B}_{G}(\mathfrak{E}))\to\mathbf{K}(\mathcal{B}_{G}(\mathfrak{E},\mathcal{Y})).

More general, if
\mathcal{Y}_{0}
is another collection of subsets of
X
also satisfying the
conditions from Definition
4.20
, and if
\mathcal{Y}_{0}\subseteq\mathcal{Y}
, then

(4.23)

\mathbf{K}(\mathcal{B}_{G}(\mathfrak{E}|_{\mathcal{Y}},\mathcal{Y}_{0}))\to\mathbf{K}(\mathcal{B}_{G}(\mathfrak{E},\mathcal{Y}_{0}))\to\mathbf{K}(\mathcal{B}_{G}(\mathfrak{E},\mathcal{Y}))

is a fibration sequence
29
29

29

(
4.22
) applies to the two vertical
and the upper horizontal sequence in

\textstyle{\mathcal{B}_{G}((\mathfrak{E}|_{\mathcal{Y}})|_{\mathcal{Y}_{0}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{=}
\textstyle{\mathcal{B}_{G}(\mathfrak{E}|_{\mathcal{Y}_{0}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{B}_{G}(\mathfrak{E}|_{\mathcal{Y}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{B}_{G}(\mathfrak{E})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{B}_{G}(\mathfrak{E},\mathcal{Y})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{=}
\textstyle{\mathcal{B}_{G}(\mathfrak{E}|_{\mathcal{Y}},\mathcal{Y}_{0})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{B}_{G}(\mathfrak{E},\mathcal{Y}_{0})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{B}_{G}(\mathfrak{E},\mathcal{Y})}

and the lower horizontal sequence is therefore a fibration sequence in K-theory.
.
We will refer to sequences of additive categories of the form

\mathcal{B}_{G}(\mathfrak{E}|_{\mathcal{Y}},\mathcal{Y}_{0})\to\mathcal{B}_{G}(\mathfrak{E},\mathcal{Y}_{0})\to\mathcal{B}_{G}(\mathfrak{E},\mathcal{Y})

as Karoubi sequences. K-theory takes Karoubi sequences to a fibration sequences of
spectra.

4.
e
.
Excision

Let
\mathfrak{E}
be a
G
-control structure on
X
. Let
\mathcal{Y}_{0}
and
\mathcal{Y}_{1}
be two
collections of subsets of
X
satisfying the assumptions in Definition
4.19
.
Then
\mathcal{Y}_{0}\cap\mathcal{Y}_{1}
also satisfies these assumptions. The union of
\mathcal{Y}_{0}
and

\mathcal{Y}_{1}
may not, but we abuse notation and define
\mathcal{Y}_{0}\cup\mathcal{Y}_{1}
as the
collection of all sets
Y_{0}\cup Y_{1}
with
Y_{i}\in\mathcal{Y}_{i}
.
There is a natural functor

(4.24)

\mathcal{B}_{G}(\mathfrak{E}|_{\mathcal{Y}_{1}},\mathcal{Y}_{0}\cap\mathcal{Y}_{1})\to\mathcal{B}_{G}(\mathfrak{E}|_{\mathcal{Y}_{0}\cup\mathcal{Y}_{1}},\mathcal{Y}_{0}).

It is not difficult to check that (
4.24
) is surjective on morphism
sets and on isomorphism classes of objects, but it may fail to be injective on morphism
sets. There are different possible assumptions that guarantee
that (
4.24
) is an equivalence. We later use the following.

Lemma 4.25
.

Assume that for all
Y_{1}\in\mathcal{Y}_{1}
,

E\in\mathfrak{E}_{2}
there are
Y_{0}\in\mathcal{Y}_{0}
,
Y_{1}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{Y}_{1}
such that

Y_{1}\subseteq Y_{1}^{\prime}\cup Y_{0}
and

(Y_{1}^{\prime})^{E}:=\{y^{\prime}\in X\mid\exists y\in Y\;\text{with}\;{\begin{pmatrix}{y^{\prime}}\\
{y}\end{pmatrix}}\in E\}\in\mathcal{Y}_{1}.

Then (
4.24
) is an equivalence.

Proof.

We only need to discuss faithfulness on morphisms. Let
\varphi\colon\mathbf{B}\to\mathbf{B}^{\prime}

be a morphism in
\mathcal{B}_{G}(\mathfrak{E}|_{\mathcal{Y}_{1}})
. Assume that
\varphi
can be factored in

\mathcal{B}_{G}(\mathfrak{E}|_{\mathcal{Y}_{0}\cup\mathcal{Y}_{1}})
as

\mathbf{B}\xrightarrow{\varphi_{-}}\mathbf{X}\xrightarrow{\varphi_{+}}\mathbf{B}

where
\mathbf{X}\in\mathcal{B}_{G}(\mathfrak{E}|_{\mathcal{Y}_{0}})
, i.e.,
\supp_{1}\mathbf{X}\in\mathcal{Y}_{0}
. We need to
produce such a factorization over an
\mathbf{X}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{B}_{G}(\mathfrak{E}|_{\mathcal{Y}_{0}\cap\mathcal{Y}_{1}})
,
i.e.,
\supp_{1}\mathbf{X}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{Y}_{0}\cap\mathcal{Y}_{1}
. We have

Y_{1}:=\supp_{G}\varphi\cdot\supp_{1}\mathbf{B}\in\mathcal{Y}_{1}
and

E:=(\supp_{G}\varphi_{-})^{-1}\cdot\supp_{2}\in\mathbf{E}_{2}
. Applying the assumption we find

Y_{0}\in\mathcal{Y}_{0}
,
Y_{1}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{Y}_{1}
such that
Y_{1}\subseteq Y_{1}^{\prime}\cup Y_{0}
and

(Y_{1}^{\prime})^{E}\in\mathcal{Y}_{1}
. Now
\mathbf{B}=\mathbf{B}|_{Y_{1}^{\prime}}\oplus\mathbf{B}|_{Y_{0}}
. Write

\pi_{Y_{1}^{\prime}},\pi_{Y_{0}}\colon\mathbf{B}\to\mathbf{B}
for the corresponding projections. Then

\varphi-\varphi\circ\pi_{Y_{1}^{\prime}}=\varphi\circ\pi_{Y_{0}}
factors over

\mathbf{B}|_{Y_{0}}\in\mathcal{B}_{G}(\mathfrak{E}|_{\mathcal{Y}_{0}})
. This allows us to replace
\varphi
with

\varphi\circ\pi_{Y_{1}^{\prime}}
and
\varphi_{-}
with
\varphi_{-}\circ\pi_{Y_{1}^{\prime}}
, or put
differently, we may assume without loss of generality
\supp_{1}\mathbf{B}\subseteq Y_{1}^{\prime}
.

Let now
Y^{\prime}_{0}\subseteq\supp_{1}\mathbf{X}
consist of all
y_{0}\in\supp_{1}\mathbf{X}
for which
there are
y_{1}\in\supp_{1}\mathbf{B}
and
g\in\supp_{G}\varphi_{-}
with

{\begin{pmatrix}{y_{0}}\\
{gy_{1}}\end{pmatrix}}\in\supp_{2}\varphi_{-}
, i.e., the matrix entry of
\varphi
for

{\begin{pmatrix}{y_{0}}\\
{y_{1}}\end{pmatrix}}
is non-trivial. Then
\varphi_{-}
factors canonically over the
inclusion
\mathbf{X}|_{Y^{\prime}_{0}}\to\mathbf{X}
. This allows us to replace
\mathbf{X}
with

\mathbf{X}|_{Y^{\prime}_{0}}
. It remains to check that
Y^{\prime}_{0}\in\mathcal{Y}_{0}\cap\mathcal{Y}_{1}
. As

Y^{\prime}_{0}\subseteq\supp_{1}\mathbf{X}\in\mathcal{Y}_{0}
we have
Y^{\prime}_{0}\in\mathcal{Y}_{0}
. For

y_{0}\in Y^{\prime}_{0}
there are
y_{1}\in\supp_{1}\mathbf{B}\subseteq Y^{\prime}_{1}
and

g\in\supp_{G}\varphi_{-}
with
{\begin{pmatrix}{y_{0}}\\
{gy_{1}}\end{pmatrix}}\in\supp_{2}\varphi_{-}
. This implies

y_{0}\in(Y^{\prime}_{1})^{E}
. As
(Y^{\prime}_{1})^{E}\in\mathcal{Y}_{1}
we now also have
Y^{\prime}_{0}\in\mathcal{Y}_{1}
.
∎

Lemma 4.26
.

Let
\mathcal{Y}
and
\mathcal{Y}_{0}
be two collections
of subsets of
X
satisfying the assumptions from Definition
4.19
. Then the
canonical functor

\mathcal{B}_{G}(\mathfrak{E}|_{\mathcal{Y}_{0}},\mathcal{Y})\to\mathcal{B}_{G}(\mathfrak{E}|_{\mathcal{Y}_{0}\cup\mathcal{Y}},\mathcal{Y})

is an equivalence.

Proof.

The only difference between the two categories is that the category on the right has
more objects, i.e., objects with support in
\mathcal{Y}
. But, by Definition, these
additional objects are trivial (isomorphic to zero).
∎

Lemma 4.27
.

Let
\mathcal{Y},\mathcal{Y}_{0},\mathcal{Y}_{1}
be
collections of subsets of
X
satisfying the assumptions in Definition
4.19
.
Assume that the condition from Lemma
4.25
holds. Then

\textstyle{\mathbf{K}(\mathcal{B}_{G}(\mathfrak{E}|_{\mathcal{Y}_{0}\cap\mathcal{Y}_{1}},\mathcal{Y}))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathbf{K}(\mathcal{B}_{G}(\mathfrak{E}|_{\mathcal{Y}_{1}},\mathcal{Y}))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathbf{K}(\mathcal{B}_{G}(\mathfrak{E}|_{\mathcal{Y}_{0}},\mathcal{Y}))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathbf{K}(\mathcal{B}_{G}(\mathfrak{E}|_{\mathcal{Y}_{0}\cup\mathcal{Y}_{1}},\mathcal{Y}))}

is a homotopy pushout square.

Proof.

We first argue that we may assume that
\mathcal{Y}\subseteq\mathcal{Y}_{0}\cap\mathcal{Y}_{1}
. Indeed,
Lemma
4.26
allows us to replace
\mathcal{Y}_{0}
with

\mathcal{Y}_{0}\cup\mathcal{Y}
and
\mathcal{Y}_{1}
with
\mathcal{Y}_{1}\cup\mathcal{Y}
. It is not difficult to
check that the condition from Lemma
4.25
is preserved.

Now the horizontal homotopy cofibers of the above diagram are determined
by (
4.23
) and are given by

\mathbf{K}(\mathcal{B}_{G}(\mathfrak{E}|_{\mathcal{Y}_{0}},\mathcal{Y}_{0}\cap\mathcal{Y}_{1}))
and

\mathbf{K}(\mathcal{B}_{G}(\mathfrak{E}|_{\mathcal{Y}_{0}\cup\mathcal{Y}_{1}},\mathcal{Y}_{1}))
. The excision result of
Lemma
4.25
applies to show that the induced maps between the
homotopy cofibers is an equivalence. This implies the assertion.
∎

4.
f
.
Swindles

Let
\mathfrak{E}
be a
G
-control structure on
X
. Let
\mathcal{Y}
be a collection of subsets of

X
satisfying the assumptions in Definition
4.19
.
Sometimes it is easy to produce Eilenberg swindles on

\mathcal{B}_{G}(\mathfrak{E},\mathcal{Y})
. Often such a swindle either comes from some map
f\colon X\to X

that pushes everything to
\infty
, as in
(4.28a)
below. We will
use the following formal result later on.

Lemma 4.28
.

Assume that there is a
G
-map
f\colon X\to X

satisfying

(4.28a)

for all
x\in X
there is
n
such that

(f^{\circ n})^{-1}(x)=\emptyset
;

(4.28b)

for all
Y\in\mathfrak{E}_{1}
we have

\bigcup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}f^{n}(Y)\in\mathfrak{E}_{1}
;

(4.28c)

for all
Y\in\mathcal{Y}
we have

\bigcup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}f^{n}(Y)\in\mathcal{Y}
;

(4.28d)

for all
E\in\mathfrak{E}_{2}
we have

\bigcup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}(f\times f)^{\circ n}(E)\in\mathfrak{E}_{2}
;

(4.28e)

for all
M\in\mathfrak{E}_{G}
we have

\{{\begin{pmatrix}{f(x)}\\
{gx}\end{pmatrix}}\mid x\in X;g\in M\}\in\mathfrak{E}_{2}
.

Then the K-theory of
\mathcal{B}_{G}(\mathfrak{E},\mathcal{Y})
is trivial.

Proof.

For
\mathbf{B}=(S,\pi,B)\in\mathcal{B}_{G}(\mathfrak{E})
we define

\mathbf{B}^{\infty}=(S^{\infty},\pi^{\infty},B^{\infty})
where
S^{\infty}=S\times\mathbb{N}
,

\pi^{\infty}(s,n)=f^{\circ n}(s)
,
B^{\infty}(s,t)=B(s)
.
Assumption
(4.28a)
implies that
\pi^{\infty}
is finite-to-one.
Assumption
(4.28b)
implies
\supp_{1}\mathbf{B}^{\infty}\in\mathfrak{E}_{1}
.
Assumption
(4.28d)
implies
\supp_{2}\mathbf{B}^{\infty}\in\mathfrak{E}_{2}
. Thus

\mathbf{B}^{\infty}\in\mathcal{B}_{G}(\mathfrak{E})
.

For
\varphi\colon(S,\pi,B)\to(S^{\prime},\pi^{\prime},B^{\prime})\in\mathcal{B}_{G}(\mathfrak{E})
we define

\varphi^{\infty}
by
(\varphi^{\infty})^{s^{\prime},t^{\prime}}_{s,t}:=\varphi_{s}^{s^{\prime}}
.
Assumption
(4.28d)
implies
\supp_{2}\varphi^{\infty}\in\mathfrak{E}_{2}
. As

\varphi^{\infty}
is also row and column finite (because
\varphi
is), we have

\varphi^{\infty}\in\mathcal{B}_{G}(\mathfrak{E})
. Compatibility with composition is straight forward
and we obtain an endofunctor
(\--)^{\infty}
of
\mathcal{B}_{G}(\mathfrak{E})
. For

\mathbf{B}=(S,\pi,B)\in\mathcal{B}_{G}(\mathfrak{E})
let
i_{\mathbf{B}}\colon\mathbf{B}\to\mathbf{B}^{\infty}
and

\sh_{\mathbf{B}}\colon\mathbf{B}^{\infty}\to\mathbf{B}^{\infty}
be induced by the inclusions

\displaystyle S

\displaystyle\to

\displaystyle S\times\mathbb{N},\quad s\mapsto(s,0);

\displaystyle S\times\mathbb{N}

\displaystyle\to

\displaystyle S\times\mathbb{N},\quad(s,t)\mapsto(s,t+1).

Clearly,
i_{\mathbf{B}}\in\mathcal{B}_{G}(\mathfrak{E})
. Assumption
(4.28e)
(for

M=\supp_{G}\mathbf{B}
) implies that
\sh_{\mathbf{B}}\in\mathcal{B}_{G}(\mathfrak{E})
.
Now
i\oplus\sh
is a
natural isomorphism
(\--)^{\infty}\oplus\id_{\mathcal{B}_{G}(\mathfrak{E})}\cong(\--)^{\infty}
.
Altogether we defined a swindle on
\mathcal{B}_{G}(\mathfrak{E})
.
Assumption
(4.28c)
ensures that this swindle descends to

\mathcal{B}_{G}(\mathfrak{E},\mathcal{Y})
.
∎

We will need a variation of the swindle from Lemma
4.28
, where we can
swindle towards some
Z\in\mathcal{Y}
instead of towards
\infty
30
30

30

Of course,
Lemma
4.28
is implied by Lemma
4.29
by taking

Z=\emptyset
.
.

Lemma 4.29
.

Assume that there are a
G
-map

f\colon X\to X
and
Z\in\mathcal{Y}
satisfying

(4.29a)

for all
x\in X\setminus Z
there is
n
such
that
(f^{\circ n})^{-1}(x)=\emptyset
;

(4.29b)

for all
Y\in\mathfrak{E}_{1}
we have

\bigcup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}Y_{n}\in\mathfrak{E}_{1}
where
Y_{0}=Y
and

Y_{n+1}=f(Y_{n}\setminus Z)
;

(4.29c)

for all
Y\in\mathcal{Y}
we have

\bigcup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}Y_{n}\in\mathcal{Y}
where
Y_{0}=Y
and

Y_{n+1}=f(Y_{n}\setminus Z)
;

(4.29d)

for all
E\in\mathfrak{E}_{2}
we have

\bigcup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}(f\times f)^{\circ n}(E)\in\mathfrak{E}_{2}
;

(4.29e)

for all
M\in\mathfrak{E}_{G}
we have

\{{\begin{pmatrix}{gx}\\
{f(x)}\end{pmatrix}}\mid x\in X;g\in M\}\in\mathfrak{E}_{2}
.

Then the K-theory of
\mathcal{B}_{G}(\mathfrak{E},\mathcal{Y})
is trivial.

Proof.

In
\mathcal{B}_{G}(\mathfrak{E},\mathcal{Y})
we have
\mathbf{B}|_{Z}\cong 0
and so

\mathbf{B}\cong\mathbf{B}|_{X\setminus Z}
. Thus we can systematically get ride of everything
over
Z
. A swindle on
\mathcal{B}_{G}(\mathfrak{E},\mathcal{Y})
can be constructed almost verbatim as in
Lemma
4.28
. The only difference is that we use a subset

S^{\infty}_{-}
in place of
S^{\infty}
. To define this subset set
S_{0}:=S
,

\pi_{0}:=\pi
and inductively
S_{n+1}:=(\pi_{n})^{-1}(X\setminus Z)
,

\pi_{n+1}:=(f\circ\pi_{n})|_{S_{n+1}}
. Then

S^{\infty}_{-}:=\bigcup_{n}S_{n}\times\{n\}
. We remark that
\pi_{n}
is just the
restriction of
\pi^{\infty}
to
S_{n}\cong S_{n}\times\{n\}
.

After restricting everything from
S^{\infty}
to
S^{\infty}_{-}
, we obtain a swindle on

\mathcal{B}_{G}(\mathfrak{E},\mathcal{Y})
as before.
∎

5.
The
{\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.0pt\mathrm{vcy}}
-Farrell–Jones Conjecture

5.
a
.
Delooping

In order to formulate the
{\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.0pt\mathrm{vcy}}
-Farrell–Jones Conjecture we will need categories

\matheurm{C}_{G}(P;\mathcal{B})
for
P\in\matheurm{P}\hskip-2.5pt_{+}\hskip-1.2pt{\mathcal{A}\hskip-0.3pt\mathrm{ll}}(G)
. To prepare for their construction in
Definition
5.9
later on we discuss the Pedersen-Weibel delooping of K-theory
from
[
45
]
in our set-up. Let
X
be a
G
-set. Define the

G
-control structure

\mathfrak{E}^{\text{pw}}(X)=(\mathfrak{E}^{\text{pw}}_{1}(X),\mathfrak{E}^{\text{pw}}_{2}(X),\mathfrak{E}^{\text{pw}}_{G}(X))
on
X\times\mathbb{N}

as follows.

\bullet

\mathfrak{E}^{\text{pw}}_{1}(X)
is the collection of all subsets
F
of
X\times\mathbb{N}
for which

F\cap X\times\{t\}
is finite for all
t
;

\bullet

\mathfrak{E}^{\text{pw}}_{2}(X)
is the collection of all
E\subseteq\mathbb{N}\times\mathbb{N}

with

\supp\Big\{|t-t^{\prime}|\;\Big|\;{\begin{pmatrix}{x^{\prime},t^{\prime}}\\
{x,t}\end{pmatrix}}\in E\Big\}<\infty;

\bullet

\mathfrak{E}^{\text{pw}}_{G}(X)
is the collection of all relatively compact subsets of
G
.

Let
\mathcal{Y}
be the collection of subsets
Y
of
X\times\mathbb{N}
that are contained in

X\times\{1,\dots,N\}
for some
N
(depending on
Y
). Let
\mathcal{B}
be a category with

G
-support. Then

\mathcal{B}_{G}(\mathfrak{E}^{\text{pw}}(X)|_{\mathcal{Y}})\to\mathcal{B}_{G}(\mathfrak{E}^{\text{pw}}(X))\to\mathcal{B}_{G}(\mathfrak{E}^{\text{pw}}(X),\mathcal{Y})

is a Karoubi sequence. Applying K-theory we obtain a fibration sequence,
see (
4.22
).
Combining this sequence with Lemma
5.1
below we obtain

\Omega\mathbf{K}\big(\mathcal{B}_{G}(\mathfrak{E}^{\text{pw}}(X),\mathcal{Y})\big)\simeq\mathbf{K}(\mathcal{B})
.
Lemma
5.1
is standard, but the proof is instructive as we
will use variation thereof later on.

Lemma 5.1
.

(5.1a)

There is an equivalence

\mathcal{B}_{G}(\mathfrak{E}^{\text{pw}}(X)|_{\mathcal{Y}})\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathcal{B}_{\oplus}

defined by
(S,\pi,B)\mapsto\oplus_{s\in S}B(s)
;

(5.1b)

The K-theory of

\mathcal{B}_{G}(\mathfrak{E}^{\text{pw}}(X))
is trivial.

Proof.

If
(S,\pi,B)\in\mathcal{B}_{G}(\mathfrak{E}^{\text{pw}}(X))
, then by definition of
\mathfrak{E}^{\text{pw}}_{1}(X)
, for
each
t\in\mathbb{N}
,
\pi^{-1}(X\times\{t\})
is finite. If

(S,\pi,B)\in\mathcal{B}_{G}(\mathfrak{E}^{\text{pw}}(X)|_{\mathcal{Y}})
, then in addition
\pi^{-1}(X\times\{t\})
is
non-empty for only finitely many
t
. Thus
S
is finite and

(S,\pi,B)\mapsto\oplus_{s\in S}B(s)
defines a functor

\mathcal{B}_{G}(\mathfrak{E}^{\text{pw}}(X)|_{\mathcal{Y}})\to\mathcal{B}_{\oplus}
. It is straight forward to check that
this functor is an equivalence.

Let
f\colon X\times\mathbb{N}\to X\times\mathbb{N}
be the shift
(x,t)\mapsto(x,t+1)
. It
is not difficult to check that
f
induces an Eilenberg swindle on

\mathcal{B}_{G}(\mathfrak{E}^{\text{pw}}(X))
. More precisely, Lemma
4.28
applies to
f

and
\mathcal{B}_{G}(\mathfrak{E}^{\text{pw}}(X))
.
∎

Recall from Subsection
1.m
that for the Farrell–Jones
Conjecture for a discrete group
\Gamma
the group rings over virtually cyclic subgroups
of
\Gamma
play a central role. For td-groups the K-theory of the categories

\matheurm{C}_{G}(P;\mathcal{B})
will take this role. Let
G
be a td-group and
V\in{\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.0pt\mathrm{vcy}}
. Our
difficulty is that, if
V
is closed but not open in
G
, there is no inclusion of

\mathcal{H}(V;R)
into
\mathcal{H}(G;R)
. For a category
\mathcal{B}
with
G
-support we can restrict
to
V
and only consider morphisms whose support is contained in
V
. However, as the

G
-support is typically open this is not sensible and it is not clear how one might
exhibit a subcategory associated to
V
. But once we use the (K-theoretic) deloopings

\mathcal{B}_{G}(\mathfrak{E}^{\text{pw}}(X),\mathcal{Y})
of
\mathcal{B}
this changes;

\mathcal{B}_{G}(\mathfrak{E}^{\text{pw}}(X),\mathcal{Y})
has many subcategories. For example for
any
G
-control structure
\mathfrak{E}
on
X\times\mathbb{N}
that is contained in
\mathfrak{E}^{\text{pw}}(X)

we obtain a subcategory
\mathcal{B}_{G}(\mathfrak{E},\mathcal{Y})
. The
\mathbb{N}
-factor in

X\times\mathbb{N}
allows us create
G
-control structures that become more restrictive with

t\to\infty
. In our definition later on we use this to approximate
V
by smaller and
smaller neighborhoods of
V
in
G
as
t\to\infty
. For the precise
G
-control
structure we use see Definition
5.8
later on. We will only change

\mathfrak{E}^{\text{pw}}_{2}(X)
by adding what we call the foliated control condition. (There are many
possible variations for this
G
-control structure; our choice is carefully tailored to
enable us to prove both the
{\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.0pt\mathrm{vcy}}
-Farrell–Jones Conjecture for p-adic groups and the
Reduction Theorem
14.1
.)

5.
b
.
Two functors to
G
-spaces

We define two functors
\matheurm{P}\hskip-2.5pt_{+}\hskip-1.2pt{\mathcal{A}\hskip-0.3pt\mathrm{ll}}(G)\to G\text{-}\matheurm{Spaces}
as follows. We recall from
Subsection
3.d
that objects in
\matheurm{P}\hskip-2.5pt_{+}\hskip-1.2pt{\mathcal{A}\hskip-0.3pt\mathrm{ll}}(G)
are
n
-tuples

(G/V_{1},\ldots,G/V_{n})
, where
n=0
is allowed and each
V_{i}
is a closed subgroup of

G
. Given two such objects
(G/V_{1},\ldots,G/V_{n})
and
(G/V_{1}^{\prime},\ldots,G/V^{\prime}_{n^{\prime}})
,
a morphism
u\colon(G/V_{1},\ldots,G/V_{n})\to(G/V_{1}^{\prime},\ldots,G/V_{n^{\prime}})
is given by a
function
u\colon\{1,\ldots,n^{\prime}\}\to\{1,\ldots,n\}
for which
V^{\prime}_{i}=V_{u(i)}

holds
31
31

31

if
n^{\prime}=0
there is precisely one such
u
, if
n^{\prime}\geq 1
and
n=0
, then
there is no such
u
.
. The first functor
32
32

32

Unlike the second the first one
factors over
\matheurm{P}\hskip-2.5pt_{+}\hskip-1.2pt\matheurm{Or}(G)
.
, written as
P\mapsto|P|
, is defined on objects by

|(G/V_{1},\dots,G/V_{n})|:=G/V_{1}\times\cdots\times G/V_{n}.

It sends a morphism
u\colon(G/V_{1},\ldots,G/V_{n})\to(G/V^{\prime}_{1},\ldots,G/V^{\prime}_{n^{\prime}})
to the map

(x_{1}V_{1},\dots,x_{n}V_{n})\mapsto(x_{u(1)}V^{\prime}_{1},\dots,x_{u(n^{\prime})}V^{\prime}_{n^{\prime}}).

The second one, written as
P\mapsto|P|^{\wedge}
, is defined on objects by

|G/V_{1},\dots,G/V_{n}|^{\wedge}:=G\times\cdots\times G=G^{n}.

It sends a morphism
u\colon(G/V_{1},\dots,G/V_{n})\to(G/V^{\prime}_{1},\ldots,G/V^{\prime}_{n^{\prime}})
to the map

(x_{1},\dots,x_{n})\mapsto(x_{u(1)},\dots,x_{u(n^{\prime})}).

There is a canonical natural transformation
|\cdot|^{\wedge}\to|\cdot|
given on

(G/V_{1},\dots,G/V_{n})
by the map

G\times\cdots\times G\to G/V_{1}\times\cdots\times G/V_{n},\quad(x_{1},\dots,x_{n})\mapsto(x_{1}V_{1},\dots,x_{n}V_{n}).

For
P\in\matheurm{P}{\mathcal{A}\hskip-0.3pt\mathrm{ll}}(G)
the action of
G
on
|P|^{\wedge}
is free, and we can think of

|P|^{\wedge}\to|P|
as a resolution. For the empty tuple
\ast
, both,
|\ast|
and

|\ast|^{\wedge}
are the empty product, i.e., a point.

5.
c
.
V
-foliated distance

Let
G
be a td-group. We can equip
G
with a left invariant proper metric
d_{G}
that
generates the topology of
G
, see
[
30
, Thm. 4.5]

or
[
1
, Thm. 1.1]
. Let
V
be a closed
subgroup of
G
. For
g,g^{\prime}\in G
,
\beta\geq 0,\eta>0
we write

d_{V\text{-}\mathrm{fol}}(g,g^{\prime})<(\beta,\eta),

iff there is
v\in V
with
d_{G}(e,v)=d_{G}(g,gv)\leq\beta
and
d_{G}(gv,g^{\prime})<\eta
.
Similarly, for
g,g^{\prime}\in G
,
\beta,\eta\geq 0
we write

d_{V\text{-}\mathrm{fol}}(g,g^{\prime})\leq(\beta,\eta)
, iff there is
v\in V
with

d_{G}(e,v)=d_{G}(g,gv)\leq\beta
and
d_{G}(gv,g^{\prime})\leq\eta
. We will not consider

<(\beta,0)
.

The general idea here is to treat traveling in cosets of
V
different from traveling in
arbitrary directions in
G
. Typically,
\beta
will be a bounded number, whereas
\eta

will be a small number. This definition is motivated by similar constructions for flow
spaces, see Subsection
D.ii
.

Remark 5.2
.

We have
d_{V\text{-}\mathrm{fol}}(g,g^{\prime})\leq(\beta,0)
, iff
g^{-1}g^{\prime}\in V
and
d_{G}(g,g^{\prime})\leq\beta
.

Remark 5.3
.

Let
U
be an open subgroup of
G
. Then there is
\eta_{0}>0
such that the

\eta_{0}
-neighborhood of
U
is just
U
. Thus for
g,g^{\prime}\in G
and
\eta<\eta_{0}
we
have

d_{U\text{-}\mathrm{fol}}(g,g^{\prime})\leq(\beta,\eta)\quad\implies\quad d_{U\text{-}\mathrm{fol}}(g,g^{\prime})\leq(\beta+\eta,0).

Remark 5.4
.

In our constructions of controlled categories later on (see Definition
5.9
)
we would ideally like to work with a
G
-invariant metric on
G/V
. Typically we would
be interested in small distances in
G/V
33
33

33

For
V
open in
G
we could simply
work with any discrete metric on
G/V
, for example the metric that put different
points at distance
1
. The difficulty here arises only if
G/V
is not discrete.
.
Often there are however no
G
-invariant metrics on
G/V
(and neither are there

G
-invariant uniform structures on
G/V
). The notion of
V
-foliated control on
G

is (left)
G
-invariant and will serve us as a replacement for
G/V
with (a
non-existing)
G
-invariant metric. One way to think about this replacement is that we
have to add to points in
G/V
choices of lifts to
G
, where the choice of lifts is
only relevant up to bounded distance in the fibers for
G\to G/V
. On the level of
flow spaces this corresponds to the difference between parametrized geodesics and their
images.

5.
d
.
P
-foliated distance

There is a natural extension of
V
-foliated distance to products. For

P=(V_{1},\dots,V_{n})\in\matheurm{P}\hskip-2.5pt_{+}\hskip-1.2pt{\mathcal{A}\hskip-0.3pt\mathrm{ll}}(G)
,

g=(g_{1},\dots,g_{n}),g^{\prime}=(g^{\prime}_{1},\dots,g^{\prime}_{n})\in|P|^{\wedge}=G^{n}
we
write

d_{P\text{-}\mathrm{fol}}(g,g^{\prime})<(\beta,\eta),

iff
d_{V_{i}\text{-}\mathrm{fol}}(g_{i},g^{\prime}_{i})<(\beta,\eta)
for
i=1,\dots,n
. Similarly, we write

d_{P\text{-}\mathrm{fol}}(g,g^{\prime})\leq(\beta,\eta)
, iff
d_{V_{i}\text{-}\mathrm{fol}}(g_{i},g^{\prime}_{i})\leq(\beta,\eta)
for

i=1,\dots,n
. Note that if
P=\ast
is the empty tuple, then

d_{P\text{-}\mathrm{fol}}(g,g^{\prime})<(\beta,\eta)
and
d_{P\text{-}\mathrm{fol}}(g,g^{\prime})\leq(\beta,\eta)
are empty
conditions and thus always satisfied. However,
|\ast|^{\wedge}
is just a point so this
is sensible
34
34

34

Recall that we do not allow
\eta=0
when considering

<(\beta,\eta)
.

Remark 5.5
.

Remark
5.3
also applies to
P\in\matheurm{P}\hskip-2.5pt_{+}\hskip-1.2pt{\mathcal{O}\mathrm{p}}(G)
: if

d_{P\text{-}\mathrm{fol}}(\lambda,\lambda^{\prime})\leq(\beta,\eta)
with sufficiently small
\eta
, then

d_{P\text{-}\mathrm{fol}}(\lambda,\lambda^{\prime})\leq(\beta+\eta,0)
.

We will need the following version of the triangle inequality for
d_{P\text{-}\mathrm{fol}}
. Note that
in the statement
\delta
depends not on
P
.

Lemma 5.6

(Foliated triangle inequality)
.

Let
\alpha\geq 0
. Then for any
\epsilon>0
there is
\delta>0
such that for

P\in\matheurm{P}\hskip-2.5pt_{+}\hskip-1.2pt{\mathcal{A}\hskip-0.3pt\mathrm{ll}}(G)
and
g,g^{\prime},g^{\prime\prime}\in|P|^{\wedge}=G^{n}

d_{P\text{-}\mathrm{fol}}(g,g^{\prime}),\;d_{P\text{-}\mathrm{fol}}(g^{\prime},g^{\prime\prime})\leq(\alpha,\delta)\quad\implies\quad d_{P\text{-}\mathrm{fol}}(g,g^{\prime\prime})\leq(2\alpha,\epsilon).

Proof.

This is an easy consequence of
(5.7b)
below.
∎

Lemma 5.7
.

(5.7a)

Let
M\subseteq G
be compact. For any

\epsilon>0
there is
\delta>0
such that for all
g,g^{\prime}\in G
,
v\in M
we have

d_{G}(g,g^{\prime})<\delta\implies d_{G}(gv,g^{\prime}v)<\epsilon;

(5.7b)

Let
\alpha\geq 0
. Then for any
\epsilon>0

there is
\delta>0
such that for any closed subgroup
V
of
G
and

g,g^{\prime},g^{\prime\prime}\in G
we haven

d_{V\text{-}\mathrm{fol}}(g,g^{\prime}),d_{V\text{-}\mathrm{fol}}(g^{\prime},g^{\prime\prime})\leq(\alpha,\delta)\quad\implies\quad d_{V\text{-}\mathrm{fol}}(g,g^{\prime\prime})\leq(2\alpha,\epsilon).

Proof.

This is
[
6
, Lem. 3.1]
.
∎

5.
e
.
The category
\matheurm{C}_{G}(P)

Definition 5.8
.

Let
P\in\matheurm{P}\hskip-2.5pt_{+}\hskip-1.2pt{\mathcal{A}\hskip-0.3pt\mathrm{ll}}(G)
. We define the
G
-control structure

\mathfrak{C}(P)=\big(\mathfrak{C}_{1}(P),\mathfrak{C}_{2}(P),\mathfrak{C}_{G}(P)\big)
on

|P|^{\wedge}\times\mathbb{N}
as follows:

\bullet

\mathfrak{C}_{1}(P)
consists of all subsets
F
of
|P|^{\wedge}\times\mathbb{N}
for which

F\cap|P|^{\wedge}\times\{t\}
is finite for all
t\in\mathbb{N}
;

\bullet

\mathfrak{C}_{2}(P)
consists of all subsets
E
of

\big(|P|^{\wedge}\times\mathbb{N}\big)^{\times 2}
satisfying the following two conditions

–

bounded control over
\mathbb{N}
: there is
\alpha>0
such that for all

{\begin{pmatrix}{\lambda^{\prime},t^{\prime}}\\
{\lambda,t}\end{pmatrix}}\in E
we have
|t-t^{\prime}|\leq\alpha
;

–

foliated control over
|P|^{\wedge}
: there is
\beta\geq 0
such that for
any
\eta>0
there is
t_{0}
such that for all
t\geq t_{0}
and all

\lambda,\lambda^{\prime},t^{\prime}
we have

{\begin{pmatrix}{\lambda^{\prime},t^{\prime}}\\
{\lambda,t}\end{pmatrix}}\in E\quad\implies\quad d_{P\text{-}\mathrm{fol}}(\lambda,\lambda^{\prime})<(\beta,\eta);

\bullet

\mathfrak{C}_{G}(P)
consists of all relatively compact subsets of
G
.

We write
\mathcal{Y}(P)
for the collection of all subsets of
|P|^{\wedge}\times\mathbb{N}
that
are contained in
|P|^{\wedge}\times\{0,\dots,N\}
for some
N
.

It is an exercise to check that this is indeed a
G
-control structure. To check that

\mathfrak{C}_{2}(P)
is closed under composition, the triangle inequality from
Lemma
5.6
is used.

Definition 5.9
.

Let
G
be a td-group and
\mathcal{B}
be a category with
G
-support. We set

\matheurm{C}_{G}(P;\mathcal{B}):=\mathcal{B}_{G}(\mathfrak{C}(P),\mathcal{Y}(P)).

We will often drop
\mathcal{B}
from the notation and abbreviate
\matheurm{C}_{G}(P)=\matheurm{C}_{G}(P;\mathcal{B})
.

The assignment
P\mapsto\matheurm{C}_{G}(P)
is functorial in

\matheurm{P}\hskip-2.5pt_{+}\hskip-1.2pt{\mathcal{A}\hskip-0.3pt\mathrm{ll}}(G)
35
35

35

P\mapsto\matheurm{C}_{G}(P)
is not strictly functorial in

\matheurm{P}\hskip-2.5pt_{+}\hskip-1.2pt\matheurm{Or}(G)
. This can be fixed, using a construction that will appear later in
Subsection
14.a
. But for now we will ignore this.
. We obtain an

\matheurm{P}\hskip-2.5pt_{+}\hskip-1.2pt{\mathcal{A}\hskip-0.3pt\mathrm{ll}}(G)
-spectrum

P\mapsto\mathbf{K}\big(\matheurm{C}_{G}(P)\big).

5.
f
.
The
{\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.0pt\mathrm{vcy}}
-Farrell–Jones Conjecture

Definition 5.10

(
{\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.0pt\mathrm{vcy}}
-assembly map)
.

Let
G

be a td-group and
\mathcal{B}
be a category with
G
-support. The maps
P\to\ast
for

P\in\matheurm{P}{\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.0pt\mathrm{vcy}}(G)
induce a map

(5.11)

\hocolimunder_{P\in\matheurm{P}{\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.0pt\mathrm{vcy}}(G)}\mathbf{K}\big(\matheurm{C}_{G}(P;\mathcal{B})\big)\;\to\;\mathbf{K}\big(\matheurm{C}_{G}(\ast;\mathcal{B})\big).

This is the
{\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.0pt\mathrm{vcy}}
-assembly map for
\mathcal{B}
.

In light of the Farrell–Jones Conjecture for discrete groups one might expect that the
homotopy colimit in (
5.11
) should be taken over
\matheurm{Or}_{{\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.0pt\mathrm{vcy}}}(G)

instead of
\matheurm{P}{\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.0pt\mathrm{vcy}}(G)
. However, as discussed in
Subsection
1.m
it is an important point that we allow
products here. On the other hand with slightly different definitions we could use

\matheurm{P}\matheurm{Or}_{{\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.0pt\mathrm{vcy}}}(G)
in place of
\matheurm{P}{\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.0pt\mathrm{vcy}}(G)
, see
Subsection
14.a
.

Conjecture 5.12

(
{\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.0pt\mathrm{vcy}}
-Farrell–Jones Conjecture)
.

Let
G
be a td-group and
\mathcal{B}
be a Hecke category with
G
-support.
Then the
{\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.0pt\mathrm{vcy}}
-assembly
map (
5.11
) for
\mathcal{B}
is an equivalence.

Remark 5.13
.

By Proposition
5.16
below
(for
P=\ast
) we have

\Omega\mathbf{K}(\matheurm{C}_{G}(\ast;\mathcal{B}))\simeq\mathbf{K}(\mathcal{B}_{G}[\ast])=\mathbf{K}(\mathcal{B}).

If
\mathcal{B}=\mathcal{B}(G;R)
, then
\mathbf{K}(\mathcal{B})=\mathbf{K}(\mathcal{H}(G;R))
and so in this case
the
{\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.0pt\mathrm{vcy}}
-Farrell–Jones Conjecture
5.12
is about
the K-theory of the Hecke algebra
\mathcal{H}(G;R)
.

Remark 5.14
.

Let
\Gamma
be a discrete group and
\mathcal{A}
be an additive category with a

\Gamma
-action. One obtains a category
\mathcal{A}[\Gamma]
whose objects are the objects
of
\mathcal{A}
. Morphisms
A\to A^{\prime}
in
\mathcal{A}[\Gamma]
are finite formal sums

\sum_{\gamma}\varphi_{\gamma}\cdot\gamma
where

\varphi_{\gamma}\colon\gamma A\to A^{\prime}
is a morphism in
\mathcal{A}
. The K-theoretic
Farrell–Jones conjecture with coefficients for
\Gamma
concerns the K-theory of

\mathcal{A}[\Gamma]
. As
\mathcal{A}[\Gamma]
is a Hecke category with
\Gamma
-support in an
obvious way, one can use Proposition
3.13
and
Proposition
5.16
below to check that for discrete groups the

{\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.0pt\mathrm{vcy}}
-Farrell–Jones Conjecture
5.12
implies the usual
K-theoretic Farrell–Jones Conjecture with coefficients. In fact, for discrete groups the two conjectures are equivalent see
[
7
, Remark 5.7]
.

Theorem 5.15

(
{\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.0pt\mathrm{vcy}}
-Farrell–Jones Conjecture for reductive
p
-adic groups)
.

Let
G
be a reductive
p
-adic group and
\mathcal{B}
be a Hecke category with
G
-support.
Then the
{\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.0pt\mathrm{vcy}}
-assembly map (
5.11
) for
\mathcal{B}
is an
equivalence.

The formal framework of the proof of
Theorem
5.15
is discussed in
Section
6
. The proof is then carried out in
Sections
7
to
13
.

5.
g
.
Relating
\matheurm{C}_{G}(P;\mathcal{B})
to
\mathcal{B}[P]

Proposition 5.16
.

Let
\mathcal{B}
be a category with
G
-support.
There is a zig-zag of weak equivalences
between the
\matheurm{P}\hskip-2.5pt_{+}\hskip-1.2pt{\mathcal{O}\mathrm{p}}(G)
-
\matheurm{Spectra}

P\mapsto\Omega\mathbf{K}\big(\matheurm{C}_{G}(P;\mathcal{B})\big)\quad\text{and}\quad P\mapsto\mathbf{K}\big(\mathcal{B}[P]\big).

We start the proof of Proposition
5.16
with the following
observation. Let
P\in\matheurm{P}\hskip-2.5pt_{+}\hskip-1.2pt{\mathcal{O}\mathrm{p}}(G)
. As noted in Remark
5.5
, if

d_{P\text{-}\mathrm{fol}}(\lambda,\lambda^{\prime})\leq(\beta,\eta)
with small
\eta
, then

d_{P\text{-}\mathrm{fol}}(\lambda,\lambda^{\prime})\leq(\beta+\eta,0)
. This implies that (using the foliated
control condition) for
E\in\mathfrak{C}_{2}(P)
there are
\beta>0
and
t_{0}\in\mathbb{N}
such
that for all
t\geq t_{0}
and all
\lambda,\lambda^{\prime},t^{\prime}
we have

{\begin{pmatrix}{\lambda^{\prime},t^{\prime}}\\
{\lambda,t}\end{pmatrix}}\in E\quad\implies\quad d_{P\text{-}\mathrm{fol}}(\lambda,\lambda^{\prime})<(\beta,0).

In the following definition we strengthen this to all
t
, not just sufficiently large

t
. This produce a control structure that is discrete over
|P|
(with respect to the
projection
|P|^{\wedge}\to|P|
).

Definition 5.17
.

Let
P\in\matheurm{P}\hskip-2.5pt_{+}\hskip-1.2pt{\mathcal{O}\mathrm{p}}(G)
. We define the
G
-control structure

\mathfrak{C}^{\mathrm{dis}}(P)=\big(\mathfrak{C}^{\mathrm{dis}}_{1}(P),\mathfrak{C}^{\mathrm{dis}}_{2}(P),\mathfrak{C}^{\mathrm{dis}}_{G}(P)\big)
as follows. We set
\mathfrak{C}^{\mathrm{dis}}_{1}(P):=\mathfrak{C}_{1}(P)
,

\mathfrak{C}^{\mathrm{dis}}_{G}(P):=\mathfrak{C}_{G}(P)
and define
\mathfrak{C}^{\mathrm{dis}}_{2}(P)
to consist of
all
E\in\mathfrak{C}_{2}(P)
satisfying in addition the following: there is
\beta>0

such that

{\begin{pmatrix}{\lambda^{\prime},t^{\prime}}\\
{\lambda,t}\end{pmatrix}}\in E\quad\implies\quad d_{P\text{-}\mathrm{fol}}(\lambda,\lambda^{\prime})\leq(\beta,0).

We define

\matheurm{C}_{G}^{\mathrm{dis}}(P):=\mathcal{B}_{G}(\mathfrak{C}^{\mathrm{dis}}(P),\mathcal{Y}(P))
.

Lemma 5.18
.

Let
P\in\matheurm{P}\hskip-2.5pt_{+}\hskip-1.2pt{\mathcal{O}\mathrm{p}}(G)
.

(5.18a)

The inclusion

\matheurm{C}_{G}^{\mathrm{dis}}(P)\to\matheurm{C}_{G}(P)
is an equivalence.

(5.18b)

The projection
|P|^{\wedge}\times\mathbb{N}\to|P|

induces an equivalence

\mathcal{B}_{G}(\mathfrak{C}^{\mathrm{dis}}(P)|_{\mathcal{Y}(P)})\to(\mathcal{B}[P])_{\oplus}
.

(5.18c)

The K-theory of the category

\mathcal{B}_{G}(\mathfrak{C}^{\mathrm{dis}}(P))
vanishes.

Proof.

The first two are easy exercises in the Definitions. The third comes from the standard
Eilenberg swindle on
\mathcal{B}_{G}(\mathfrak{C}^{\mathrm{dis}}(P))
using the shift

(\lambda,t)\mapsto(\lambda,t+1)
, i.e., Lemma
4.28

applies
36
36

36

It is instructive to note that this swindle does not work on

\mathcal{B}_{G}(\mathfrak{C}(P))
: For
\varphi\in\mathcal{B}_{G}(\mathfrak{C}(P))
there can be

{\begin{pmatrix}{\lambda^{\prime},t^{\prime}}\\
{\lambda,t}\end{pmatrix}}\in\supp_{2}\varphi
where
\lambda
and
\lambda^{\prime}

have different images in
|P|
, i.e., there is
\eta
such that

d_{P\text{-}\mathrm{fol}}(\lambda,\lambda^{\prime})<(\beta,\eta)
fails regardless of
\beta
. Then

{\begin{pmatrix}{\lambda^{\prime},t^{\prime}+n}\\
{\lambda,t+n}\end{pmatrix}}\in\supp_{2}\varphi^{\infty}
for all
n
and this
violates the foliated control condition over
|P|^{\wedge}
, i.e.,

\varphi^{\infty}\not\in\mathcal{B}_{G}(\mathfrak{C}(P))
. In other
words
(4.29d)
fails.
.
∎

Proof of Proposition
5.16
.

The Karoubi sequence

\mathcal{B}_{G}(\mathfrak{C}^{\mathrm{dis}}(P)|_{\mathcal{Y}(P)})\to\mathcal{B}_{G}(\mathfrak{C}^{\mathrm{dis}}(P))\to\mathcal{B}_{G}(\mathfrak{C}^{\mathrm{dis}}(P),{\mathcal{Y}(P)})=\matheurm{C}_{G}^{\mathrm{dis}}(P)

induces a fibration sequence in K-theory, see (
4.22
).
Using
(5.18c)
we obtain a weak equivalence

\Omega\mathbf{K}\big(\matheurm{C}_{G}^{\mathrm{dis}}(P)\big)\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathbf{K}\big(\mathcal{B}_{G}(\mathfrak{C}^{\mathrm{dis}}(P)|_{\mathcal{Y}(P)})\big)
.
Now
(5.18a)
and
(5.18b)
give
the result.
∎

6.
Formal framework of proof of the
{\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.0pt\mathrm{vcy}}
-Farrell–Jones Conjecture for reductive
p
-adic groups

The proof of the
{\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.0pt\mathrm{vcy}}
-Farrell–Jones Conjecture for reductive
p
-adic groups
(Theorem
5.15
) is organized around two
functors

\mathbf{D}_{G}(\--;\mathcal{B}),\;\mathbf{D}_{G}^{0}(\--;\mathcal{B})\;\colon\mathcal{R}\;\to\;\matheurm{Spectra}.

We will define the source category below and then discuss some properties of these
functors. Theorem
5.15
is then an easy
consequence of these properties. The functors

\mathbf{D}_{G}(\--;\mathcal{B})=\mathbf{K}(\matheurm{D}_{G}(\--;\mathcal{B}))
and

\mathbf{D}_{G}^{0}(\--;\mathcal{B})=\mathbf{K}(\matheurm{D}^{0}_{G}(\--;\mathcal{B}))
will be constructed in
Section
7
as the K-theory of certain additive categories. The
verification of their properties will occupy Sections
7

to
13
. For most of these properties we can work with any category
with
G
-support
\mathcal{B}
. The exception is the transfer from
Theorem
6.7
, for which we need
\mathcal{B}
to be a Hecke category with

G
-support. Often we will drop
\mathcal{B}
from the notation and write

\mathbf{D}_{G}(\--)=\mathbf{D}_{G}(\--;\mathcal{B})
and
\mathbf{D}_{G}^{0}(\--)=\mathbf{D}_{G}^{0}(\--;\mathcal{B})
.

6.
a
.
\mathcal{C}
-simplicial complexes.

Definition 6.1

(
\mathcal{C}
-simplicial complexes)
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be a category. A
\mathcal{C}
-simplicial complex
is a pair

{\bf\Sigma}=(\Sigma,C)
, where
\Sigma
is a simplicial complex and

P\colon\simp(\Sigma)^{{\mathrm{op}}}\to\mathcal{C}
is a contravariant functor from the poset of
simplices of
\Sigma
, ordered by inclusion, to
\mathcal{C}
. A map of
\mathcal{C}
-complexes

(\Sigma,C)\to(\Sigma^{\prime},C^{\prime})
is a pair
\mathbf{f}=(f,\kappa)
, where

f\colon\Sigma\to\Sigma^{\prime}
is a simplicial map and
\kappa\colon C\to C^{\prime}\circ f_{*}

is a natural transformation. Here we write

f_{*}\colon\simp(\Sigma)\to\simp(\Sigma^{\prime})
for the map induced by
f
.

The
dimension
of
{\bf\Sigma}=(\Sigma,C)
is the dimension of
\Sigma
; its

d
-skeleton
is
{\bf\Sigma}^{d}:=(\Sigma^{d},C|_{\simp(\Sigma^{d})})
, where

\Sigma^{d}
is the
d
-skeleton of
\Sigma
.

Definition 6.2
.

We define
\mathcal{R}
as the category of
\matheurm{P}\hskip-2.5pt_{+}\hskip-1.2pt{\mathcal{A}\hskip-0.3pt\mathrm{ll}}(G)
-simplicial complexes and
write
\mathcal{R}^{0}
for the full subcategory of
0
-dimensional

\matheurm{P}\hskip-2.5pt_{+}\hskip-1.2pt{\mathcal{A}\hskip-0.3pt\mathrm{ll}}(G)
-simplicial complexes.

We can think about
\mathcal{R}^{0}
as being obtained from
\matheurm{P}\hskip-2.5pt_{+}\hskip-1.2pt{\mathcal{A}\hskip-0.3pt\mathrm{ll}}(G)
by adding
arbitrary coproducts to
\matheurm{P}\hskip-2.5pt_{+}\hskip-1.2pt{\mathcal{A}\hskip-0.3pt\mathrm{ll}}(G)
. There is a product

(6.3)

\begin{split}\mathcal{R}\times\mathcal{R}^{0}\qquad&\to\qquad\mathcal{R}\\
\big((\Sigma,P),(M,Q)\big)\;&\mapsto\;\big(\Sigma\times M,(\sigma,m)\mapsto P(\sigma)\times Q(m)\big).\end{split}

We write
\mathcal{R}^{0}_{\mathcal{F}}
for the full subcategory of
\mathcal{R}^{0}

on all
(M,P)
where
P
takes values in
\matheurm{P}\mathcal{F}(G)
. A drawback
of our notation is that
\mathcal{R}^{0}_{{\mathcal{A}\hskip-0.3pt\mathrm{ll}}(G)}\subsetneq\mathcal{R}^{0}
,
because the empty product
\ast
is not contained in
\matheurm{P}{\mathcal{A}\hskip-0.3pt\mathrm{ll}}(G)
. However, typically

\mathcal{F}
will be a proper collection of subgroups, so this should not lead to serious
confusion.

Example 6.4
.

Let
\mathcal{F}
be a collection of closed subgroups of
G
.
We write
\Sigma_{\mathcal{F}}(G)
for the following simplicial complex. Vertices of

\Sigma_{\mathcal{F}}(G)
are pairs
(n,V)
with
n\in\mathbb{N}
and
V\in\mathcal{F}
. Vertices

(n_{0},V_{0}),\dots,(n_{k},V_{k})
form a simplex of
\Sigma_{\mathcal{F}}(G)
, if and only if the

n_{i}
are pairwise distinct
37
37

37

Alternatively,
\Sigma_{\mathcal{F}}(G)
is the infinite
join
\ast_{n\in\mathbb{N}}(\coprod_{F\in\mathcal{F}}G/F)
.
. There is an evident functor

P_{\mathcal{F}}(G)\colon\simp(\Sigma_{\mathcal{F}}(G))^{{\mathrm{op}}}\to\matheurm{P}\mathcal{F}(G)
that sends a simplex

\sigma=\big\{(n_{0},V_{0}),\ldots,(n_{k},V_{k})\big\}
to
(G/V_{0},\ldots,G/V_{k})
with

n_{0}<\cdots<n_{k}
, where we choose the numbering such that
n_{0}<\cdots<n_{k}
. We
obtain
\mathbf{J}_{\mathcal{F}}(G):=\big(\Sigma_{\mathcal{F}}(G),P_{\mathcal{F}}(G)\big)\in\mathcal{R}
.

We write
\Sigma^{N}_{\mathcal{F}}(G)
for the subcomplex of
\Sigma_{\mathcal{F}}(G)
spanned by all
vertices
(n,V)
with
n\leq N
38
38

38

Alternatively,

\Sigma^{N}_{\mathcal{F}}(G)=\ast_{n\leq N}(\coprod_{F\in\mathcal{F}}G/F)
.
. Then

\Sigma^{N}_{\mathcal{F}}(G)
is a proper subcomplex of the
N
-skeleton

\big(\Sigma_{\mathcal{F}}(G)\big)^{N}
. For finite
\mathcal{F}
,
\Sigma^{N}_{\mathcal{F}}(G)
is a finite
complex, while the
N
-skeleton
\big(\Sigma_{\mathcal{F}}(G)\big)^{N}
is never finite. We set

\mathbf{J}^{N}_{\mathcal{F}}(G):=\big(\Sigma^{N}_{\mathcal{F}}(G),P_{\mathcal{F}}(G)|_{\simp(\Sigma^{N}_{\mathcal{F}}(G))}\big)
.

We will discuss in Subsection
7.b
realization
functors from
\mathcal{R}
to
G
-spaces. The realization
|\mathbf{J}_{\mathcal{F}}(G)|
of

\mathbf{J}_{\mathcal{F}}(G)
is the numerable classifying spaces for

\mathcal{F}

[
10
, A1]
, see Example
7.2
.
This motivated the definition of
\mathbf{J}_{\mathcal{F}}(G)
.

6.
b
.
Coefficients of
\mathbf{D}_{G}

Write

I\colon\matheurm{P}\hskip-2.5pt_{+}\hskip-1.2pt{\mathcal{A}\hskip-0.3pt\mathrm{ll}}(G)\;\to\;\mathcal{R}^{0}
for the inclusion.
The underlying simplicial complex of
I(P)
consist of one vertex which is sent to
P
.
We will show in
Proposition
8.1
that there exists a zig-zag of equivalences of

\matheurm{P}\hskip-2.5pt_{+}\hskip-1.2pt{\mathcal{A}\hskip-0.3pt\mathrm{ll}}(G)
-spectra between
I^{*}\Omega\mathbf{D}_{G}(\--)
and

\mathbf{K}\big(\matheurm{C}_{G}(\--)\big)
.
To ease notation we will often abbreviate
P=I(P)
and omit
I^{*}
from the notation.

6.
c
.
Computation of
\mathbf{D}_{G}
on

\mathcal{R}^{0}

Let

(M,P)\in\mathcal{R}^{0}
. We will show in
Proposition
8.5
that the canonical map

(6.5)

\bigvee_{m\in M}\mathbf{D}_{G}(P(m))\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathbf{D}_{G}((M,P))

is an equivalence.

6.
d
.
\mathbf{D}_{G}^{0}
determines
\mathbf{D}_{G}

We will construct in
Proposition
8.9
a diagram in
\mathcal{R}\text{-}\matheurm{Spectra}

\textstyle{\mathbf{D}_{G}^{0}(\--)}
\textstyle{\mathbf{D}_{G}^{0}(\--)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathbf{D}_{G}^{0}(\--)}
\textstyle{\mathbf{D}_{G}^{0}(\--)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathbf{D}_{G}^{0}(\--)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathbf{D}_{G}^{0}(\--)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathbf{D}_{G}^{0}(\--)}
\textstyle{\mathbf{D}_{G}^{0}(\--)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathbf{D}_{G}^{0}(\--)}

whose homotopy colimit is equivalent to

.

6.
e
.
Homotopy invariance for
\mathbf{D}_{G}^{0}

Let

\mathbf{M}=(M,P)\in\mathcal{R}^{0}
. Let

\pi\colon M\times\Delta^{d}\to M
be the projection.
We obtain

{\bf\Delta}^{d}_{\mathbf{M}}:=(M\times\Delta^{d},P\circ\pi_{*})\in\mathcal{R}.

A choice of a point
x_{0}\in|\Delta^{d}|
determines an inclusion

\mathbf{i}\colon\mathbf{M}\to{\bf\Delta}^{d}_{\mathbf{M}}
. We show in
Proposition
8.16
that
\mathbf{i}
induces an equivalence

\mathbf{D}_{G}^{0}(\mathbf{M})\;\xrightarrow{\sim}\;\mathbf{D}_{G}^{0}({\bf\Delta}^{d}_{\mathbf{M}}).

6.
f
.
Excision for
\mathbf{D}_{G}^{0}

Let
{\bf\Sigma}=(\Sigma,P)
in

\mathcal{R}
be
d
-dimensional. Assume that the vertices of
\Sigma
are locally
ordered; then any simplex of
\Sigma
is canonically isomorphic to a standard simplex

\Delta^{k}
. Let
B
be the set of
d
-simplices of
\Sigma
. We obtain a canonical map

f\colon B\times\Delta^{d}\to\Sigma
. Let
\pi\colon B\times\Delta^{d}\to B
be the
projection. Set
\widehat{\Sigma}:=B\times\Delta^{d}
,
\widehat{P}:=p|_{B}\circ\pi_{*}

and
\widehat{\bf\Sigma}:=(\widehat{\Sigma},\widehat{P})
. Let

\mathbf{f}:=(f,\kappa)\colon\widehat{\bf\Sigma}\to{\bf\Sigma}
where
\kappa
is defined as
follows. Let
\tau
be a simplex of
\widehat{\Sigma}
. Then
\tau
is contained in

\{\sigma\}\times\Delta^{d}
for some
d
-simplex
\sigma
of
\Sigma
and we have

\widehat{P}(\tau)=P(\sigma)
and
f(\tau)\subseteq\sigma
. We define

\kappa_{\tau}\colon\widehat{P}(\tau)\to P(f(\tau))
as the evaluation of
P
on the
inclusion
f(\tau)\subseteq\sigma
. Let
\bf\Sigma^{\prime}=(\Sigma^{\prime},P^{\prime})
be the

(d\,\text{-}1)
-skeleton of
{\bf\Sigma}
and
\widehat{\bf\Sigma}^{\prime}=(\Sigma^{\prime},P^{\prime})
be the

(d\,\text{-}1)
-skeleton of
\widehat{\bf\Sigma}
. Then
\mathbf{f}
restricts to

\mathbf{f}^{\prime}\colon\widehat{\bf\Sigma}^{\prime}\to{\bf\Sigma}^{\prime}
. We write

\widehat{\iota}\colon\widehat{\bf\Sigma}^{\prime}\to\widehat{\bf\Sigma}
and

\iota\colon{\bf\Sigma}^{\prime}\to{\bf\Sigma}
for the canonical inclusions and obtain

(6.6)

\textstyle{\widehat{\bf\Sigma}^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathbf{f}^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\widehat{\iota}}
\textstyle{{\bf\Sigma}^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\iota}
\textstyle{\widehat{\bf\Sigma}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathbf{f}}
\textstyle{{\bf\Sigma}.}

We show in Proposition
8.20
that
\mathbf{D}_{G}^{0}(\--)

takes this diagram to a homotopy pushout diagram of spectra.

6.
g
.
Skeleton continuity of
\mathbf{D}_{G}^{0}

Let
{\bf\Sigma}\in\mathcal{R}
. We show
in Proposition
8.22
that the canonical map

\hocolimunder_{d\in\mathbb{N}}\mathbf{D}_{G}^{0}({\bf\Sigma}^{d})\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathbf{D}_{G}^{0}({\bf\Sigma})

is an equivalence.

6.
h
.
Transfer

We use
\mathbf{J}_{{\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.0pt\mathrm{vcy}}}(G)
from
Example
6.4
and consider

\displaystyle\mathbf{D}_{G}^{0}\big(\mathbf{J}_{\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.0pt\mathrm{vcy}}(G)\times\--;\mathcal{B}\big)\colon\mathcal{R}\to\matheurm{Spectra}.

The projections
\mathbf{J}_{\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.0pt\mathrm{vcy}}(G)\times P\to P
induce a projection

\mathbf{p}\colon\mathbf{D}_{G}^{0}\big(\mathbf{J}_{\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.0pt\mathrm{vcy}}(G)\times\--;\mathcal{B}\big)\to\mathbf{D}_{G}^{0}(\--;\mathcal{B})

in
\mathcal{R}
-spectra.

Theorem 6.7
.

Assume that
G
is a reductive
p
-adic group and that
\mathcal{B}
is a Hecke category with

G
-support. Then the projection
\mathbf{p}
admits a section, i.e., there is

\mathbf{tr}\colon\mathbf{D}_{G}^{0}(\--;\mathcal{B})\to\mathbf{D}_{G}^{0}\big(\mathbf{J}_{\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.0pt\mathrm{vcy}}(G)\times\--;\mathcal{B}\big)
such that
\mathbf{p}\circ\mathbf{tr}
is equivalent to the identity in

\mathcal{R}^{0}
-spectra.

Proof of Theorem
5.15

modulo properties of
\mathbf{D}_{G}
and
\mathbf{D}_{G}^{0}
.

We need to show that the

{\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.0pt\mathrm{vcy}}
-assembly map

(6.8)

\hocolimunder_{P\in\matheurm{P}{\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.0pt\mathrm{vcy}}(G)}\mathbf{K}\big(\matheurm{C}_{G}(P)\big)\;\to\;\mathbf{K}\big(\matheurm{C}_{G}(\ast)\big)

is an equivalence. By the equivalence from Subsection
6.b
we
can equivalently show that

(6.9)

\hocolimunder_{P\in\matheurm{P}{\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.0pt\mathrm{vcy}}(G)}\mathbf{D}_{G}(P)\;\to\;\mathbf{D}_{G}(\ast)

is an equivalence. We obtain the following factorization
of (
6.9
)

(6.10)

\hocolimunder_{P\in\matheurm{P}{\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.0pt\mathrm{vcy}}(G)}\mathbf{D}_{G}(P)\;\to\;\hocolimunder_{(M,P)\in\mathcal{R}^{0}_{{\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.0pt\mathrm{vcy}}}}\mathbf{D}_{G}(M,P)\;\to\;\mathbf{D}_{G}(\ast).

For fixed
(M,P)\in\mathcal{R}^{0}_{{\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.0pt\mathrm{vcy}}}
consider the
canonical map

(6.11)

\hocolimunder_{Q\in I\downarrow(M,P)}\mathbf{D}_{G}(Q)\to\mathbf{D}_{G}(M,P)

where
I
denotes the inclusion
\matheurm{P}{\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.0pt\mathrm{vcy}}(G)\to\mathcal{R}^{0}_{{\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.0pt\mathrm{vcy}}}
. It
is not difficult to identify (
6.11
)
with (
6.5
), which is an equivalence. The transitivity
Lemma
A.2
for homotopy colimits implies now that the first map
in (
6.10
) is an equivalence. As
\mathbf{D}_{G}
can be
expressed as a homotopy colimit in
\mathbf{D}_{G}^{0}
, see Subsection
6.d
,
the second map in (
6.10
) is an equivalence if

(6.12)

\hocolimunder_{(M,P)\in\mathcal{R}^{0}_{{\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.0pt\mathrm{vcy}}}}\mathbf{D}_{G}^{0}(M,P)\;\to\;\mathbf{D}_{G}^{0}(\ast)

is an equivalence. Theorem
6.7
implies
that (
6.12
) is a retract of

(6.13)

\hocolimunder_{(M,P)\in\mathcal{R}^{0}_{{\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.0pt\mathrm{vcy}}}}\mathbf{D}_{G}^{0}\big(\mathbf{J}_{\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.0pt\mathrm{vcy}}(G)\times(M,P)\big)\;\to\;\mathbf{D}_{G}^{0}(\mathbf{J}_{\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.0pt\mathrm{vcy}}(G)).

We now use that
\mathbf{D}_{G}^{0}
is homotopy invariant
(Subsection
6.e
), satisfies an excision result
(Subsection
6.f
) and skeleta continuity
(Subsection
6.g
). These properties imply that

\mathbf{D}_{G}^{0}\big(\mathbf{J}_{\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.0pt\mathrm{vcy}}(G)\times\--\big)
can in
\mathcal{R}^{0}
-spectra be
constructed as a homotopy colimit of functors of the form

\mathbf{D}_{G}^{0}\big((M_{0},P_{0})\times\--\big)
with

(M_{0},P_{0})\in\mathcal{R}^{0}_{{\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.0pt\mathrm{vcy}}}
.
Lemma
A.3
implies that for all

(M_{0},P_{0})\in\mathcal{R}^{0}_{{\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.0pt\mathrm{vcy}}}

\hocolimunder_{(M,P)\in\mathcal{R}^{0}_{{\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.0pt\mathrm{vcy}}}}\mathbf{D}_{G}^{0}\big((M_{0},P_{0})\times(M,P)\big)\;\xrightarrow{\sim}\;\mathbf{D}_{G}^{0}(M_{0},P_{0})

is an equivalence. Thus (
6.13
) is an equivalence and so
is (
6.8
).
∎

Remark 6.14
.

It is possible to show that the functor
\mathbf{D}_{G}
, which we construct later on, also
satisfies homotopy invariance, excision and continuity exactly as
\mathbf{D}_{G}^{0}
. Moreover,
Theorem
6.7
holds also for
\mathbf{D}_{G}
. Thus it is possible to prove
the Farrell–Jones Conjecture for reductive
p
-adic groups using only
\mathbf{D}_{G}
. In
fact, homotopy invariance, excision and continuity for
\mathbf{D}_{G}
can be proven in exactly
the same way as for
\mathbf{D}_{G}^{0}
. However, the construction of the transfer map
\mathbf{tr}
in
Theorem
6.7
is technically easier for
\mathbf{D}_{G}^{0}
than for
\mathbf{D}_{G}
;
this is the reason for our small detour through
\mathbf{D}_{G}^{0}
. The other way round, we
cannot replace
\mathbf{D}_{G}
with
\mathbf{D}_{G}^{0}
throughout; the equivalences from
Subsections
6.b
and
6.c
do
not hold for
\mathbf{D}_{G}^{0}
in place of
\mathbf{D}_{G}
.

7.
The categories
\matheurm{D}_{G}({\bf\Sigma})
and
\matheurm{D}^{0}_{G}({\bf\Sigma})

In this section we construct the two functors
\mathbf{D}_{G}
and
\mathbf{D}_{G}^{0}
promised in
Section
6
as the K-theory of functors
\matheurm{D}_{G}(\--)
and

\matheurm{D}_{G}^{0}(\--)
to additive categories. We will need some preparations.

7.
a
.
Some notation for simplicial complexes

Let
\Sigma
be an (abstract) simplicial complex. We write
\vertices(\Sigma)
for the
set of vertices of
\Sigma
. We will write
|\Sigma|
for the realization of
\Sigma
to
topological spaces. For a simplex
\sigma
of
\Sigma
we write
\Delta_{\sigma}
for the
simplicial subcomplex of
\Sigma
spanned by
\sigma
and
\partial\Delta_{\sigma}
for its
boundary. So
\partial\Delta_{\sigma}
is obtained from
\Delta_{\sigma}
by omitting

\sigma
. For a vertex
v\in\vertices(\Sigma)
we will not distinguish between the
abstract vertex
v
and the corresponding point
v\in|\Sigma|
. Any point

x\in|\Sigma|
has unique barycentric coordinates,

x=\sum_{v\in\vertices(\Sigma)}x(v)\cdot v
with
x(v)\in[0,1]
,

\sum_{v\in\vertices(\Sigma)}x(v)=1
,
x(v)\neq 0
for only finitely many
v
. Of
course,
\sigma:=\{v\mid x(v)\not=0\}
forms a simplex with

x\in|\Delta_{\sigma}|\setminus|\partial\Delta_{\sigma}|
.
The
\ell^{\infty}
-metric
39
39

39

In general, the topology of the
\ell^{\infty}
-metric is
coarser than the weak topology on
|\Sigma|
, but we will mostly only use it on finite
subcomplexes of
\Sigma
, where both topologies coincide. Also, on finite dimensional
subcomplexes the
\ell^{\infty}
-metric and the
\ell^{1}
-metric (that we used for example
in
[
9
]
) are Lipschitz equivalent. Using the

\ell^{\infty}
-metric is more convenient here, but there is no substantial difference.

on
|\Sigma|
is

(7.1)

d^{\infty}(x,x^{\prime}):=\max_{v\in\vertices(\Sigma)}|x(v)-x^{\prime}(v)|.

For
\sigma\in\simp(\Sigma)
we set

U_{\sigma}:=\big\{x\in|\Sigma|\;\big|\;\forall v\in\sigma:x(v)>0\big\},

this is the open star of
\sigma
, i.e., the union of the interiors of those simplices
which contain
\sigma
as face. It is an open neighborhood of

|\Delta_{\sigma}|\setminus|\partial\Delta_{\sigma}|
. For
\epsilon>0
we set

K_{\sigma,\epsilon}:=\{x\in|\Sigma|\mid\forall v\in\sigma:x(v)\geq\epsilon\}.

This is a closed subset of
U_{\sigma}
. We record that the
K_{\sigma,\epsilon}
get
larger with decreasing
\epsilon
and that

U_{\sigma}=\bigcup_{\epsilon>0}K_{\sigma,\epsilon}
. Moreover
U_{\sigma}
is the

\epsilon
-neighborhood of
K_{\sigma,\epsilon}
with respect to
d^{\infty}
.

U_{\sigma}
K_{\sigma,\epsilon}
\sigma

Figure 1.
U_{\sigma}
and
K_{\sigma,\epsilon}

7.
b
.
The
G
-spaces
|{\bf\Sigma}|
and
|{\bf\Sigma}|^{\wedge}

Write
\Delta\colon\simp(\Sigma)\to\matheurm{Spaces}
for the functor

\sigma\mapsto|\Delta_{\sigma}|
and define the realization functor

\displaystyle|\--|\;\colon\mathcal{R}

\displaystyle\to

\displaystyle G\text{-}{\matheurm{Spaces}}

\displaystyle{\bf\Sigma}=(\Sigma,P)

\displaystyle\mapsto

\displaystyle\Delta\times_{\simp(\Sigma)}|P(-)|.

In the construction of
\matheurm{C}_{G}(P)
for
P\in\matheurm{P}\hskip-2.5pt_{+}\hskip-1.2pt{\mathcal{A}\hskip-0.3pt\mathrm{ll}}(G)
we used the
G
-space

|P|^{\wedge}
from Subsection
5.b
, thought of as a resolution
of
|P|
. We will need a similar resolution for
|{\bf\Sigma}|
. Define

\displaystyle|\--|^{\wedge}\;\colon\mathcal{R}

\displaystyle\to

\displaystyle G\text{-}{\matheurm{Spaces}}

\displaystyle{\bf\Sigma}=(\Sigma,P)

\displaystyle\mapsto

\displaystyle\Delta\times_{\simp(\Sigma)}|P(\--)|^{\wedge}.

The projections maps
|P|^{\wedge}\to\ast
induce a map

p_{\bf\Sigma}\colon|{\bf\Sigma}|^{\wedge}\to|\Sigma|=\Delta\times_{\simp{\Sigma}}\ast.

Let
\sigma
be a simplex of
\Sigma
. It comes with a canonical map

|\Delta_{\sigma}|\times|P(\sigma)|^{\wedge}\to|{\bf\Sigma}|^{\wedge}.

We will write this map as
(x,\lambda)\mapsto[x,\lambda]_{\sigma}
. Of course

p_{\bf\Sigma}\big([x,\lambda]_{\sigma}\big)=x
. Altogether these canonical maps define
the projection

\coprod_{\tau}|\Delta_{\tau}|\times|P(\tau)|^{\wedge}\;\xrightarrow{q_{\bf\Sigma}}\;|{\bf\Sigma}|^{\wedge}

which is an identification of topological spaces. For a simplex
\sigma
the preimage of

U_{\sigma}\subseteq|\Sigma|
under
p_{\bf\Sigma}\circ q_{\bf\Sigma}
is

\coprod_{\tau,\sigma\leq\tau}\big(|\Delta_{\tau}|\setminus\partial_{\sigma}|\Delta_{\tau}|\big)\times|P(\tau)|^{\wedge}
, where

\partial_{\sigma}|\Delta_{\tau}|=\bigcup_{\mu\subseteq\tau,\sigma\not\subseteq\tau}|\Delta_{\mu}|
. We define

\lambda_{\sigma,\tau}\colon\big(|\Delta_{\tau}|\setminus\partial_{\sigma}|\Delta_{\tau}|\big)\times|P(\tau)|^{\wedge}\to|P(\sigma)|^{\wedge}
to
be the composite of the projection

\big(|\Delta_{\tau}|\setminus\partial_{\sigma}|\Delta_{\tau}|\big)\times|P(\tau)|^{\wedge}\to|P(\tau)|^{\wedge}
with the map

|P(\tau)|^{\wedge}\to|P(\sigma)|^{\wedge}
induced by
\sigma\subseteq\tau
. One easily
checks that the map

\coprod_{\tau,\sigma\leq\tau}\lambda_{\sigma,\tau}\;\;\colon\coprod_{\tau,\sigma\leq\tau}\big(|\Delta_{\tau}|\setminus\partial_{\sigma}|\Delta_{\tau}|\big)\times|P(\tau)|^{\wedge}\to|P(\sigma)|^{\wedge}

factorizes over the projection

\coprod_{\tau,\sigma\leq\tau}|\Delta_{\tau}|\setminus\partial_{\sigma}|\Delta_{\tau}|\times|P(\tau)|^{\wedge}\to(p_{\bf\Sigma})^{-1}(U_{\sigma})
, to a map
\lambda_{\sigma}\colon(p_{\bf\Sigma})^{-1}(U_{\sigma})\to|P(\sigma)|^{\wedge}
. We
note that
\lambda_{\sigma}\big([x,\lambda]_{\sigma}\big)=\lambda
.

Example 7.2

(Realizations of
\mathbf{J}_{\mathcal{F}}(G)
and

\mathbf{J}^{N}_{\mathcal{F}}(G)
)
.

Let
\mathbf{J}_{\mathcal{F}}(G)=(\Sigma_{\mathcal{F}}(G),P_{\mathcal{F}}(G))
be as in Example
6.4
. It
is not hard to check that then

|\mathbf{J}_{\mathcal{F}}(G)|=\ast_{n\in\mathbb{N}}(\coprod_{F\in\mathcal{F}}G/F)
and

|\mathbf{J}_{\mathcal{F}}(G)|^{\wedge}=\ast_{n\in\mathbb{N}}(\coprod_{F\in\mathcal{F}}G)=\ast_{n\in\mathbb{N}}(G\times\mathcal{F})
hold. The canonical projection

(\coprod_{F\in\mathcal{F}}G)=G\times\mathcal{F}\to\mathcal{F}
induces the projection

p_{\mathbf{J}_{\mathcal{F}}(G)}\colon|\mathbf{J}_{\mathcal{F}}(G)|^{\wedge}=\ast_{n\in\mathbb{N}}(G\times\mathcal{F})\to\ast_{n\in\mathbb{N}}(\mathcal{F})=|\Sigma_{\mathcal{F}}(G)|
.

Similarly,
|\mathbf{J}^{N}_{\mathcal{F}}(G)|=\ast_{n\leq N}(\coprod_{F\in\mathcal{F}}G/F)
and

|\mathbf{J}_{\mathcal{F}}(G)|^{\wedge}=\ast_{n\leq N}(\coprod_{F\in\mathcal{F}}G)=\ast_{n\leq N}(G\times\mathcal{F})
. In this description of points in
|\mathbf{J}_{\mathcal{F}}(G)|^{\wedge}
can be
written as
z=[t_{0}\cdot(g_{0},V_{0}),\dots,t_{N}\cdot(g_{N},V_{N})]
with
t_{i}\in[0,1]
,

g_{i}\in G
,
V_{i}\in\mathcal{F}
where
\sum t_{i}=1
. In this notation

[t_{0}\cdot(g_{0},V_{0}),\dots,t_{N}\cdot(g_{N},V_{N})]=[t^{\prime}_{0}\cdot(g^{\prime}_{0},H^{\prime}_{0}),\dots,t^{\prime}_{N}\cdot(g^{\prime}_{N},H^{\prime}_{N})]
if and only if
t_{i}=t^{\prime}_{i}
for
i=0,\dots,N
, and

(g_{i},V_{i})=(g^{\prime}_{i},H^{\prime}_{i})
for all
i
with
t_{i}=t^{\prime}_{i}\neq 0
.

7.
c
.
Foliated distance in
|{\bf\Sigma}|^{\wedge}

We extend the notion of foliated distance from Subsection
5.d

to
\mathcal{R}
. Let
{\bf\Sigma}=(\Sigma,P)\in\mathcal{R}
and

\beta,\eta,\epsilon>0
. For
z,z^{\prime}\in|{\bf\Sigma}|^{\wedge}
we write

d_{{\bf\Sigma}\text{-}\mathrm{fol}}(z,z^{\prime})<(\beta,\eta,\epsilon),

iff the following two conditions are satisfied

(7.3a)

d^{\infty}\big(p_{\bf\Sigma}(z),p_{\bf\Sigma}(z^{\prime})\big)<\epsilon
;

(7.3b)

for all
\sigma\in\simp(\Sigma)
with

p_{\Sigma}(z)\in K_{\sigma,\epsilon}
or
p_{\Sigma}(z^{\prime})\in K_{\sigma,\epsilon}
, we
require

d_{P(\sigma)\text{-}\mathrm{fol}}\big(\lambda_{\sigma}(z),\lambda_{\sigma}(z^{\prime})\big)<(\beta,\eta).

Note that the first condition implies that if
p_{\Sigma}(z)\in K_{\sigma,\epsilon}
or

p_{\Sigma}(z^{\prime})\in K_{\sigma,\epsilon}
, then both,
p_{\Sigma}(z)
and
p_{\Sigma}(z^{\prime})
,
belong to
U_{\sigma}
, and both,
\lambda_{\sigma}(z)
and
\lambda_{\sigma}(z^{\prime})
, are
defined.

We note that this definition is compatible with restrictions to subcomplexes. More
precisely, let
\Sigma^{\prime}\subseteq\Sigma
be a subcomplex and let

{\bf\Sigma}^{\prime}:=(\Sigma^{\prime},P|_{\simp(\Sigma^{\prime})})
. If
\sigma
is a simplex of
\Sigma^{\prime}
, then

K^{\Sigma^{\prime}}_{\sigma,\epsilon}=\Sigma^{\prime}\cap K^{\Sigma}_{\sigma,\epsilon}
, where the
upper index indicates in which complex we form
K_{\sigma,\epsilon}
. Thus for

z,z^{\prime}\in|{\bf\Sigma}^{\prime}|^{\wedge}\subseteq|{\bf\Sigma}|^{\wedge}
we have

d_{{\bf\Sigma}^{\prime}\text{-}\mathrm{fol}}(z,z^{\prime})<(\beta,\eta,\epsilon)
iff

d_{{\bf\Sigma}\text{-}\mathrm{fol}}(z,z^{\prime})<(\beta,\eta,\epsilon)
.

Remark 7.4
.

Recall that we think of
V
-foliated distance on
G
as a way to get around the problem
that there may not exist
G
-invariant metrics on
G/V
, see
Remark
5.4

Given
{\bf\Sigma}=(\Sigma,P)\in\mathcal{R}
, we would ideally like to equip the

G
-space
|{\bf\Sigma}|
with a
G
-invariant metric (and as for
G/V
we would be
interested in small distances in
|{\bf\Sigma}|
). However, this space can have isotropy
groups for which the orbit
G/V
admits no
G
-invariant metric and then neither does

|{\bf\Sigma}|
. The notion of foliated distance in
|{\bf\Sigma}|^{\wedge}
is our replacement
for
|{\bf\Sigma}|
with (a non-existing)
G
-invariant metric. A way to think about this
replacement is that we add to points in
|{\bf\Sigma}|
a choice of lift to

|{\bf\Sigma}|^{\wedge}
, where the choice of lift is only relevant up to bounded distance in
the fibers for
|{\bf\Sigma}|^{\wedge}\to|{\bf\Sigma}|
. Alternatively, we can think of this
as adding to points in
|\Sigma|
a choice of lift to
|{\bf\Sigma}|^{\wedge}
, where the
choice of lift is only relevant up to foliated distance in the fibers for

p_{\bf\Sigma}\colon|{\bf\Sigma}|^{\wedge}\to|\Sigma|
.

Remark 7.5
.

One should think of
d_{{\bf\Sigma}\text{-}\mathrm{fol}}(z,z^{\prime})<(\beta,\eta,\epsilon)
as a two stage
condition. The first stage just uses the images of
z,z^{\prime}
in
|\Sigma|
and requires
their distance to be
<\epsilon
. For general
z
and
z^{\prime}
we can not compare the two
fibers for
p_{\bf\Sigma}\colon|{\bf\Sigma}|^{\wedge}\to|\Sigma|
containing them. But
whenever one of
z
and
z^{\prime}
projects into
K_{\sigma,\epsilon}
, then

\lambda_{\sigma}(z),\lambda_{\sigma}(z^{\prime})\in P(\sigma)
are both defined and we require

d_{P(\sigma)\text{-}\mathrm{fol}}(\lambda_{\sigma}(z),\lambda_{\sigma}(z^{\prime}))<(\beta,\eta)
in the second
stage of the condition.

We point out the following detail about the second stage. Recall

K_{\sigma,\epsilon}\subseteq U_{\sigma}
. One might be tempted to require

d_{P(\sigma)\text{-}\mathrm{fol}}(\lambda_{\sigma}(z),\lambda_{\sigma}(z^{\prime}))<(\beta,\eta)
whenever both

z
and
z^{\prime}
project into
U_{\sigma}
, as this suffices for

\lambda_{\sigma}(z),\lambda_{\sigma}(z^{\prime})
to be defined. However, if neither
z
nor
z^{\prime}

projects into
K_{\sigma,\epsilon}
(but their images in
|\Sigma|
are close), then
both
z
and
z^{\prime}
are close to
p_{{\bf\Sigma}}^{-1}\big(|\Delta_{\tau}|\big)
for a simplex

\tau
(of smaller dimension than
\sigma
) and in passing from
z
to
z^{\prime}
one might
take a short-cut through
p_{{\bf\Sigma}}^{-1}(|\Delta_{\tau}|)
and mostly avoid

p_{{\bf\Sigma}}^{-1}(|\Delta_{\sigma}|)
. Thus in this situation

d_{P(\sigma)\text{-}\mathrm{fol}}\big(\lambda_{\sigma}(z),\lambda_{\sigma}(z^{\prime})\big)
is not necessarily
relevant in comparing
z
and
z^{\prime}
. Requiring that that at least one of
z
and
z^{\prime}

projects into
K_{\sigma,\epsilon}
avoids this problem.

More formally, our formulation of
(7.3a)

and
(7.3b)
guarantees (using
Lemma
5.6
) the following version of the triangle inequality.
Given
\beta>0
,
\eta>0
there is
\rho>0
such that for
\epsilon>0
,

z,z^{\prime},z^{\prime\prime}\in|{\bf\Sigma}|^{\wedge}
we have

d_{{\bf\Sigma}\text{-}\mathrm{fol}}(z,z^{\prime})<(\beta,\rho,\epsilon),d_{{\bf\Sigma}\text{-}\mathrm{fol}}(z^{\prime},z^{\prime\prime})<(\beta,\rho,\epsilon)\implies d_{{\bf\Sigma}\text{-}\mathrm{fol}}(z,z^{\prime\prime})<(2\beta,\eta,2\epsilon).

Example 7.6

(Foliated distance for
\mathbf{J}^{N}_{\mathcal{F}}(G)
)
.

For
\mathbf{J}^{N}_{\mathcal{F}}(G)=(\Sigma^{N}_{\mathcal{F}}(G),P_{\mathcal{F}}(G))
from Example
6.4
we
can use the join description of

|\mathbf{J}^{N}_{\mathcal{F}}(G)|^{\wedge}=\ast_{n\leq N}(G\times\mathcal{F})
from
Example
7.2
to unravel the definition of the foliated distance
as follows. Let
z:=[t_{0}\cdot(g_{0},V_{0}),\dots,t_{N}\cdot(g_{N},V_{N})]
,

z^{\prime}:=[t^{\prime}_{0}\cdot(g^{\prime}_{0},V^{\prime}_{0}),\dots,t^{\prime}_{N}\cdot(g^{\prime}_{N},V^{\prime}_{N})]\in|\mathbf{J}^{N}_{\mathcal{F}}(G)|^{\wedge}
. Then
d_{\mathbf{J}^{N}_{\mathcal{F}}(G)\text{-}\mathrm{fol}}(z,z^{\prime})<(\beta,\eta,\epsilon)

if and only if

(7.6a)

|t_{i}-t^{\prime}_{i}|<\epsilon
for all
i
;

(7.6b)

for all
i
with
\max\{t_{i},t^{\prime}_{i}\}
we have
V_{i}=V^{\prime}_{i}
and

d_{V_{i}\text{-}\mathrm{fol}}(g_{i},g^{\prime}_{i})<(\beta,\eta)
.

7.
d
.
The
G
-control structures
\mathfrak{D}({\bf\Sigma})
and
\mathfrak{D}^{0}({\bf\Sigma})

Let
{\bf\Sigma}=(\Sigma,P)\in\mathcal{R}
. In the following definition we will
define a control structure
\mathfrak{D}({{\bf\Sigma}})
on

|{\bf\Sigma}|^{\wedge}\times\mathbb{N}^{\times 2}
. The two
\mathbb{N}
-directions will be used to
encode two different control conditions. The first factor will be used to encode a
foliated control conditions over the
P(\sigma)
, that is compatible with

\mathfrak{C}(P(\sigma))
. The second
\mathbb{N}
-factor will be used to encode an

\epsilon
-control condition over
|\Sigma|
with respect to
d^{\infty}
. In particular
Definition
7.7
is not symmetric in
t_{0}
and
t_{1}
. The remarks following
the definition provide some discussion and motivation.

We will use the
\ell^{1}
-norm
|(t_{0},t_{1})|=t_{0}+t_{1}
on
\mathbb{N}^{\times 2}
.

Definition 7.7
.

Let
{\bf\Sigma}=(\Sigma,P)\in\mathcal{R}
. We define the
G
-control structure

\mathfrak{D}({{\bf\Sigma}})=\big(\mathfrak{D}_{1}({{\bf\Sigma}}),\mathfrak{D}_{2}({{\bf\Sigma}}),\mathfrak{D}_{G}({{\bf\Sigma}})\big)
on the
G
-space

|{\bf\Sigma}|^{\wedge}\times\mathbb{N}^{\times 2}
as follows.

(7.7a)

\mathfrak{D}_{1}({{\bf\Sigma}})
consists of all subsets
F
of

|{\bf\Sigma}|^{\wedge}\times\mathbb{N}^{\times 2}
satisfying the following conditions

•

Finiteness over
\mathbb{N}^{\times 2}
: for all

{\underline{t}}\in\mathbb{N}^{\times 2}
the set

F\cap|{\bf\Sigma}|^{\wedge}\times\{{\underline{t}}\}
is finite;

•

Compact support in
|\Sigma|
: for every
t_{0}\in\mathbb{N}
there exists a
finite subcomplex
\Sigma_{0}
of
\Sigma
such that

F\cap|{\bf\Sigma}|^{\wedge}\times\{t_{0}\}\times\mathbb{N}\subseteq p_{\bf\Sigma}^{-1}\big(|\Sigma_{0}|\big)\times\mathbb{N}^{\times 2}
;

•

Finite dimensional support
:

F\subseteq|{\bf\Sigma}^{d}|^{\wedge}\times\mathbb{N}^{\times 2}
;

(7.7b)

\mathfrak{D}_{2}({{\bf\Sigma}})
consists of all subsets
E
of

\big(|{\bf\Sigma}|^{\wedge}\times\mathbb{N}^{\times 2}\big)^{\times 2}
satisfying the
following conditions

•

Bounded control over
\mathbb{N}^{\times 2}
: there is
\alpha>0
such that if

{\begin{pmatrix}{z^{\prime},{\underline{t}}^{\prime}}\\
{z,{\underline{t}}}\end{pmatrix}}\in E
, then

|{\underline{t}}-{\underline{t}}^{\prime}|\leq\alpha
;

•

Foliated control over
{\bf\Sigma}
: for any
\epsilon>0
there is

k_{0}\in\mathbb{N}
such that for all
t_{0}\in\mathbb{N}_{\geq k_{0}}
there is
\beta>0
such
that for all
\eta>0
there is
k_{1}\in\mathbb{N}
such that for all

t_{1}\geq\mathbb{N}_{\geq k_{1}}
and all
z,z^{\prime}\in|{\bf\Sigma}|^{\wedge}
,

{\underline{t}}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{N}^{\times 2}
, with
{\underline{t}}:=(t_{0},t_{1})
we
have

\qquad{\begin{pmatrix}{z^{\prime},{\underline{t}}^{\prime}}\\
{z,{\underline{t}}}\end{pmatrix}}\in E\;\implies\;d_{{\bf\Sigma}\text{-}\mathrm{fol}}(z,z^{\prime})<(\beta,\eta,\epsilon);

(7.7c)

\mathfrak{D}_{G}({\bf\Sigma})
consists of all relatively compact
subsets of
G
.

It is an exercise to check that this is indeed a
G
-control structure. To check that

\mathfrak{D}_{2}({{\bf\Sigma}})
is closed under composition, the triangle inequality from
Remark
7.5
is used.

We define the
G
-control structure

\mathfrak{D}^{0}({\bf\Sigma})=\big(\mathfrak{D}_{1}^{0}({\bf\Sigma}),\mathfrak{D}_{2}^{0}({\bf\Sigma}),\mathfrak{D}_{G}^{0}({\bf\Sigma})\big)
as
follows. Set
\mathfrak{D}_{1}^{0}({\bf\Sigma}):=\mathfrak{D}_{1}({\bf\Sigma})
,

\mathfrak{D}_{G}^{0}({\bf\Sigma}):=\mathfrak{D}_{G}({\bf\Sigma})
. We define
\mathfrak{D}_{2}^{0}({\bf\Sigma})

to consist of all

satisfying

\qquad{\begin{pmatrix}{z^{\prime},{\underline{t}}^{\prime}}\\
{z,{\underline{t}}}\end{pmatrix}}\in E\;\implies\;{\underline{t}}^{\prime}={\underline{t}}.

Remark 7.8
.

Using quantifiers the foliated
control in Definition

reads as

\displaystyle\forall\epsilon>0\;\exists k_{0}\;\forall t_{0}\geq k_{0}\;\exists\beta>0\;\forall\eta>0\;\exists

\displaystyle k_{1}\;\forall\big(t_{1}\geq k_{1},z,z^{\prime},{\underline{t}}^{\prime}\big)\;\text{we have}

\displaystyle{\begin{pmatrix}{z^{\prime},{\underline{t}}^{\prime}}\\
{z,{\underline{t}}}\end{pmatrix}}\in E\implies d_{{\bf\Sigma}\text{-}\mathrm{fol}}(z,z^{\prime})<(\beta,\eta,\epsilon).
