\textstyle{\mathbf{C}_{*}(D)\otimes^{U}\mathbf{B}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i\otimes^{U}{\id_{\mathbf{B}}}}
\textstyle{\res_{G}^{U}\mathbf{S}_{*}(X)\otimes^{U}\mathbf{B}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{r\otimes^{U}{\id_{\mathbf{B}}}}

where

(r\otimes^{U}{\id_{\mathbf{B}}})\circ(i\otimes^{U}{\id_{\mathbf{B}}})=\id_{\mathbf{C}_{*}(D)\otimes^{U}\mathbf{B}}

and

H\otimes^{U}{\id_{\mathbf{B}}}\colon(i\otimes^{U}{\id_{\mathbf{B}}})\circ\,(r\,\otimes^{U}{\id_{\mathbf{B}}})\simeq\id_{\res_{G}^{U}\mathbf{S}_{*}(X)\otimes\mathbf{B}}
. Applying
\ind_{U}^{G}
and
using (
11.12
) we obtain

\textstyle{\ind_{U}^{G}\big(\mathbf{C}_{*}(D)\otimes^{U}\mathbf{B}\big)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\ind_{U}^{G}(i\otimes^{U}{\id_{\mathbf{B}}})}
\textstyle{\mathbf{S}_{*}(X)\otimes^{G}\ind_{U}^{G}\mathbf{B}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\ind_{U}^{G}(r\otimes^{U}{\id_{\mathbf{B}}})}

in
\ch\Idem\mathcal{B}_{G}(S(X)\times\Lambda)
.
The main advantage of

\ind_{U}^{G}(\mathbf{C}_{*}(D)\otimes^{U}\mathbf{B})
over
\mathbf{S}_{*}(X)\otimes^{G}\ind_{U}^{G}\mathbf{B}
is that it
is smaller and has better chances of satisfying control conditions. Its disadvantage is
that, as
D
is only
U
-invariant and not
G
-invariant, morphisms

\varphi\colon\ind_{U}^{G}\mathbf{B}\to\ind_{U}^{G}\mathbf{B}^{\prime}
in
\mathcal{B}_{G,U}(\Lambda)
do not induce
maps
\ind_{U}^{G}(\mathbf{C}_{*}(D)\otimes^{U}\mathbf{B})\to\ind_{U}^{G}(\mathbf{C}_{*}(D)\otimes^{U}\mathbf{B}^{\prime})
. But

\id_{\mathbf{S}_{*}(X)}\otimes\varphi
is defined and we can use the composition

(11.15)

\textstyle{\ind_{U}^{G}\big(\mathbf{C}_{*}(D)\otimes^{U}\mathbf{B}\big)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\ind_{U}^{G}(i\otimes^{U}{\id_{\mathbf{B}}})}
\textstyle{\mathbf{S}_{*}(X)\otimes^{G}\ind_{U}^{G}\mathbf{B}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\id_{\mathbf{S}_{*}(X)}\otimes^{G}\varphi}
\textstyle{\ind_{U}^{G}\big(\mathbf{C}_{*}(D)\otimes^{U}\mathbf{B}^{\prime}\big)}
\textstyle{\mathbf{S}_{*}(X)\otimes^{G}\ind_{U}^{G}\mathbf{B}^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\ind_{U}^{G}(r\otimes^{U}{\id_{\mathbf{B}}})}

instead. While this is not strictly compatible with composition, the homotopies

\ind_{U}^{G}(H\otimes^{U}{\id_{\mathbf{B}}})
guarantee that it is compatible with composition up to
coherent homotopy. From now on we will simply the notation as alluded to in
Subsection
11.e
and drop
\ind_{U}^{G}
and
\res_{G}^{U}
from the notation
and simply write
\otimes=\otimes^{G}=\otimes^{U}
.
Thus (
11.15
) abbreviates to

\textstyle{\mathbf{C}_{*}(D)\otimes\mathbf{B}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i\otimes{\id_{\mathbf{B}}}}
\textstyle{\mathbf{S}_{*}(X)\otimes\mathbf{B}\phantom{{}^{\prime}.}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\id_{\mathbf{S}_{*}(X)}\otimes\varphi}
\textstyle{\mathbf{C}_{*}(D)\otimes\mathbf{B}^{\prime}}
\textstyle{\mathbf{S}_{*}(X)\otimes\mathbf{B}^{\prime}.\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{r\otimes{\id_{\mathbf{B}}^{\prime}}}

In summary, we can use the data chosen in
(11.14a)
and
(11.14b)
to define a
homotopy coherent functor, see Definition
C.1
,

(11.16)

F=(F^{0},F^{1},\dots)\;\colon\mathcal{B}_{G,U}(\Lambda)\to\ch\mathcal{B}_{G}(S(X)\times\Lambda)

as follows. For
\mathbf{B}\in\mathcal{B}_{G,U}(\Lambda)
we set

F^{0}(\mathbf{B}):=\mathbf{C}_{*}(D)\otimes\mathbf{B}.

For a chain

\mathbf{B}_{0}\xleftarrow{\varphi_{1}}\mathbf{B}_{1}\xleftarrow{\varphi_{2}}\dots\xleftarrow{\varphi_{n}}\mathbf{B}_{n}

of composable morphisms in
\mathcal{B}_{G,U}(\Lambda)
we define

\displaystyle F^{n}(\varphi_{1},\dots,\varphi_{n}):=(r\otimes

\displaystyle\id_{\mathbf{B}_{0}})\circ(\id_{\mathbf{S}_{*}(X)}\otimes\varphi_{1})\circ(H\otimes\id_{\mathbf{B}_{1}})\circ\dots

\displaystyle\dots\circ(H\otimes\id_{\mathbf{B}_{n-1}})\circ(\id_{\mathbf{S}_{*}(X)}\otimes\varphi_{n})\circ(i\otimes\id_{\mathbf{B}_{n}}).

It is not difficult to check that this defines a homotopy coherent functor, compare
Example
C.3
.

In Section
12
we will discuss the effect
of (
11.16
) on
\supp_{2}
. For now we record the following easy
facts.

Lemma 11.17
.

(11.17a)

If
\mathbf{B}
is finite, then all chain modules
(F^{0}(\mathbf{B}))_{n}
of
F^{0}(\mathbf{B})
are finite as well;

(11.17b)

We have
\supp_{1}(F^{0}(\mathbf{B}))_{n}\subseteq\simp_{n}(D)\times\supp_{1}\mathbf{B}

and
\supp_{G}(F^{0}(\mathbf{B}))_{n}\subseteq\supp_{G}\mathbf{B}
;

(11.17c)

The
n
-th boundary map
\partial^{F^{0}(\mathbf{B})}_{n}
of
F^{0}(\mathbf{B})

satisfies
\supp_{G}\partial^{F^{0}(\mathbf{B})}_{n}\subseteq\supp_{G}\mathbf{B}
.

Proof.

This follows from
(11.9a)
,
(11.9b)

and
(11.9d)
.
∎

11.
h
.
Projection back to
\Lambda

The
projection
\pr\colon S(X)\times\Lambda\to\Lambda
induces a functor

P\colon\ch\mathcal{B}_{G}(X\times\Lambda)\to\ch\mathcal{B}_{G}(\Lambda).

We are interested in the composition of
P
with
F
from (
11.16
)

P\circ F\colon\mathcal{B}_{G,U}(\Lambda)\to\ch\mathcal{B}_{G}(\Lambda).

Let

I\colon\mathcal{B}_{G,U}(\Lambda)\to\ch\mathcal{B}_{G}(\Lambda)

be the inclusion
60
60

60

Recall that
\mathcal{B}_{G,U}(\Lambda)
is a subcategory of

\mathcal{B}_{G}(\Lambda)
.
. We construct a natural transformation

\tau\colon P\circ F\to I
. Write
\star
for the one-point space. Let
p(\mathbf{C}_{*}(D))

be the image of
\mathbf{C}_{*}(D)
under the functor
\mathcal{S}^{U}(X)\to\mathcal{S}^{U}(\star)
induced by
the projection
X\to\star
. We can identify

(P\circ F)(\mathbf{A})\cong p(\mathbf{C}_{*}(D))\otimes\mathbf{B}
, for
\mathbf{B}\in\mathcal{B}_{G,U}(\Lambda)
.
Let
\mathbbm{1}\hskip-2.15277pt_{G}:=(\star,\id_{\star})\in\mathcal{S}^{G}(\star)
. We have

\mathbbm{1}\hskip-2.15277pt_{U}:=\res_{G}^{U}\mathbbm{1}\hskip-2.15277pt_{G}=(\star,\id_{\star})\in\mathcal{S}^{U}(\star)
. We can
identify
I(\mathbf{B})\cong\mathbbm{1}\hskip-2.15277pt_{U}\otimes\mathbf{B}
. The projection
D\to\star
induces an
augmentation
\epsilon\colon p(\mathbf{C}_{*}(D))\to\mathbbm{1}\hskip-2.15277pt_{U}
. Now for

\mathbf{B}\in\mathcal{B}_{G,U}(\Lambda)
we define

(11.18)

\tau_{\mathbf{B}}:=\epsilon\otimes\id_{\mathbf{B}}\colon p(C_{*}(D))\otimes\mathbf{B}\to\mathbbm{1}\hskip-2.15277pt_{U}\otimes\mathbf{B}.

We will need the notion of a strict natural transformation between homotopy coherent
functors, see Definition
C.4
.

Lemma 11.19
.

Under the canonical
identifications
(P\circ F)(\mathbf{B})\cong p(\mathbf{C}_{*}(D))\otimes\mathbf{B}
and

I(\mathbf{B})\cong\mathbbm{1}\hskip-2.15277pt_{U}\otimes\mathbf{B}
the maps (
11.18
) define a strict
natural transformation
\tau\colon P\circ F\to I
, see
Example
C.5
.

Proof.

This is a straight forward exercise in the definitions.
∎

Lemma 11.20
.

Suppose that
D
is
contractible. Let
\mathbf{B}\in\mathcal{B}_{G,U}(\Lambda)
. Then in

\ch\Idem\mathcal{B}_{G,U}(\Lambda)
there are a chain map

f\colon\mathbbm{1}\hskip-2.15277pt_{U}\otimes\mathbf{B}\to p(\mathbf{C}_{*}(D))\otimes\mathbf{B}
, and chain homotopies

h\colon f\circ\tau_{\mathbf{B}}\simeq\id_{p(\mathbf{C}_{*}(D))\otimes\mathbf{B}}
,

k\colon\tau_{\mathbf{B}}\circ f\simeq\id_{\mathbbm{1}}\hskip-2.15277pt{}_{U}\otimes\mathbf{B}
such that

\displaystyle\supp_{2}f,\supp_{2}h,\supp_{2}h^{\prime}

\displaystyle\subseteq\supp_{2}\mathbf{B},

\displaystyle\supp_{G}f,\supp_{G}h,\supp_{G}h^{\prime}

\displaystyle\subseteq\supp_{G}\mathbf{B}\subseteq U.

In particular,
\tau_{\mathbf{B}}
is a homotopy equivalence.

Proof.

Under the assumptions on
D
,
p(\mathbf{C}_{*}(D))
and
\mathbbm{1}\hskip-2.15277pt_{U}
are homotopy equivalent in

\ch\mathcal{S}^{U}(\star)
(because the simplicial chain complex of
D
is homotopy equivalent
to the simplicial chain complex of a point). Thus in
\ch\mathcal{S}^{U}(\star)
there are a
chain map
f_{0}\colon\mathbbm{1}\hskip-2.15277pt_{U}\to p(\mathbf{C}_{*}(D))
, and chain homotopies

h_{0}\colon f_{0}\circ\epsilon\simeq\id_{p(\mathbf{C}_{*}(D))}
,

k_{0}\colon\epsilon\circ f_{0}\simeq\id_{\mathbbm{1}\hskip-1.50694pt_{U}}
. Now set

f:=f_{0}\otimes\id_{\mathbf{B}}
,
h:=h_{0}\otimes\id_{\mathbf{B}}
, and

k:=k_{0}\otimes\id_{\mathbf{B}}
. The claims about
\supp_{2}
and
\supp_{G}
follow
from
(11.9c)
and
(11.9d)
.
∎

12.
Support estimates for homotopy coherent functors

Let
X
be a
G
-space equipped with a
G
-invariant metric
d_{X}
.
We assume that for
K\subseteq X
compact the pointwise isotropy group
G_{K}
is open in
G
.
Let
J
and
\Lambda
be further
G
-spaces.
Let
\mathcal{B}
be a Hecke category with
G
-support.

We will refine the construction of the homotopy coherent functor from
Subsection
11.g
and will be interested in its
effect on supports of objects and morphisms. Its construction and analysis will depend on
a list of data. This section is very formal and will be used later to check that a
sequence of homotopy coherent functors does descend to
\matheurm{D}^{0}_{G}(\--)
as discussed in
Subsection
9.a
.

12.
a
.
Set-up

Throughout this section we fix

-

numbers
L\in\mathbb{N}
,
\rho>0
;

-

a compact open subgroup
U\subseteq G
and a compact subset
M\subseteq G
with

U\subseteq M
;

-

a subspace
D\subseteq X
with a locally ordered triangulation all whose simplices
are of diameter
<\rho
;

-

a sequence of further subspaces

D=D^{(0)}\subseteq D^{(1)}\subseteq\dots\subseteq D^{(L)}
with

M\cdot D^{(l-1)}\subseteq D^{(l)}
for
l=1,\dots,L
; moreover we require that

D^{(L)}
is pointwise fixed by
U
;

-

a retraction
r^{0}\colon X\to D
for the inclusion
i^{0}\colon D\to X
, i.e.,

r^{0}\circ i^{0}=\id_{D}
;

-

a homotopy
H^{0}\colon i^{0}\circ r^{0}\simeq\id_{X}
; we will assume that
H^{0}
is
non-expanding, i.e., for all
\tau\in[0,1]
,
x,x^{\prime}\in X
we require

d_{X}(H^{0}(x,\tau),H^{0}(x^{\prime},\tau))\leq d_{X}(x,x^{\prime})
; we also assume that
H^{0}
preserves the

D^{(l)}
, i.e., we require
H^{0}(D^{(l)}\times[0,1])\subseteq D^{(l)}
;

-

a map
f\colon X\to J
;

-

a subset
E\subseteq J\times J
.

We do
not
assume that
f
is
G
-equivariant.

Remark 12.1

(Some explanations for the list of data)
.

Later

-

X
will be the extended Bruhat-Tits building for the reductive
p
-adic group
G
;

-

the
D^{(i)}
will be an increasing sequence of balls around a common center;

-

r^{0}
will be the radial projection and
H^{0}
will be the associated radial
homotopy;

-

U
will be the pointwise isotropy group of
D^{(L)}
;

-

\Lambda
will be
|\mathbf{M}|^{\wedge}
for some

\mathbf{M}\in\mathcal{R}^{0}
;

-

J
will be
|\mathbf{J}_{{\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.0pt\mathrm{vcy}}}(G)|^{\wedge}
;

-

f\colon X\to J
will come from Theorem
D.1
.

Below
U
,
B
,
r^{0}
, and
H^{0}
will be used to construct a homotopy coherent functor

\mathcal{B}_{G,U}(\Lambda)\to\ch\mathcal{B}_{G}(S(X)\times\Lambda)

as in Subsection
11.g
.
Let
\bary\colon S(X)\to X
be the map that sends a singular simplex to its barycenter.
We can then compose with
(f\circ\bary\times\id_{\Lambda})_{*}
and obtain a homotopy coherent functor

\mathcal{B}_{G,U}(\Lambda)\to\ch\mathcal{B}_{G}(J\times\Lambda)

and will be interested in estimates for the support of objects and morphisms under this
latter functor, see Proposition
12.8
below. For these estimates we will
bound the
G
-support of morphisms and objects by
M
and we will treat chains of at
most
L
-composable morphisms. The upper bound for
\supp_{2}
will be in terms of
E
.

We will work under the following assumptions throughout this section.

Assumption 12.2
.

(12.2a)

\Big\{{\begin{pmatrix}{f(gx)}\\
{gf(x)}\end{pmatrix}}\;\Big|\;x\in D^{(L)},g\in M\Big\}\subseteq E
;

(12.2b)

\Big\{{\begin{pmatrix}{f(H^{0}(x,\tau))}\\
{f(x)}\end{pmatrix}}\;\Big|\;x\in D^{(L)},\tau\in[0,1]\Big\}\subseteq E
;

(12.2c)

\Big\{{\begin{pmatrix}{f(x^{\prime})}\\
{f(x)}\end{pmatrix}}\;\Big|\;x,x^{\prime}\in D^{(L)},d_{X}(x,x^{\prime})\leq(L+1)\rho\Big\}\subseteq E
.

12.
b
.
From
S(X)
to
J

Set

\displaystyle E_{X}

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\big\{{\begin{pmatrix}{x^{\prime}}\\
{x}\end{pmatrix}}\;\big|\;x,x^{\prime}\in D^{(L)},d_{X}(x,x^{\prime})\leq(L+1)\rho\big\}

\displaystyle\qquad\cup\;\big\{{\begin{pmatrix}{H^{0}(x,\tau)}\\
{x}\end{pmatrix}}\;\big|\;x\in D^{(L)},\tau\in[0,1]\big\}\quad\subseteq X\times X;

\displaystyle E_{S}

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle(\bary\times\bary)^{-1}(E_{X})\qquad\subseteq S(X)\times S(X).

We note that
E_{X}\subseteq E_{X}^{\circ 2}
and
E_{S}\subseteq E_{S}^{\circ 2}
.
We use again the convention for products from (
11.8
).

Lemma 12.3
.

Let
E^{\prime}\subseteq\Lambda\times\Lambda
,

\Phi\colon\mathbf{B}\to\mathbf{B}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{B}_{G}(S(X)\times\Lambda)
, and

\nu\in\mathbb{N}
. Suppose
\supp_{1}\mathbf{B}\subseteq S(D^{(L)})\times\Lambda
,

\supp_{2}\Phi\subseteq E_{S}^{\circ\nu}\times E^{\prime}
, and
\supp_{G}\Phi\subseteq M
. Then

\supp_{2}(f\circ\bary\times\id_{\Lambda})_{*}\Phi\subseteq E^{\circ(\nu+1)}\times E^{\prime}
.

Proof.

By (
4.7
), and since
\bary
and
\id_{\Lambda}
are

G
-equivariant,
\supp_{2}(f\circ\bary\times\id_{\Lambda})_{*}\Phi
is contained in

(f\circ\bary\id_{\Lambda})^{\times 2}(E_{S}^{\circ\nu}\times E^{\prime})\circ\Big\{{\begin{pmatrix}{f(g\bary(\sigma)),g\lambda}\\
{gf(\bary(\sigma)),g\lambda}\end{pmatrix}}\,\Big|\,(\sigma,\lambda)\in S(D^{(L)})\times\Lambda,g\in M\Big\}.

We have

(f\circ\bary\id_{\Lambda})^{\times 2}(E_{S}^{\circ\nu}\times E^{\prime})\subseteq((f\circ\bary)^{\times 2}(E_{S}^{\circ\nu}))\times E^{\prime}\\
\subseteq((f\circ\bary)^{\times 2}(E_{S}))^{\circ\nu}\times E^{\prime}\subseteq E^{\circ\nu}\times E^{\prime}

and, by
(12.2a)

\Big\{{\begin{pmatrix}{f(g\bary(\sigma)),g\lambda}\\
{gf(\bary(\sigma)),g\lambda}\end{pmatrix}}\,\Big|\,(\sigma,\lambda)\in S(D^{(L)})\times\Lambda,g\in M\Big\}\subseteq E\times\Big\{{\begin{pmatrix}{\lambda}\\
{\lambda}\end{pmatrix}}\;\big|\;\lambda\in\Lambda\Big\}.

Thus

\supp_{2}(f\circ\bary\times\id_{\Lambda})_{*}\Phi\subseteq\Big(E^{\circ\nu}\times E^{\prime}\Big)\circ\Big(E\times\Big\{{\begin{pmatrix}{\lambda}\\
{\lambda}\end{pmatrix}}\;\big|\;\lambda\in\Lambda\Big\}\Big)\subseteq E^{\circ(\nu+1)}\times E^{\prime}.

∎

12.
c
.
Construction of
r
and
H

We write
\mathbf{C}_{*}(D)\in\ch\mathcal{S}^{U}(S(X))
for the simplicial chain complex of
D

and
\mathbf{S}_{*}(X)\in\ch\mathcal{S}^{G}(S(X))
for the singular chain complex of
X
. Let

i\colon\mathbf{C}_{*}(D)\to\mathbf{S}_{*}(X)
be the inclusion. For
k,l\leq L
we define

S^{k,l}\subseteq S(X)
as the collection of all singular simplices in
X
that are
contained in
D^{(l)}
and are of diameter
<k\rho
.

Lemma 12.4
.

There is a chain map
r\colon\mathbf{S}_{*}(X)\to\mathbf{C}_{*}(D)
and a chain homotopy

H\colon\mathbf{S}_{*}(X)\to\mathbf{S}_{*}(X)
in
\ch\mathcal{S}^{U}(S(X))
with

r\circ i=\id_{\mathbf{C}_{*}(B)}
,
H\colon i\circ r\simeq\id_{\mathbf{S}_{*}(X)}
satisfying
the following: if
{\begin{pmatrix}{\sigma^{\prime}}\\
{\sigma}\end{pmatrix}}\in\supp_{2}r\cup\supp_{2}H
with

\sigma\in S^{k,l}
and
k\leq L-1,l\leq L
, then

(12.4a)

\sigma^{\prime}\in S^{k+1,l}
;

(12.4b)

{\begin{pmatrix}{\sigma^{\prime}}\\
{\sigma}\end{pmatrix}}\in E_{S}^{\circ 2}
.

Proof.

We will use
\Image\sigma
and
(\Image\sigma)^{\rho}
as introduced in
Subsection
10.d
. We write

i^{1}\colon\mathbf{C}_{*}(D)\to\mathbf{S}_{*}(D)
for the inclusion. By
Lemma
10.5
there is

r^{1}\colon\mathbf{S}_{*}(D)\to\mathbf{C}_{*}(D)
with
r^{1}\circ i^{1}=\id_{\mathbf{C}_{*}(D)}

and a chain homotopy
H^{1}\colon\mathbf{S}_{*}(D)\to\mathbf{S}_{*+1}(D)
for

i^{1}\circ r^{1}\simeq\id_{\mathbf{S}_{*}(D)}
. Moreover,
Lemma
10.5
also yields

(12.4c)

if

{\begin{pmatrix}{\sigma^{\prime}}\\
{\sigma}\end{pmatrix}}\in\supp_{2}(r^{1})\cup\in\supp_{2}(H^{1})
, then

\Image\sigma^{\prime}\subseteq(\Image\sigma)^{\rho}
.

Next we use that
H^{0}
is not expanding and apply
Lemma
10.4
to the homotopy
H^{0}
and obtain
a chain homotopy

\widetilde{H}^{0}\colon(i^{0})_{*}\circ(r^{0})_{*}\simeq\id_{\mathbf{S}_{*}(X)}
such
that

(12.4d)

if

{\begin{pmatrix}{\sigma^{\prime}}\\
{\sigma}\end{pmatrix}}\in\supp_{2}(\widetilde{H}^{0})
, then

\diam\Image(\sigma^{\prime})<\diam\Image(\sigma)+\rho
and

\Image\sigma^{\prime}\subseteq\Image(H^{0}\circ(\sigma\times\id_{[0,1]}))
.

We have
i=(i^{0})_{*}\circ i^{1}\colon\mathbf{C}_{*}(D)\to\mathbf{S}_{*}(X)
and set

\displaystyle r

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle r^{1}\circ(r^{0})_{*}\colon\mathbf{S}_{*}(X)\to\mathbf{C}_{*}(D);

\displaystyle H

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle(i^{0})_{*}\circ H^{1}\circ(r^{0})_{*}+(\widetilde{H}^{0})_{*}\colon i\circ r\simeq\id_{\mathbf{S}_{*}(X)}.

Suppose
{\begin{pmatrix}{\sigma^{\prime}}\\
{\sigma}\end{pmatrix}}\in\supp_{2}r
with
\sigma\in S^{k,l}
.
As
r
is a map to
\mathbf{C}_{*}(D)
,
\sigma^{\prime}
is simplex in the
triangulation of
D
, in particular
\sigma^{\prime}\in S^{k+1,l}
. Now

r_{\sigma}^{\sigma^{\prime}}\neq 0
implies that there is
\tau
with

(r^{1})_{\tau}^{\sigma^{\prime}}\neq 0
and
((r^{0})_{*})_{\sigma}^{\tau}\neq 0
. The
latter means
\tau=r^{0}\circ\sigma=H^{0}(\sigma(\--),1)
. In
particular,
{\begin{pmatrix}{\tau}\\
{\sigma}\end{pmatrix}}\in E_{S}
. As
H^{0}
is non-expanding,

\diam\Image\tau\leq\diam\Image\sigma<k\rho
.
By
(12.4c)
,
\Image\sigma^{\prime}\subseteq(\Image\tau)^{\rho}
.
Thus

d_{X}(\bary(\sigma^{\prime}),\bary(\tau))\leq\rho+\diam\Image\tau<(k+1)\rho\leq L\rho
and
{\begin{pmatrix}{\sigma^{\prime}}\\
{\tau}\end{pmatrix}}\in E_{S}
. Therefore

{\begin{pmatrix}{\sigma^{\prime}}\\
{\sigma}\end{pmatrix}}\in E_{S}^{\circ 2}
.

Suppose
{\begin{pmatrix}{\sigma^{\prime}}\\
{\sigma}\end{pmatrix}}\in\supp_{2}H
with
\sigma\in S^{k,l}
.
Then
{\begin{pmatrix}{\sigma^{\prime}}\\
{\sigma}\end{pmatrix}}\in\supp_{2}\widetilde{H}
or

{\begin{pmatrix}{\sigma^{\prime}}\\
{\sigma}\end{pmatrix}}\in\supp_{2}((i^{0})_{*}\circ H^{1}\circ(r^{0}))
. In
the first case,
(12.4d)
implies

\diam\sigma^{\prime}<\diam\sigma+\rho<(k+1)\rho
. Moreover,

\Image\sigma^{\prime}\subseteq H_{0}(\Image\sigma\times[0,1])
. As
H^{0}

preserves
D^{(l)}
, this implies
\sigma^{\prime}\in S^{k+1,l}
. Also

\bary(\sigma^{\prime})=H_{0}(x,t)
for some
t\in[0,1]
and

x\in\Image\sigma
. Let
\tau:=H_{0}(\sigma(\--),t)
. Then

{\begin{pmatrix}{\sigma}\\
{\tau}\end{pmatrix}}\in E_{S}
. Also
\bary(\sigma^{\prime})\in\Image\tau
.
As
H_{0}(\--,t)
is non-expanding,

d_{X}(\bary(\sigma^{\prime}),\bary(\tau))<\diam\Image\tau<\diam\Image\sigma<k\rho
. Thus
{\begin{pmatrix}{\tau}\\
{\sigma}\end{pmatrix}}\in E_{S}
. Altogether,

{\begin{pmatrix}{\sigma^{\prime}}\\
{\sigma}\end{pmatrix}}\in E_{S}^{\circ 2}
.

In the second case there are
\tau
,
\tau^{\prime}
with

{\begin{pmatrix}{\sigma^{\prime}}\\
{\tau^{\prime}}\end{pmatrix}}\in\supp_{2}(i^{0})_{*}
,

{\begin{pmatrix}{\tau^{\prime}}\\
{\tau}\end{pmatrix}}\in\supp_{2}H^{1}
, and

{\begin{pmatrix}{\tau}\\
{\sigma}\end{pmatrix}}\in\supp_{2}(r^{0})_{*}
. Now

{\begin{pmatrix}{\sigma^{\prime}}\\
{\tau^{\prime}}\end{pmatrix}}\in\supp_{2}(i^{0})_{*}
means
\tau^{\prime}=\sigma^{\prime}
, while

{\begin{pmatrix}{\tau}\\
{\sigma}\end{pmatrix}}\in\supp_{2}(r^{0})_{*}
means

\tau=r^{0}\circ\sigma=H^{0}(\sigma(\--),1)
. In particular,

{\begin{pmatrix}{\tau}\\
{\sigma}\end{pmatrix}}\in E_{S}
. Also, as
H^{0}
is non-expanding,

\diam\Image\tau\leq\diam\Image\sigma<k\sigma
.
Using
(12.4c)
,
{\begin{pmatrix}{\tau^{\prime}}\\
{\tau}\end{pmatrix}}\in\supp_{2}H^{1}

implies
\Image\tau^{\prime}\subseteq(\Image\tau)^{\rho}
. In particular,

d_{X}(\bary(\tau^{\prime}),x)<\rho
for some
x\in\Image\tau
. Moreover,

\diam\Image\sigma^{\prime}=\diam\Image\tau^{\prime}<\diam\Image\tau+\rho<(k+1)\rho
. As
H^{1}
is a map to
\mathbf{S}_{*}(D)
, we have

\Image\sigma^{\prime}=\Image\tau^{\prime}\subseteq D
. In particular,

\sigma^{\prime}\in S^{k+1,l}
. Now

d_{X}(\bary(\sigma^{\prime}),\bary(\tau))=d_{X}(\bary(\tau^{\prime}),\bary(\tau))\\
\leq d_{X}(\bary(\tau^{\prime}),x)+d_{X}(x,\bary(\tau))<\rho+\diam\Image\tau<\rho+k\rho=(k+1)\rho

implies
{\begin{pmatrix}{\sigma^{\prime}}\\
{\tau}\end{pmatrix}}\in E_{S}
. Thus

{\begin{pmatrix}{\sigma^{\prime}}\\
{\tau}\end{pmatrix}}\in E_{S}^{\circ 2}
.
∎

12.
d
.
Estimates over
S(X)\times\Lambda

We now fix
r
and
H
as in Lemma
12.4
.
We obtain a homotopy coherent functor as in (
11.16
)

F=(F^{0},F^{1},\dots,)\colon\mathcal{B}_{G,U}(\Lambda)\to\ch\mathcal{B}_{G}(S(X)\times\Lambda).

Recall that
F_{0}(\mathbf{B})=\mathbf{C}(D)_{*}\otimes\mathbf{B}
.
Its
n
-th chain module is
F_{0}(\mathbf{B})_{n}=\mathbf{C}(D)_{n}\otimes\mathbf{B}
.
Its
n
-th boundary map is
{\partial}_{n}^{F_{0}(\mathbf{B})}={\partial}^{\mathbf{C}_{*}(D)}_{n}\otimes\id_{\mathbf{B}}
.

Lemma 12.5
.

Let
\mathbf{B}\in\mathcal{B}_{G,U}(\Lambda)
.
Then

(12.5a)

\supp_{G}F_{0}(\mathbf{B})_{n}\subseteq U
,
\supp_{2}F_{0}(\mathbf{B})_{n}\subseteq E_{S}\times\supp_{2}\mathbf{B}
;

(12.5b)

\supp_{G}{\partial}_{n}^{F_{0}(\mathbf{B})}\subseteq U
,
\supp_{2}{\partial}_{n}^{F_{0}(\mathbf{B})}\subseteq E_{S}\times\supp_{2}\mathbf{B}
.

Proof.

We have

\supp_{2}\id_{\mathbf{C}_{n}(D)}\subseteq\{{\begin{pmatrix}{\sigma}\\
{\sigma}\end{pmatrix}}\mid\sigma\in S(D)\}\subset E_{S}
. Diameters of simplices in the triangulation of
D
are

<\rho
. If
(\partial_{n}^{\mathbf{C}_{*}(D)})_{\sigma}^{\sigma^{\prime}}\neq 0
, then

\Image\sigma^{\prime}\subseteq\Image\sigma
and so

d_{X}(\bary{\sigma^{\prime}},\bary{\sigma})<\rho
. Therefore

\supp_{2}\partial_{n}^{\mathbf{C}_{*}(D)}\subseteq E_{S}
. We have

\supp_{G}\mathbf{B}=\supp_{G}\id_{\mathbf{B}}\subseteq U
, as

\mathbf{B}\in\mathcal{B}_{G,U}(\Lambda)
. Both
(12.5a)
,
and
(12.5b)
follow now from Lemma
11.9
.
∎

We will now use summands and corners as in Remarks
4.14

and
4.15
relative to
S^{k,l}\subseteq S(X)
. For

\mathbf{B}\in\mathcal{B}_{G,U}(\Lambda)
we abbreviate

(\mathbf{S}_{n}(X)\otimes\mathbf{B})_{k,l}:=(\mathbf{S}_{n}(X)\otimes\mathbf{B})|_{S^{s,l}\times\Lambda}
and
write

(\mathbf{S}_{n}(X)\otimes\mathbf{B})_{k,l}\xrightarrow{i_{k,l}}\mathbf{S}_{n}(X)\otimes\mathbf{B}\xrightarrow{r^{k,l}}(\mathbf{S}_{n}(X)\otimes\mathbf{B})_{k,l}

for the corresponding inclusion and retraction. For

\Phi\colon\mathbf{S}_{n}(X)\otimes\mathbf{B}\to\mathbf{S}_{n^{\prime}}(X)\otimes\mathbf{B}^{\prime}
we set

\Phi_{k,l}^{k^{\prime},l^{\prime}}:=r^{k^{\prime},l^{\prime}}\circ\Phi\circ i_{k,l}
. We also set

(i\otimes\id_{\mathbf{B}})^{k^{\prime},l^{\prime}}:=r^{k^{\prime},l^{\prime}}\circ(i\otimes\id_{\mathbf{B}})
, and

(r\otimes\id_{\mathbf{B}})_{k,l}:=(r\otimes\id_{\mathbf{B}})\circ i^{k,l}
.
This means

\displaystyle(\Phi_{k,l}^{k^{\prime},l^{\prime}})_{\sigma,s}^{\sigma^{\prime},s^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\Phi_{\sigma,s}^{\sigma^{\prime},s^{\prime}}\hskip 30.56937pt&\sigma\in S^{k,l},\sigma^{\prime}\in S^{k^{\prime},l^{\prime}};\\
0&\text{else};\end{cases}

\displaystyle((i\otimes\id_{\mathbf{B}})^{k^{\prime},l^{\prime}})_{\sigma,s}^{\sigma^{\prime},s^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\begin{cases}(i\otimes\id_{\mathbf{B}})_{\sigma,s}^{\sigma^{\prime},s^{\prime}}&\sigma^{\prime}\in S^{k^{\prime},l^{\prime}};\\
0&\text{else};\end{cases}

\displaystyle((r\otimes\id_{\mathbf{B}})_{k,l})_{\sigma,s}^{\sigma^{\prime},s^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\begin{cases}(r\otimes\id_{\mathbf{B}})_{\sigma,s}^{\sigma^{\prime},s^{\prime}}&\sigma\in S^{k,l};\\
0&\text{else}.\end{cases}

Lemma 12.6
.

Let
k,l\leq L
,
\mathbf{B},\mathbf{B}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{B}_{G,U}(\Lambda)
,

\varphi\colon\mathbf{B}\to\mathbf{B}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{B}_{G,U}(\Lambda)
with

\supp_{G}\varphi\subseteq M
. Set
E^{\prime}:=\supp_{2}\varphi\cup\supp_{2}\mathbf{B}
. Then

(12.6a)

i_{k,l+1}\circ(\id_{\mathbf{S}_{*}(X)}\otimes\varphi)_{k,l}^{k,l+1}=(\id_{\mathbf{S}_{*}(X)}\otimes\varphi)\circ i_{k,l}
(provided
l+1\leq L)
;

(12.6b)

\supp_{2}\big((\id_{\mathbf{S}_{*}(X)}\otimes\varphi)_{k,l}^{k,l+1}\big)\subseteq E_{S}\times E^{\prime}
(provided
l+1\leq L)
;

(12.6c)

i_{k+1,l}\circ(H\otimes\id_{\mathbf{B}})_{k,l}^{k+1,l}=(H\otimes\id_{\mathbf{B}})\circ i_{k,l}
(provided
k+1\leq L)
;

(12.6d)

\supp_{2}\big((H\otimes\id_{\mathbf{B}})_{k,l}^{k+1,l}\big)\subseteq E_{S}^{\circ 2}\times E^{\prime}
(provided
k+1\leq L)
;

(12.6e)

i_{k,l}\circ(i\otimes\id_{\mathbf{B}})^{k,l}=i\otimes\id_{\mathbf{B}}
;

(12.6f)

\supp_{2}\big((i\otimes\id_{\mathbf{B}})^{k,l}\big)\subseteq E_{S}^{\circ 2}\times E^{\prime}
;

(12.6g)

(r\otimes\id_{\mathbf{B}})_{k,l}=(r\otimes\id_{\mathbf{B}})\circ i_{k,l}
;

(12.6h)

\supp_{2}\big((r\otimes\id_{\mathbf{B}})_{k,l}\big)\subseteq E_{S}^{\circ 2}\times E^{\prime}
.

Proof.

From
(11.9d)
we obtain

\supp_{G}\id_{\mathbf{S}_{*}(X)}\otimes\varphi\subseteq\supp_{G}\varphi\subseteq M
. Using

M\cdot D^{(l)}\subseteq D^{(l+1)}
we obtain

(\supp_{G}\id_{\mathbf{S}_{*}(X)}\otimes\varphi)\cdot S^{k,l}\subseteq M\cdot S^{k,l}\subseteq S^{k,l+1}
. If

{\begin{pmatrix}{\sigma^{\prime},\lambda^{\prime}}\\
{\sigma,\lambda}\end{pmatrix}}\in\supp_{2}\id_{\mathbf{S}_{*}(X)}\otimes\varphi
,
then, by
(11.9c)
,

{\begin{pmatrix}{\sigma^{\prime}}\\
{\sigma}\end{pmatrix}}\in\supp_{2}\id_{\mathbf{S}_{*}(X)}
and so
\sigma=\sigma^{\prime}
. Now
Lemma
4.17

gives
(12.6a)
. Using
(11.9c)

and (
4.16
) we obtain

\supp_{2}(\id_{\mathbf{S}_{*}(X)}\otimes\varphi)_{k,l}^{k,l+1}\subseteq\Big\{{\begin{pmatrix}{\sigma,\lambda^{\prime}}\\
{\sigma,\lambda}\end{pmatrix}}\Big|\sigma\in S^{k,l+1},{\begin{pmatrix}{\lambda^{\prime}}\\
{\lambda}\end{pmatrix}}\in\supp_{2}\varphi\Big\}

and this implies
(12.6b)
.

By
(11.9d)

\supp_{G}H\otimes\id_{\mathbf{B}}
,

\supp_{G}r\otimes\id_{\mathbf{B}}
, and
\supp_{G}i\otimes\id_{\mathbf{B}}
are all contained in

\supp_{G}\mathbf{B}\subseteq U
. As
U
fixes
D^{(l)}
, we have

U\cdot S^{k,l}=S^{k,l}
. If

{\begin{pmatrix}{\sigma^{\prime},\lambda^{\prime}}\\
{\sigma,\lambda}\end{pmatrix}}\in\supp_{2}H\otimes\id_{\mathbf{B}}
, then,
by
(11.9c)
,
{\begin{pmatrix}{\sigma^{\prime}}\\
{\sigma}\end{pmatrix}}\in\supp_{2}H
. If,
in addition,
\sigma\in S^{k,l}
, then, by
(12.4a)
,

\sigma^{\prime}\in S^{k+1,l}
and by
(12.4b)

{\begin{pmatrix}{\sigma^{\prime}}\\
{\sigma}\end{pmatrix}}\in E_{S}^{\circ 2}
. Now
(12.6c)
follows
from Lemma
4.17
and
(12.6d)
follows
from
(11.9c)
and (
4.16
).
As
(12.4a)
and
(12.4b)
also apply to
r
, and
hold directly by definition for

i
,
(12.6e)
,
(12.6f)
,
and
(12.6h)
follow from the same argument.
Finally,
(12.6g)
is the definition of
(r\otimes\id_{\mathbf{B}})_{k,l}
.
∎

Proposition 12.7
.

Let

\mathbf{B}_{0}=(S_{0},\pi_{0},B_{0})\xleftarrow{\varphi_{1}}\dots\xleftarrow{\varphi_{l}}\mathbf{B}_{l}=(S_{l},\pi_{l},B_{l})
be a chain of composable morphisms in

\mathcal{B}_{G,U}(\Lambda)
. Assume
l\leq L
and

\supp_{G}(\varphi_{j})\subseteq M
for all
j
. Let

E^{\prime}:=\bigcup\supp_{2}\varphi_{j}\cup\bigcup_{j}\supp_{2}\mathbf{B}_{j}\subseteq\Lambda\times\Lambda
. Then there are

\Phi_{i}\colon\mathbf{B}^{\prime}_{i}\to\mathbf{B}^{\prime}_{i+1}\in\mathcal{B}_{G,U}(S(X)\times\Lambda)
,
i=0,\dots,2l
such that

F^{l}(\varphi_{1},\dots,\varphi_{l})=\Phi_{2l}\circ\dots\circ\Phi_{0}

and
\supp_{G}\Phi_{i}\subseteq M
,
\supp_{2}\Phi\subseteq E_{S}^{\circ 2}\times E^{\prime}
,

\supp_{1}\mathbf{B}^{\prime}_{i}\subseteq S(D)\times\Lambda
for all
i
.

Proof.

Recall that by construction

F^{l}(\varphi_{1},\dots,\varphi_{l})=\\
\big(r\otimes\id_{\mathbf{B}_{0}}\big)\circ\big(\id_{\mathbf{S}_{*}(X)}\otimes\varphi_{1}\big)\circ\big(H\otimes\id_{\mathbf{B}_{1}}\big)\circ\dots{\\
}\dots\circ\big(H\otimes\id_{\mathbf{B}_{l-1}}\big)\circ\big(\id_{\mathbf{S}_{*}(X)}\otimes\varphi_{l}\big)\circ\big(i\otimes\id_{\mathbf{B}_{l}}\big).

Using
(12.6a)
,
(12.6c)
,
(12.6e)
,
and
(12.6g)
we can rewrite this as

F^{l}(\varphi_{1},\dots,\varphi_{l})=\\
\big(r\otimes\id_{\mathbf{B}_{0}}\big)_{l,l}\circ\big(\id_{\mathbf{S}_{*}(X)}\otimes\varphi_{1}\big)^{l,l}_{l,l-1}\circ\big(H\otimes\id_{\mathbf{B}_{1}}\big)^{l,l-1}_{l-1,l-1}\circ\dots{\\
}\dots\circ\big(H\otimes\id_{\mathbf{B}_{l-1}}\big)_{1,1}^{2,1}\circ\big(\id_{\mathbf{S}_{*}(X)}\otimes\varphi_{l}\big)_{1,0}^{1,1}\circ\big(i\otimes\id_{\mathbf{B}_{l}}\big)^{1,0}.

By
(11.9d)
(and since
U\subseteq M
) for each of these
factors
\supp_{G}
is contained in
M
.
By
(12.6b)
,
(12.6d)
,
(12.6f)
,
and
(12.6h)
,
\supp_{2}
of each factor, is contained in

E_{S}^{\circ 2}\times E^{\prime}
61
61

61

Here we use that
E_{S}\subseteq E_{S}^{\circ 2}
for
our specific
E_{S}
.
. Finally, the domains of the factors are of the form

(\mathbf{S}_{n}(X)\otimes\mathbf{B}_{i})_{k,l}
(or
\mathbf{C}(D)\otimes\mathbf{B}_{0}
) and have therefore

\supp_{1}
contained in
S(D^{(L)})
.
∎

12.
e
.
Estimates over
J\times\Lambda

We now consider the homotopy coherent functor

\widetilde{F}:=(f\circ\bary\times\id_{\Lambda})_{*}\circ F\colon\mathcal{B}_{G,U}(\Lambda)\to\ch\mathcal{B}_{G}(J\times\Lambda).

Proposition 12.8
.

(12.8a)

Let
\mathbf{B}\in\mathcal{B}_{G,U}(\Lambda)
and
E^{\prime}:=\supp_{2}\mathbf{B}
. Then

\begin{array}[]{rlrl}\supp_{G}\widetilde{F}_{0}(\mathbf{B})_{n}&\subseteq U,&\supp_{2}\widetilde{F}_{0}(\mathbf{B})_{n}&\subseteq E^{\circ 2}\times E^{\prime},\\
\supp_{G}{\partial}_{n}^{\widetilde{F}_{0}(\mathbf{B})}&\subseteq U,&\supp_{2}{\partial}_{n}^{\widetilde{F}_{0}(\mathbf{B})}&\subseteq E^{\circ 2}\times E^{\prime};\end{array}

(12.8b)

Let

\mathbf{B}_{0}=(S_{0},\pi_{0},B_{0})\xleftarrow{\varphi_{1}}\dots\xleftarrow{\varphi_{l}}\mathbf{B}_{l}=(S_{l},\pi_{l},B_{l})
be a chain of composable morphisms in
\mathcal{B}_{G,U}(\Lambda)
.
Assume
l\leq L
and
\supp_{G}(\varphi_{j})\subseteq M
for all
j
.
Let
E^{\prime}:=\bigcup\supp_{2}\varphi_{j}\cup\bigcup_{j}\supp_{2}\mathbf{B}_{j}
. Then

\begin{array}[]{rl}\supp_{2}\widetilde{F}^{l}(\varphi_{1},\dots,\varphi_{l})&\subseteq(M^{2l}(E^{\circ 3}\times E^{\prime}))^{\circ(2l+1)},\\
\supp_{G}\widetilde{F}^{l}(\varphi_{1},\dots,\varphi_{l})&\subseteq M^{2l+1}.\end{array}

The point here is that the upper bounds are in terms of
E
,
E^{\prime}
,
M
and
l
and
independent of
\mathbf{B}
and the
\varphi_{i}
.

Proof.

Lemma
12.5
directly implies
\supp_{G}\widetilde{F}_{0}(\mathbf{B})_{n}
,

\supp_{G}{\partial}_{n}^{\widetilde{F}_{0}(\mathbf{B})}\subseteq U
. Lemma
12.5
together
with Lemma
12.3
implies

\supp_{2}\widetilde{F}_{0}(\mathbf{B})_{n}\subseteq E^{\circ 2}\times E^{\prime}
,

\supp_{2}{\partial}_{n}^{\widetilde{F}_{0}(\mathbf{B})}\subseteq E^{\circ 2}\times E^{\prime}
62
62

62

As
U

fixes
D
pointwise we could here use
E
instead of
E^{\circ 2}
, but the
precise form of the estimates is not important.
.
Thus
(12.8a)
holds.

Applying
(f\circ\bary\times\id_{\Lambda})_{*}
to the factorization from
Proposition
12.7
gives a factorization

\widetilde{F}^{l}(\varphi_{1},\dots,\varphi_{l})=\widetilde{\Phi}_{2l}\circ\dots\circ\widetilde{\Phi}_{0}.

The estimates for the
\Phi_{i}
from Proposition
12.7
translate
to the
\tilde{\Phi}_{i}
. We get
\supp_{G}\widetilde{\Phi}_{i}\subseteq M
directly from
Proposition
12.7
. Using in addition Lemma
12.3
we
get
\supp_{2}\widetilde{\Phi}_{i}\subseteq E^{\circ 3}\times E^{\prime}
. As
\supp_{G}
is
submultiplicative we get

\supp_{G}\widetilde{F}^{l}(\varphi_{1},\dots,\varphi_{l})\subseteq M^{2l+1}
. Using the
composition formula
4.3
for
\supp_{2}
it is not difficult to obtain an
explicit bound for
\supp_{2}\widetilde{F}^{l}(\varphi_{1},\dots,\varphi_{l})
, for example

(M^{2l}(E^{\circ 3}\times E^{\prime}))^{\circ(2l+1)}
works.
Thus
(12.8b)
holds.
∎

13.
Construction of the transfer

Let
G
be a reductive
p
-adic
group and
X
be the associated extended Bruhat-Tits building. For each

{\underline{t}}\in\mathbb{N}^{\times 2}
we will construct the data considered in
Section
12
, i.e.,

-

numbers
L_{\underline{t}}\in\mathbb{N}
,
\rho_{\underline{t}}>0
;

-

compact open subgroups
U_{\underline{t}}\subseteq G
and a compact subset

M_{\underline{t}}\subseteq G
with
U_{\underline{t}}\subseteq M
;

-

a subspace
D_{\underline{t}}\subseteq X
with a locally ordered triangulation
all whose simplices are of diameter
<\rho_{\underline{t}}
;

-

a sequence of further subspaces

D_{\underline{t}}=D^{(0)}_{\underline{t}}\subseteq D^{(1)}_{\underline{t}}\subseteq\dots\subseteq D^{(L_{\underline{t}})}_{\underline{t}}
with

M_{\underline{t}}\cdot D^{l-1}_{\underline{t}}\subseteq D_{\underline{t}}^{l}
for
l=1,\dots,L_{t}
, and such that
D^{L}_{\underline{t}}

is pointwise fixed by
U_{\underline{t}}
;

-

a retraction
r^{0}_{\underline{t}}\colon X\to D_{\underline{t}}
for the inclusion

i^{0}_{\underline{t}}\colon D_{\underline{t}}\to X
, i.e.,

r^{0}_{\underline{t}}\circ i^{0}_{\underline{t}}=\id_{D_{\underline{t}}}
;

-

a homotopy

H^{0}_{\underline{t}}\colon i^{0}_{\underline{t}}\circ r^{0}_{\underline{t}}\simeq\id_{X}
, that is not expanding, i.e., for all
\tau\in[0,1]
,
x,x^{\prime}\in X
we
will have

d_{X}(H^{0}_{\underline{t}}(x,\tau),H^{0}_{\underline{t}}(x^{\prime},\tau))\leq d_{X}(x,x^{\prime})
;

-

a map
f_{\underline{t}}\colon X\to|\mathbf{J}_{{\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.0pt\mathrm{vcy}}}(G)|^{\wedge}
;

-

a subset

E_{\underline{t}}\subseteq|\mathbf{J}_{{\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.0pt\mathrm{vcy}}}(G)|^{\wedge}\times|\mathbf{J}_{{\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.0pt\mathrm{vcy}}}(G)|^{\wedge}
.

Let
\mathbf{M}\in\mathcal{R}^{0}
. As in Section
12
(with

\Lambda=|\mathbf{M}|^{\wedge}
) we obtain then for every
{\underline{t}}
a homotopy
coherent functor

F_{\underline{t}}=(F_{\underline{t}}^{0},F_{\underline{t}}^{1},\dots)\colon\mathcal{B}_{G,U_{\underline{t}}}(|\mathbf{M}|^{\wedge})\to\ch\mathcal{B}_{G}(S(X)\times|\mathbf{M}|^{\wedge})

and its composition

\widetilde{F}_{\underline{t}}:=((f_{\underline{t}}\circ\bary)\times\id_{|\mathbf{M}|^{\wedge}})_{*}\circ F_{\underline{t}}\colon\mathcal{B}_{G,U_{\underline{t}}}(|\mathbf{M}|^{\wedge})\to\ch\mathcal{B}_{G}(|\mathbf{J}_{{\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.0pt\mathrm{vcy}}}(G)|^{\wedge}\times|\mathbf{M}|^{\wedge}).

We will verify Assumption
12.2
for each
{\underline{t}}
. Thus the support
estimates from Proposition
12.8
will be
available. These estimates will allow us to combine the

(f_{\underline{t}}\times\id_{|\mathbf{M}|^{\wedge}})_{*}\circ F_{\underline{t}}
to
obtain a homotopy coherent functor

\widetilde{F}_{\mathbf{M}}\colon\matheurm{D}^{0}_{G}(\mathbf{M})\to\ch\matheurm{D}^{0}_{G}(\mathbf{J}_{{\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.0pt\mathrm{vcy}}}(G))

and Lemmas
11.19
and
11.20
will imply that
the
\tilde{F}_{\mathbf{M}}
induce the desired transfer for

\mathbf{K}\matheurm{D}^{0}_{G}\big(\mathbf{J}_{{\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.0pt\mathrm{vcy}}}(G)\times-\big)\to\mathbf{K}\matheurm{D}^{0}_{G}(-)

in
\mathcal{R}^{0}\text{-}\matheurm{Spectra}
.

13.
a
.
Retractions to balls in
X

We write
d_{X}
for the
\operatorname{CAT}(0)
-metric of
X
. We fix a base point
x_{0}\in X
and
write
D_{R}
for the closed ball in
X
centered at
x_{0}
. We write

\pi_{R}\colon X\to D_{R}
for the radial projection. We define the radial homotopy

H_{R}\colon i_{R}\circ r_{R}\simeq\id_{X}
with

H_{R}(x,\tau)=\pi_{\tau d_{X}(x,x_{0})+(1-\tau)R}(x)
. The
\operatorname{CAT}(0)
-condition implies that

H_{R}
is contracting, i.e.,

d_{X}\big(H^{X}_{R}(x,\tau),H_{R}^{X}(x^{\prime},\tau)\big)\leq d_{X}(x,x^{\prime})

for all
R\geq 0
,
\tau\in[0,1]
and
x,x^{\prime}\in X
. Let
U:=G_{x_{0}}
be the
stabilizer of
x_{0}
in
G
. As the action of
G
on
X
is smooth and proper, this is a
compact open subgroup of
G
.

13.
b
.
Choosing the data

We choose for
j\in\mathbb{N}
numbers
\epsilon_{j}>0
,
\eta_{j}>0
,
L_{j}\in\mathbb{N}
and
compact subsets
M_{j}\subseteq G
such that

\epsilon_{j}\to 0,\eta_{j}\to 0,L_{j}\to\infty\;\text{as}\;j\to\infty

and that for any
K\subseteq G
compact we have
K\subseteq M_{j}
for all but finitely
many
j
. We also assume that each
M_{j}
contains an open subgroup
63
63

63

To construct

M_{j}
we can choose a metric on
G
and take
M_{j}
to be the closed ball of radius
j

around the unit in
G
. Since
G
is a td-group,
M_{j}
contains a compact open subgroup
of
G
.
. We set
L^{+}_{j}:=L_{j}\cdot\max\{d_{X}(x_{0},gx_{0})\mid g\in M_{j}\}
.

Let
N
be the first number appearing in Theorem
D.1
. Given
t_{0}\in\mathbb{N}
,
we obtain from Theorem
D.1
applied to
M=M_{t_{0}}
and

\epsilon=\epsilon_{t_{0}}
a number
\beta_{t_{0}}
and
\mathcal{V}_{t_{0}}\subseteq{\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.0pt\mathrm{vcy}}

finite. Given further
t_{1}\in\mathbb{N}
, we obtain again from Theorem
D.1

applied to
\eta=\eta_{t_{1}}
and
L=L^{+}_{t_{1}}
numbers
R_{t_{0},t_{1}}>0
,

\rho^{\prime}_{t_{0},t_{1}}>0
and a map

f_{t_{0},t_{1}}\colon X\to|\mathbf{J}^{N}_{\mathcal{V}_{t_{0}}}(G)|^{\wedge}

such that

(13.1a)

for
x\in D_{R_{t_{0},t_{1}}+L^{+}_{t_{1}}}
,
g\in M_{t_{0}}
we have

d_{\mathbf{J}\text{-}\mathrm{fol}}\big(f_{t_{0},t_{1}}(gx),gf_{t_{0},t_{1}}(x)\big)<(\beta_{t_{0}},\eta_{t_{1}},\epsilon_{t_{0}});

(13.1b)

for
x\in D_{R_{t_{0},t_{1}}+L^{+}_{t_{1}}}
,
R^{\prime}\geq R_{t_{0},t_{1}}
we have

d_{\mathbf{J}\text{-}\mathrm{fol}}\big(f_{t_{0},t_{1}}(x),f_{t_{0},t_{1}}(\pi_{R^{\prime}}(x))\big)<(\beta_{t_{0}},\eta_{t_{1}},\epsilon_{t_{0}});

(13.1c)

for all
x,x^{\prime}\in X
with
d_{X}(x,x^{\prime})<\rho^{\prime}_{t_{1}}
we have

d_{\mathbf{J}\text{-}\mathrm{fol}}\big(f_{t_{0},t_{1}}(x),f_{t_{0},t_{1}}(x^{\prime})\big)<(\beta_{t_{0}},\eta_{t_{1}},\epsilon_{t_{0}}).

Here and later we abbreviate
d_{\mathbf{J}\text{-}\mathrm{fol}}=d_{\mathbf{J}_{{\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.0pt\mathrm{vcy}}}(G)\text{-}\mathrm{fol}}
. For

{\underline{t}}=(t_{0},t_{1})
we now define respectively choose

-

L_{\underline{t}}:=L_{t_{1}}
,

\rho_{\underline{t}}:=\rho^{\prime}_{\underline{t}}/(L_{t}+1)
,

M_{\underline{t}}:=M_{t_{0}}
,
D_{\underline{t}}:=D_{R_{t}}
; as balls in a
building the
D_{\underline{t}}
can be triangulated;

-

D_{\underline{t}}^{(l)}:=D_{R_{t_{0},t_{1}}+\frac{l}{L_{\underline{t}}}L^{+}_{t_{1}}}
for
l=0,\dots,L_{t}
;

-

U_{\underline{t}}
a compact open subgroup
that fixes
D_{\underline{t}}^{(L_{\underline{t}})}

pointwise and is contained in
M_{\underline{t}}
;

-

i^{0}_{\underline{t}}\colon D_{\underline{t}}\to X
the inclusion;

-

r^{0}_{\underline{t}}:=\pi_{R_{{\underline{t}}}}\colon X\to D_{\underline{t}}
,
the radial projection;

-

f_{\underline{t}}:=f_{t_{0},t_{1}}
;

-

H^{0}_{\underline{t}}:=H_{R_{\underline{t}}}
the radial homotopy

i_{\underline{t}}^{0}\circ r_{\underline{t}}^{0}\simeq\id_{X}
;

-

E_{\underline{t}}:=\big\{{\begin{pmatrix}{z}\\
{z^{\prime}}\end{pmatrix}}\;\big|\;d_{\mathbf{J}\text{-}\mathrm{fol}}(z,z^{\prime})<(\beta_{t_{0}},\eta_{t_{1}},\epsilon_{t_{0}})\big\}
.

We note that
(13.1a)
,
(13.1b)
and
(13.1c)
imply that
Assumption
12.2
holds. We can now apply the construction from
Section
12
and obtain for

\mathbf{M}\in\mathcal{R}^{0}
the
homotopy coherent functor

((f_{\underline{t}}\circ\bary)\times\id_{|\mathbf{M}|^{\wedge}})_{*}\circ F_{\underline{t}}\colon\mathcal{B}_{G,U_{\underline{t}}}(|\mathbf{M}|^{\wedge})\to\ch\mathcal{B}_{G}(|\mathbf{J}_{{\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.0pt\mathrm{vcy}}}(G)|^{\wedge}\times|\mathbf{M}|^{\wedge}).

13.
c
.
The
\tilde{F}_{t}
combine to a homotopy coherent functor on

\matheurm{D}^{0}_{G,\mathbf{U}}(\mathbf{M})
.

We will now use the
G
-control structure
\mathfrak{D}^{0}({\bf\Sigma})
from
Definition
7.7
for
{\bf\Sigma}=\mathbf{J}_{{\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.0pt\mathrm{vcy}}}(G)
.

Lemma 13.2
.

Let

E:=\Big\{{\begin{pmatrix}{z^{\prime},{\underline{t}}}\\
{z,{\underline{t}}}\end{pmatrix}}\;\Big|\;{\underline{t}}\in\mathbb{N}^{\times 2},{\begin{pmatrix}{z^{\prime}}\\
{z}\end{pmatrix}}\in E_{\underline{t}}\Big\}
.
Then
E\in\mathfrak{D}^{0}_{2}(\mathbf{J}_{{\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.0pt\mathrm{vcy}}}(G))
.

Proof.

We need to verify the foliated control condition from Definition
7.7
.
Let
\epsilon>0
be given. Choose
k_{0}
such that
\epsilon_{t_{0}}<\epsilon
for all

t_{0}\geq k_{0}
. Let
t_{0}\geq k_{0}
be given. Set
\beta:=\beta_{t_{0}}
. Let

\eta>0
be given. Choose
k_{1}
such that
\eta_{t_{1}}<\eta
for all
t_{1}\geq k_{1}
.
Let
t_{1}\geq k_{1}
be given. Set
{\underline{t}}:=(t_{0},t_{1})
. Let

z,z^{\prime}\in|\mathbf{J}_{{\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.0pt\mathrm{vcy}}}(G)|^{\wedge}
be given with

{\begin{pmatrix}{z^{\prime},{\underline{t}}}\\
{z,{\underline{t}}}\end{pmatrix}}\in E
. By definition of
E
we
then have
{\begin{pmatrix}{z^{\prime}}\\
{z}\end{pmatrix}}\in E_{{\underline{t}}}
. By definition of

E_{{\underline{t}}}
we then have

d_{\mathbf{J}\text{-}\mathrm{fol}}(z,z^{\prime})<(\beta_{t_{0}},\eta_{t_{1}},\epsilon_{t_{0}})
. Since

\beta=\beta_{t_{0}}
,
\eta_{t_{1}}<\eta
and
\epsilon_{t_{0}}<\epsilon
this implies

d_{\mathbf{J}\text{-}\mathrm{fol}}(z,z^{\prime})<(\beta,\eta,\epsilon),

as required.
∎

Following Remark
7.17
we will view
\matheurm{D}^{0}_{G}(\mathbf{M})
and

\matheurm{D}^{0}_{G}(\mathbf{J}_{{\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.0pt\mathrm{vcy}}}(G)\times\mathbf{M})
respectively as subcategories of

{\sideset{}{{}^{\prime}}{\textstyle\prod}}_{\mathbb{N}^{\times 2}}\mathcal{B}_{G}(|\mathbf{M}|^{\wedge})
and

{\sideset{}{{}^{\prime}}{\textstyle\prod}}_{\mathbb{N}^{\times 2}}\mathcal{B}_{G}(|\mathbf{J}_{{\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.0pt\mathrm{vcy}}}(G)\times\mathbf{M}|^{\wedge})

respectively. Thus objects are sequences

\mathbf{B}=(\mathbf{B}_{\underline{t}})_{{\underline{t}}\in\mathbb{N}^{\times}}
of objects in

\mathcal{B}_{G}(|\mathbf{M}|^{\wedge})

and
\mathcal{B}_{G}(|\mathbf{J}_{{\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.0pt\mathrm{vcy}}}(G)\times\mathbf{M}|^{\wedge})

respectively satisfying the conditions spelled out in
Remarks
7.17
. Similarly morphisms are equivalence classes of
sequences
(\varphi_{\underline{t}})_{{\underline{t}}\in\mathbb{N}^{\times 2}}
. We
recall that sequences

(\varphi_{\underline{t}})_{{\underline{t}}\in\mathbb{N}^{\times 2}}
and

(\varphi^{\prime}_{\underline{t}})_{{\underline{t}}\in\mathbb{N}^{\times 2}}
are equivalent, if
there is
k_{0}
such that for all
t_{0}\geq k_{0}
there is
k_{1}
such that for all

t_{1}\geq k_{1}
we have
\varphi_{t_{0},t_{1}}=\varphi^{\prime}_{t_{0},t_{1}}
. In particular, we can
ignore all
\varphi_{t_{0},t_{1}}
with
t_{0}
or
t_{1}
small. We define

\matheurm{D}^{0}_{G,\mathbf{U}}(\mathbf{M})
as the full subcategory of
\matheurm{D}^{0}_{G}(\mathbf{M})
on all
objects
\mathbf{B}=(\mathbf{B}_{\underline{t}})_{{\underline{t}}\in\mathbb{N}^{\times 2}}
with

\supp_{G}\mathbf{B}_{\underline{t}}\subseteq U_{\underline{t}}
for all
{\underline{t}}
.
We can now define the homotopy coherent functor

F_{\mathbf{M}}=(F_{\mathbf{M}}^{0},F_{\mathbf{M}}^{1},\dots)\;\colon\matheurm{D}^{0}_{G,\mathbf{U}}(\mathbf{M})\to\ch\matheurm{D}^{0}_{G}\big(\mathbf{J}_{{\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.0pt\mathrm{vcy}}}(G)\times\mathbf{M}\big).

Let
\mathbf{B}=(\mathbf{B}_{\underline{t}})_{{\underline{t}}\in\mathbb{N}^{\times 2}}
be an
object of
\matheurm{D}^{0}_{G}(\mathbf{M})
. We define

F_{\mathbf{M}}^{0}(\mathbf{B}):=\Big(((f_{\underline{t}}\circ\bary)\times\id_{|\mathbf{M}|^{\wedge}})_{*}(F_{\underline{t}}^{0}(\mathbf{B}_{\underline{t}}))\Big)_{{\underline{t}}\in\mathbb{N}^{\times 2}}.

Let
\mathbf{B}_{0}\xleftarrow{\varphi_{1}}\dots\xleftarrow{\varphi_{l}}\mathbf{B}_{l}
be a chain of
composable morphisms in
\matheurm{D}^{0}_{G}(\mathbf{M})
. Write

\varphi_{i}=\big((\varphi_{i})_{\underline{t}}\big)_{{\underline{t}}\in\mathbb{N}^{\times 2}}
. We
define

F_{\mathbf{M}}^{l}(\varphi_{1},\dots,\varphi_{l}):=\Big(((f_{\underline{t}}\circ\bary)\times\id_{|\mathbf{M}|^{\wedge}})_{*}\big(F_{\underline{t}}^{l}\big((\varphi_{1})_{\underline{t}},\dots,(\varphi_{l})_{\underline{t}}\big)\big)\Big)_{{\underline{t}}\in\mathbb{N}^{\times 2}}.

We write
\big(F^{0}_{\mathbf{M}}(\mathbf{B})\big)_{n}
for the
n
-chain module of

F_{\mathbf{M}}^{0}(\mathbf{B})
and
\partial_{n}^{F^{0}_{\mathbf{M}}(\mathbf{B})}
for the
n
-th boundary map.

Lemma 13.3
.

(a)

\big(F^{0}_{\mathbf{M}}(\mathbf{B})\big)_{n}
as above is
an object in
\matheurm{D}_{G}(\mathbf{J}_{{\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.0pt\mathrm{vcy}}}(G)\times\mathbf{M})
;

(b)

\partial_{n}^{F^{0}_{\mathbf{M}}(\mathbf{B})}

as above is a morphism in
\matheurm{D}_{G}(\mathbf{J}_{{\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.0pt\mathrm{vcy}}}(G)\times\mathbf{M})
;

(c)

F_{\mathbf{M}}^{l}(\varphi_{1},\dots,\varphi_{l})
as above
is a morphism in
\ch\matheurm{D}_{G}(\mathbf{J}_{{\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.0pt\mathrm{vcy}}}(G)\times\mathbf{M})
.

Proof.

(a)
We need to verify the four
conditions
(7.18a)

to
(7.18d)
.

For
(7.18a)
we need to check

\supp_{1}\big(F^{0}_{\mathbf{M}}(\mathbf{B})\big)_{n}\in\matheurm{D}^{0}_{1}(\mathbf{J}_{{\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.0pt\mathrm{vcy}}}(G)\times\mathbf{M})
. Let

F:=\bigcup_{{\underline{t}}\in\mathbb{N}^{\times 2}}f_{\underline{t}}(\bary_{n}(D_{{\underline{t}}}))\times\{{\underline{t}}\}.

We claim that
F\in\mathfrak{D}_{1}^{0}(\mathbf{J}_{{\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.0pt\mathrm{vcy}}}(G))
. This amounts to checking the
three conditions in
(7.7a)
for
F
: Finiteness over
\mathbb{N}^{\times 2}
is
clear as
D_{{\underline{t}}}
is compact and has only finitely many simplices. For the other two
conditions we use that
f_{\underline{t}}=f_{t_{0},t_{1}}
is a map to

|\mathbf{J}^{N}_{\mathcal{V}_{t_{0}}}(G)|^{\wedge}\subseteq|\mathbf{J}_{{\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.0pt\mathrm{vcy}}}(G)|^{\wedge}
. Thus
F
has
finite dimensional support. As
\mathcal{V}_{t_{0}}
is finite
|J^{N}_{\mathcal{V}_{t_{0}}}(G)|
is a
finite subcomplex of
|J_{{\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.0pt\mathrm{vcy}}}(G)|
. It follows that
F
has compact support in

|J_{{\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.0pt\mathrm{vcy}}}(G)|
. So
F\in\mathfrak{D}_{1}^{0}(\mathbf{J}_{{\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.0pt\mathrm{vcy}}}(G))
.
Set
F^{\prime}:=\supp_{1}\mathbf{B}\in\mathfrak{D}_{1}^{0}(\mathbf{M})
.
By
(11.9b)

\supp_{1}((f_{\underline{t}}\circ\bary)\times\id_{|\mathbf{M}|^{\wedge}})_{*}(F_{{\underline{t}}}^{0}(\mathbf{B}_{\underline{t}}))=f_{\underline{t}}(\bary_{n}(D_{\underline{t}}))\times\supp_{1}\mathbf{B}_{t}.

Thus

\displaystyle\supp_{1}\big(F^{0}_{\mathbf{M}}(\mathbf{B})\big)_{n}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bigcup_{{\underline{t}}\in\mathbb{N}^{\times 2}}\supp_{1}((f_{\underline{t}}\circ\bary)\times\id_{|\mathbf{M}|^{\wedge}})_{*}(F_{{\underline{t}}}^{0}(\mathbf{B}_{\underline{t}})\times\{{\underline{t}}\})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\big\{(z,\lambda,{\underline{t}})\mid(z,{\underline{t}})\in F,(\lambda,{\underline{t}})\in F^{\prime}\big\}.

Thus

\supp_{1}\big(F^{0}_{\mathbf{M}}(\mathbf{B})\big)_{n}\in\mathfrak{D}^{0}_{1}(\mathbf{J}_{{\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.0pt\mathrm{vcy}}}(G)\times\mathbf{M})
by
(7.19a)
.

For
(7.18b)
we need to check

\supp_{2}\big(F^{0}_{\mathbf{M}}(\mathbf{B})\big)_{n}\in\mathfrak{D}^{0}_{2}(\mathbf{J}_{{\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.0pt\mathrm{vcy}}}(G)\times\mathbf{M})
. By
(12.8a)

\supp_{2}(f_{\underline{t}}\times\bary)\subseteq E_{\underline{t}}^{\circ 2}\times\supp_{2}\mathbf{B}_{\underline{t}}.

We now use
E\in\mathfrak{D}_{2}^{0}(\mathbf{J}_{{\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.0pt\mathrm{vcy}}}(G))
from Lemma
13.2
.
Then

\supp_{2}(F^{0}_{\mathbf{M}}(\mathbf{B}))_{n}\;\subseteq\;\Big\{{\begin{pmatrix}{z^{\prime},\lambda^{\prime},{\underline{t}}}\\
{z,\lambda,{\underline{t}}}\end{pmatrix}}\,\Big|\,{\begin{pmatrix}{z^{\prime},{\underline{t}}}\\
{z,{\underline{t}}}\end{pmatrix}}\in E^{\circ 2},{\begin{pmatrix}{\lambda^{\prime},{\underline{t}}}\\
{\lambda,{\underline{t}}}\end{pmatrix}}\in\supp_{2}\mathbf{B}\Big\}

which belongs to
\mathfrak{D}_{2}^{0}(\mathbf{J}_{{\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.0pt\mathrm{vcy}}}(G)\times\mathbf{M})

by
(7.19b)
.

For
(7.18c)
we need to check

\supp_{G}\big(F^{0}_{\mathbf{M}}(\mathbf{B})\big)_{n}\in\mathfrak{D}^{0}_{G}(\mathbf{J}_{{\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.0pt\mathrm{vcy}}}(G)\times\mathbf{M})
. By
(11.17b)
we have

\supp_{G}\big(F^{0}_{\mathbf{M}}(\mathbf{B})\big)_{n}\subseteq\supp_{G}\mathbf{B}
. The latter is
relatively compact as
\mathbf{B}\in\matheurm{D}_{G}^{0}(\mathbf{M})
. Thus

\supp_{G}\big(F^{0}_{\mathbf{M}}(\mathbf{B})\big)_{n}
is relatively compact as well.

Finally, the finiteness condition
(7.18d)

for
\big(F^{0}_{\mathbf{M}}(\mathbf{B})\big)_{n}

follows from
(11.17a)
as
the
\mathbf{B}_{{\underline{t}}}
are finite.

(b)
We need to
verify
(7.18e)

and
(7.18f)
for
\partial_{n}^{F^{0}_{\mathbf{M}}(\mathbf{B})}
.
This can be done exactly in the same way as for the corresponding
properties
(7.18b)

and
(7.18c)
for
\big(F^{0}_{\mathbf{M}}(\mathbf{B})\big)_{n}

under
(a)
.
For
(7.18f)
this
uses
(11.17c)

in place of
(11.17b)
.

(c)
We need to
verify
(7.18e)

and
(7.18f)
for

F_{\mathbf{M}}^{l}(\varphi_{1},\dots,\varphi_{l})
.

Choose
k_{0}
such that
\supp_{G}\varphi_{i}\subseteq M_{k_{0}}
for
i=1,\dots,l
. Since we
are working in
\matheurm{D}^{0}_{G}(\mathbf{J}_{{\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.0pt\mathrm{vcy}}}(G)\times\mathbf{M})
we can ignore all

{\underline{t}}=(t_{0},t_{1})
with
t_{0}\leq k_{0}
. Thus for the rest of the argument
we can and will always assume
t_{0}\geq k_{0}
. From
(12.8b)
we obtain
that

\supp_{G}\big(((f_{\underline{t}}\bary)\times\id_{|\mathbf{M}|^{\wedge}})_{*}(F_{\underline{t}}^{l})\big((\varphi_{1})_{\underline{t}},\dots,(\varphi_{l})_{\underline{t}}\big)\big)\subseteq M_{k_{0}}^{2l+1}

for all
{\underline{t}}
. In particular, the union of these
G
-supports is
relatively compact in
G
. Thus

\supp_{G}F_{\mathbf{M}}^{l}(\varphi_{1},\dots,\varphi_{l})\in\mathfrak{D}_{G}^{0}(\mathbf{J}_{{\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.0pt\mathrm{vcy}}}(G)\times\mathbf{M})
as required by
(7.18f)
.

Set

E^{\prime}_{\underline{t}}:=\bigcup_{j}\supp_{2}((\varphi_{j})_{\underline{t}})\cup\bigcup_{j}\supp_{2}((\mathbf{B}_{j})_{\underline{t}})
. From
(12.8b)
we obtain

(13.4)

\supp_{2}(((f_{\underline{t}}\circ\bary)\times\id_{|\mathbf{M}|^{\wedge}})_{*}(F_{\underline{t}}^{l})\big((\varphi_{1})_{\underline{t}},\dots,(\varphi_{l})_{\underline{t}}\big)\\
\subseteq\Big\{{\begin{pmatrix}{z^{\prime},\lambda^{\prime}}\\
{z,\lambda}\end{pmatrix}}\Big|{\begin{pmatrix}{z^{\prime}}\\
{z}\end{pmatrix}}\in\big(M_{k_{0}}^{2l}\cdot E_{\underline{t}}^{\circ 3}\big)^{\circ(2l+1)},{\begin{pmatrix}{\lambda^{\prime}}\\
{\lambda}\end{pmatrix}}\in\big(M_{k_{0}}^{2l}\cdot E^{\prime}_{\underline{t}}\big)^{\circ(2l+1)}\Big\}.

We use again
E\in\mathfrak{D}_{2}^{0}(\mathbf{J}_{{\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.0pt\mathrm{vcy}}}(G))
from Lemma
13.2

and set

\displaystyle\tilde{E}:=\big(M_{k_{0}}^{2l}\cdot E^{\circ 3}\big)^{\circ(2l+1)}\in\mathfrak{D}_{2}^{0}(\mathbf{J}_{{\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.0pt\mathrm{vcy}}}(G)).

We also have, as the
\varphi_{j}
and the
\mathbf{B}_{j}
are from

\matheurm{D}^{0}_{G,\mathbf{U}}(\mathbf{M})
,

E^{\prime}:=\Big\{{\begin{pmatrix}{\lambda^{\prime},{\underline{t}}}\\
{\lambda,{\underline{t}}}\end{pmatrix}}\;\Big|\;{\begin{pmatrix}{\lambda^{\prime}}\\
{\lambda}\end{pmatrix}}\in E^{\prime}_{\underline{t}}\Big\}\in\mathfrak{D}_{2}^{0}(\mathbf{M})

and also
\tilde{E}^{\prime}:=(M_{k_{0}}^{2l}\cdot E^{\prime})^{\circ(2l+1)}\in\mathfrak{D}_{2}^{0}(\mathbf{M})
.
Now (
13.4
) implies

\supp_{2}F_{\mathbf{M}}^{l}(\varphi_{1},\dots,\varphi_{l})\subseteq\Big\{{\begin{pmatrix}{z^{\prime},\lambda^{\prime},{\underline{t}}}\\
{z,\lambda,{\underline{t}}}\end{pmatrix}}\;\Big|\;{\begin{pmatrix}{z^{\prime},{\underline{t}}}\\
{z,{\underline{t}}}\end{pmatrix}}\in\tilde{E},{\begin{pmatrix}{\lambda^{\prime},{\underline{t}}}\\
{\lambda,{\underline{t}}}\end{pmatrix}}\in\tilde{E}^{\prime}\Big\}.

The latter belongs to
\mathfrak{D}_{2}^{0}(\mathbf{J}_{{\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.0pt\mathrm{vcy}}}(G)\times\mathbf{M})

by
(7.19b)
. Thus

\supp_{2}F_{\mathbf{M}}^{l}(\varphi_{1},\dots,\varphi_{l})\in\mathfrak{D}_{2}^{0}(\mathbf{J}_{{\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.0pt\mathrm{vcy}}}(G)\times\mathbf{M})
as required by
(7.18e)
.
∎

Conclusion of the proof of Theorem
6.7
.

We have the following diagram.

\textstyle{\matheurm{D}^{0}_{G,\mathbf{U}}(\mathbf{M})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{I}
\scriptstyle{F_{\mathbf{M}}}
\textstyle{\ch_{{\mathrm{fin}}}\matheurm{D}^{0}_{G}(\mathbf{J}_{{\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.0pt\mathrm{vcy}}}(G)\times\mathbf{M})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(\pr_{\mathbf{M}})_{*}}
\textstyle{\ch_{{\mathrm{fin}}}\matheurm{D}^{0}_{G}(\mathbf{M})}

The functor
(\pr_{\mathbf{M}})_{*}
is induced by the projection

\mathbf{J}_{{\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.0pt\mathrm{vcy}}}(G)\times\mathbf{M}\to\mathbf{M}
,
F_{\mathbf{M}}
is the homotopy
coherent functor defined above (well-defined by Lemma
13.3
), and
I
is
the inclusion. We can then apply K-theory and obtain

\textstyle{\mathbf{K}\big(\matheurm{D}^{0}_{G,\mathbf{U}}(\mathbf{M})\big)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathbf{K}(I)}
\scriptstyle{\mathbf{K}(F_{\mathbf{M}})}
\textstyle{\mathbf{K}\big(\matheurm{D}^{0}_{G}(\mathbf{J}_{{\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.0pt\mathrm{vcy}}}(G)\times\mathbf{M})\big)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathbf{p}_{\mathbf{M}}=\mathbf{K}((\pr_{\mathbf{M}})_{*})}
\textstyle{\mathbf{K}\big(\matheurm{D}^{0}_{G}(\mathbf{M})\big).}

Strictly speaking the homotopy coherent functor
F_{\mathbf{M}}
induces a zig-zag in
K-theory, see Remark
C.24
. Since

\matheurm{D}^{0}_{G,\mathbf{U}}(\mathbf{M})\big)\to\matheurm{D}^{0}_{G}(\mathbf{M})
induces an equivalence of
idempotent completions, compare Subsection
11.f
, the map
\mathbf{K}(I)
is a
weak equivalence. It is not difficult to see from the construction that the diagram is
natural in
\mathbf{M}
, basically because the tensor product functor is natural for
induced functors in both entries. There is a strict natural transformation

\tau_{\mathbf{M}}\colon(\pr_{\mathbf{M}})_{*}\circ F_{\mathbf{M}}\to I
by weak equivalences.
It is given by applying the construction from Subsection
11.h
for each

t\in\mathbb{N}^{\times 2}
, see Lemma
11.19
. On each object

\tau_{\mathbf{M}}
evaluates to a chain homotopy equivalence, see
Lemma
11.20
. It follows that

(\pr_{\mathbf{M}})_{*}\circ F_{\mathbf{M}}
and
I
induce homotopic maps in K-theory, see
Remark
C.26
.
Altogether,

\textstyle{\mathbf{K}\big(\matheurm{D}^{0}_{G}(\mathbf{M})\big)}
\textstyle{\mathbf{K}\big(\matheurm{D}^{0}_{G,\mathbf{U}}(\mathbf{M})\big)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathbf{K}(I)}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\scriptstyle{\mathbf{K}(F_{\mathbf{M}})}
\textstyle{\mathbf{K}\big(\matheurm{D}^{0}_{G}(\mathbf{J}_{{\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.0pt\mathrm{vcy}}}(G)\times\mathbf{M})\big)}

is the required section
\mathbf{tr}_{\mathbf{M}}
for
\mathbf{p}_{\mathbf{M}}
.
∎

This concludes the proof of the
{\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.0pt\mathrm{vcy}}
-Farrell–Jones Conjecture for reductive
p
-adic
groups, i.e., of Theorem
5.15
.

14.
Reduction from
{\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.0pt\mathrm{vcy}}
to
{\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.0pt\mathrm{om}}

Theorem 14.1

(Reduction)
.

Let
G
be a td-group and let
\mathcal{B}
be Hecke category with
G
-support satisfying (Reg) from
Definition
3.11
.
Assume that the
{\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.0pt\mathrm{vcy}}
-assembly
map (
5.11
) for
\mathcal{B}

\hocolimunder_{P\in\matheurm{P}{\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.0pt\mathrm{vcy}}(G)}\mathbf{K}\big(\matheurm{C}_{G}(P)\big)\;\to\;\mathbf{K}\big(\matheurm{C}_{G}(\ast)\big)

is an equivalence. Then the
{\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.0pt\mathrm{op}}
-assembly map (
3.9
) for

\mathcal{B}

\hocolimunder_{G/U\in\matheurm{Or}_{{\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.0pt\mathrm{op}}}(G)}\mathbf{K}\big(\mathcal{B}[G/U]\big)\to\mathbf{K}\big(\mathcal{B}[G/G]\big)\simeq\mathbf{K}\mathcal{B}

is also an equivalence.

Modulo further results the proof of Theorem
14.1
will be given in
Subsection
14.b
.

14.
a
.
Functoriality in the orbit category

Recall that the definition of
\matheurm{C}_{G}(P)
used the (free)
G
-space
|P|^{\wedge}
. The
construction of this space is not functorial for
P\in\matheurm{P}\hskip-2.5pt_{+}\hskip-1.2pt\matheurm{Or}(G)
, only for

P\in\matheurm{P}\hskip-2.5pt_{+}\hskip-1.2pt{\mathcal{A}\hskip-0.3pt\mathrm{ll}}(G)
. For this reason,
\matheurm{C}_{G}(P)
is not functorial in

\matheurm{P}\hskip-2.5pt_{+}\hskip-1.2pt\matheurm{Or}(G)
. But this is not a serious issue and we will now tweak the definitions to
remedy this.

Let
\mathcal{B}
be a category with
G
-support and
\mathfrak{E}=(\mathfrak{E}_{1},\mathfrak{E}_{2},\mathfrak{E}_{G})
be a

G
-control structure on
X
. Let
\mathcal{Y}
be a collection of subsets of
X
as in
Definition
4.19
. We say that two maps
\pi,\rho\colon S\to X
are

\mathfrak{E}_{2}
-equivalent
if

\Big\{{\begin{pmatrix}{\rho(s)}\\
{\pi(s)}\end{pmatrix}}\;\Big|\;s\in S\Big\}\in\mathfrak{E}_{2}.

In the definition of
\mathcal{B}_{G}(\mathfrak{E},\mathcal{Y})
we can replace the maps

\pi\colon S\to X
with their
\mathfrak{E}_{2}
-equivalence classes and obtain the category

\overline{\mathcal{B}}_{G}(\mathfrak{E},\mathcal{Y})
. In more detail, objects of

\overline{\mathcal{B}}_{G}(\mathfrak{E},\mathcal{Y})
are triples
\mathbf{B}=(S,[\pi],B)
such that

(S,\pi,B)
is an object of
\mathcal{B}_{G}(\mathfrak{E},\mathcal{Y})
. The point here is
that, if
\pi
and
\rho
are
\mathfrak{E}_{2}
-equivalent, then
(S,\pi,B)
is an object of

\mathcal{B}_{G}(\mathfrak{E},\mathcal{Y})
, iff
(S,\rho,B)
is an object of

\mathcal{B}_{G}(\mathfrak{E},\mathcal{Y})
. A morphism

\mathbf{B}=(S,[\pi],B)\to\mathbf{B}^{\prime}=(S^{\prime},[\pi^{\prime}],B^{\prime})
in

\overline{\mathcal{B}}_{G}(\mathfrak{E},\mathcal{Y})
is a matrix

\varphi=(\varphi^{s^{\prime}}_{s}\colon B(s)\to B^{\prime}(s^{\prime}))_{s\in S,s^{\prime}\in S^{\prime}}
of morphisms in

\mathcal{B}
that also defines a morphism
(S,\pi,B)\to(S^{\prime},\pi^{\prime},B^{\prime})
in

\mathcal{B}_{G}(\mathfrak{E},\mathcal{Y})
. Again, the point here is that, if
\pi
and

\rho
are
\mathfrak{E}_{2}
-equivalent,
\pi^{\prime}
and
\rho^{\prime}
are
\mathfrak{E}_{2}
-equivalent, then
\varphi

defines a morphism in
(S,\pi,B)\to(S^{\prime},\pi^{\prime},B^{\prime})
in

\mathcal{B}_{G}(\mathfrak{E},\mathcal{Y})
, iff
\varphi
defines a morphism in

(S,\rho,B)\to(S^{\prime},\rho^{\prime},B^{\prime})
in
\mathcal{B}_{G}(\mathfrak{E},\mathcal{Y})
. The functor

(14.2)

\mathcal{B}_{G}(\mathfrak{E},\mathcal{Y})\xrightarrow{\sim}\overline{\mathcal{B}}_{G}(\mathfrak{E},\mathcal{Y}),\quad(S,\pi,B)\mapsto(S,[\pi],B)

is an equivalence. Essentially,
\overline{\mathcal{B}}_{G}(\mathfrak{E},\mathcal{Y})
is obtained
from
\mathcal{B}_{G}(\mathfrak{E},\mathcal{Y})
by identifying
(S,\pi,B)
and
(S,\rho,B)

along a canonical isomorphism, whenever
\pi
and
\rho
are
\mathfrak{E}_{2}
-equivalent.

Recall
\matheurm{C}_{G}(P)=\mathcal{B}_{G}(\mathfrak{C}(P),\mathcal{Y}(P))
from
Definition
5.9
. We now define

\overline{\matheurm{C}}_{G}(P):=\overline{\mathcal{B}}_{G}(\mathfrak{C}(P),\mathcal{Y}(P))
.
From (
14.2
) we obtain a natural equivalence

(14.3)

\matheurm{C}_{G}(P)\xrightarrow{\sim}\overline{\matheurm{C}}_{G}(P).

Let
f\colon P\to P^{\prime}
be a map in
\matheurm{P}\hskip-2.5pt_{+}\hskip-1.2pt\matheurm{Or}G
. It induces a map

|f|\colon|P|\to|P^{\prime}|
. There is always a lift of
|f|
to

|P|^{\wedge}\to|P^{\prime}|^{\wedge}
, but it is typically not unique.

Lemma 14.4
.

Let

\widehat{f}_{0},\widehat{f}_{1}\colon|P|^{\wedge}\to|P|^{\wedge}
be lifts of
|f|
. Then

\widehat{f}_{0}\times\id_{\mathbb{N}},\widehat{f}_{1}\times\id_{\mathbb{N}}\colon|P|^{\wedge}\times\mathbb{N}\to|P|^{\wedge}\times\mathbb{N}
are
\mathfrak{C}_{2}(P)
-equivalent.

Proof.

To simplify notation, we just treat the case
P=G/H
,
P^{\prime}=G/H^{\prime}
. Then

f=|f|\colon G/H=|G/H|\to G/H^{\prime}=|G/H^{\prime}|
. Any
G
-map
f\colon G/H\to G/H^{\prime}
is
of the form
xH\mapsto xgH^{\prime}
where
g^{-1}Hg\subseteq H^{\prime}
. Now for the two lifts

\widehat{f}_{0},\widehat{f}_{1}\colon G=|G/H|^{\wedge}\to G=|G/H^{\prime}|^{\wedge}
there are

h^{\prime}_{0},h^{\prime}_{1}\in H^{\prime}
with
\widehat{f}_{0}(x)=xgh^{\prime}_{0}
,
\widehat{f}_{1}(x)=xgh^{\prime}_{1}
for all

x\in G
. With
\beta:=d(h^{\prime}_{0},h^{\prime}_{1})
then for all
x\in G

d_{H^{\prime}\text{-}\mathrm{fol}}(\widehat{f}_{0}(x),\widehat{f}_{1}(x))\leq(\beta,0).

This implies that
\widehat{f}_{0}\times\id_{\mathbb{N}},\widehat{f}_{1}\times\id_{\mathbb{N}}
are

\mathfrak{C}_{2}(P)
-equivalent.
∎

Lemma
14.4
implies that the assignment

P\mapsto\overline{\matheurm{C}}_{G}(P)

is functorial in
\matheurm{P}\hskip-2.5pt_{+}\hskip-1.2pt\matheurm{Or}(G)
.
This allows us to consider the bottom row in the following diagram

(14.5)

\textstyle{\displaystyle\hocolimunder_{P\in\matheurm{P}{\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.0pt\mathrm{op}}(G)}\mathbf{K}\big(\matheurm{C}_{G}(P)\big)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\textstyle{\displaystyle\hocolimunder_{P\in\matheurm{P}{\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.0pt\mathrm{vcy}}(G)}\mathbf{K}\big(\matheurm{C}_{G}(P)\big)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\textstyle{\mathbf{K}\big(\matheurm{C}_{G}(\ast)\big)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\textstyle{\displaystyle\hocolimunder_{P\in\matheurm{P}{\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.0pt\mathrm{op}}(G)}\mathbf{K}\big(\overline{\matheurm{C}}_{G}(P)\big)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\textstyle{\displaystyle\hocolimunder_{P\in\matheurm{P}{\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.0pt\mathrm{vcy}}(G)}\mathbf{K}\big(\overline{\matheurm{C}}_{G}(P)\big)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\textstyle{\mathbf{K}\big(\overline{\matheurm{C}}_{G}(\ast)\big)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{=}
\textstyle{\displaystyle\hocolimunder_{P\in\matheurm{P}\matheurm{Or}_{{\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.0pt\mathrm{op}}}(G)}\mathbf{K}\big(\overline{\matheurm{C}}_{G}(P)\big)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\displaystyle\hocolimunder_{P\in\matheurm{P}\matheurm{Or}_{{\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.0pt\mathrm{vcy}}}(G)}\mathbf{K}\big(\overline{\matheurm{C}}_{G}(P)\big)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathbf{K}\big(\overline{\matheurm{C}}_{G}(\ast)\big).}

We claim that the vertical maps in this diagram are all equivalences.
For the first three vertical maps this follows from (
14.3
).
For the first two vertical maps in the bottom row, this is an application of
Lemma
A.6
.

14.
b
.
The category
\matheurm{C}^{+}_{G}(P)

We will need slightly bigger categories than
\matheurm{C}_{G}(P)
and
\overline{\matheurm{C}}_{G}(P)
, where we
relax the condition on the supports in
G
. This will be important in
Subsection
14.h
. For smooth
P
this will not change the
K-theory, but we do not know this for general
P
.

Define the
G
-control structure

\mathfrak{C}^{+}(P)=(\mathfrak{C}^{+}_{1}(P),\mathfrak{C}^{+}_{2}(P),\mathfrak{C}^{+}_{G}(P))
by

\mathfrak{C}^{+}_{1}(P):=\mathfrak{C}_{1}(P)
,
\mathfrak{C}^{+}_{2}(P):=\mathfrak{C}_{2}(P)
and

\mathfrak{C}^{+}_{G}(P):=\text{All subsets of $G$}.

We define

\matheurm{C}^{+}_{G}(P):=\mathcal{B}_{G}(\mathfrak{C}^{+}(P),\mathcal{Y}(P))\quad\text{and}\quad\overline{\matheurm{C}}^{+}_{G}(P):=\overline{\mathcal{B}}_{G}(\mathfrak{C}^{+}(P),\mathcal{Y}(P)).

As
\mathfrak{C}_{G}(P)\subseteq\mathfrak{C}^{+}_{G}(P)
, there are inclusions

\matheurm{C}_{G}(P)\to\matheurm{C}^{+}_{G}(P)
and
\overline{\matheurm{C}}_{G}(P)\to\overline{\matheurm{C}}^{+}_{G}(P)
.

Lemma 14.6
.

Suppose that
P
is smooth, i.e., that
P\in\matheurm{P}\hskip-2.5pt_{+}\hskip-1.2pt{\mathcal{O}\mathrm{p}}(G)
. Then the inclusions

\matheurm{C}_{G}(P)\to\matheurm{C}^{+}_{G}(P)
and
\overline{\matheurm{C}}_{G}(P)\to\overline{\matheurm{C}}^{+}_{G}(P)

induce equivalences in K-theory.

Proof.

Proposition
5.16
holds, with exactly the same proof, for

\matheurm{C}^{+}_{G}(P)
in place of
\matheurm{C}_{G}(P)
as well
64
64

64

This works in the generality of
categories with
G
-support in place of
\mathcal{H}_{G}(\mathcal{A})
.
. This implies the
result for
\matheurm{C}_{G}(P)\to\matheurm{C}^{+}_{G}(P)
.
For
\overline{\matheurm{C}}_{G}(P)\to\overline{\matheurm{C}}^{+}_{G}(P)
it follows now from (
14.2
).
∎

Theorem 14.7
.

Suppose that
\mathcal{B}
satisfies (Reg) from
Definition
3.11
. Then for all

P\in\matheurm{P}\matheurm{Or}_{\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.0pt\mathrm{vcy}}(G)
the canonical map

(14.8)

\hocolimunder_{(Q,f)\in\matheurm{P}\matheurm{Or}_{\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.0pt\mathrm{om}}(G)\downarrow P}\mathbf{K}\big(\overline{\matheurm{C}}^{+}_{G}(Q)\big)\;\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathbf{K}\big(\overline{\matheurm{C}}^{+}_{G}(P)\big)

is an equivalence.

The proof of Theorem
14.7
will be given later. We do
not know whether or not Theorem
14.7
holds also for

\overline{\matheurm{C}}_{G}(\--)
instead of
\overline{\matheurm{C}}^{+}_{G}(\--)
. We note that for
Theorem
14.7
it is important that we used

\matheurm{P}\matheurm{Or}_{\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.0pt\mathrm{om}}(G)
; in contrast the category
\matheurm{P}{\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.0pt\mathrm{om}}(G)\downarrow P
is typically empty.

Proof of Theorem
14.1
modulo
Theorem
14.7
.

Consider the following diagram.

\textstyle{\displaystyle\hocolimunder_{P\in\matheurm{P}\matheurm{Or}_{\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.0pt\mathrm{op}}(G)}\mathbf{K}\big(\overline{\matheurm{C}}_{G}(P)\big)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\scriptstyle{\alpha_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\textstyle{\displaystyle\hocolimunder_{P\in\matheurm{P}\matheurm{Or}_{\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.0pt\mathrm{op}}(G)}\mathbf{K}\big(\overline{\matheurm{C}}^{+}_{G}(P)\big)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\widehat{\alpha}_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\textstyle{\displaystyle\hocolimunder_{P\in\matheurm{P}\matheurm{Or}_{\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.0pt\mathrm{om}}(G)}\mathbf{K}\big(\overline{\matheurm{C}}_{G}(P)\big)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\alpha_{2}}
\textstyle{\displaystyle\hocolimunder_{P\in\matheurm{P}\matheurm{Or}_{\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.0pt\mathrm{om}}(G)}\mathbf{K}\big(\overline{\matheurm{C}}^{+}_{G}(P)\big)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\widehat{\alpha}_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\textstyle{\displaystyle\hocolimunder_{P\in\matheurm{P}\matheurm{Or}_{\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.0pt\mathrm{vcy}}(G)}\mathbf{K}\big(\overline{\matheurm{C}}_{G}(P)\big)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathit{FJ}}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\textstyle{\displaystyle\hocolimunder_{P\in\matheurm{P}\matheurm{Or}_{\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.0pt\mathrm{vcy}}(G)}\mathbf{K}\big(\overline{\matheurm{C}}^{+}_{G}(P)\big)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathbf{K}\big(\overline{\matheurm{C}}_{G}(\ast)\big)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\textstyle{\mathbf{K}\big(\overline{\matheurm{C}}^{+}_{G}(\ast)\big)}

We will argue below that all maps labeled with an
\sim
are equivalences. A diagram
chase shows then that all other maps in the diagram are equivalences as well. In
particular the left vertical composition is an equivalence.
Proposition
3.13
in combination with the equivalences
from (
14.5
) implies that this vertical composition is
equivalent to (
3.9
).

The analog of the map labeled
\mathit{FJ}
for
\matheurm{C}_{G}(P)
instead of

\overline{\matheurm{C}}_{G}(P)
and
\matheurm{P}{\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.0pt\mathrm{vcy}}(G)
instead of
\matheurm{P}\matheurm{Or}_{\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.0pt\mathrm{vcy}}(G)
is an equivalence by
assumption of Theorem
14.1
. The equivalences
from (
14.5
) imply that the map labeled
\mathit{FJ}
is an
equivalence as well.

Lemma
14.6
implies that the top and bottom horizontal maps are
equivalences. Any compact subgroup of a td-group is contained in a compact open
subgroup, see Lemma
2.3
. This implies that for all

P\in\matheurm{P}{\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.0pt\mathrm{om}}(G)
the category
P\downarrow\matheurm{P}{\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.0pt\mathrm{op}}(G)
is non-empty. Thus
Lemma
A.6
implies that
\alpha_{1}
and
\widehat{\alpha}_{1}

are equivalences.

The map
\widehat{\alpha}_{2}
is an equivalence by
Theorem
14.7
and the transitivity
Lemma
A.2
for homotopy colimits.
∎

14.
c
.
The category
\overline{\matheurm{C}}^{+,0}_{G}(P)

To prove Theorem
14.7
we introduce a further category
as an intermediate step. In many ways this is similar to the passage from

\matheurm{D}_{G}({\bf\Sigma};\mathcal{B})
to
\matheurm{D}^{0}_{G}({\bf\Sigma};\mathcal{B})
, see
Definition
7.15
.

For
P\in\matheurm{P}\hskip-2.5pt_{+}\hskip-1.2pt{\mathcal{A}\hskip-0.3pt\mathrm{ll}}(G)
we define the
G
-control structure

\mathfrak{C}^{+,0}(P)=(\mathfrak{C}^{+,0}_{1}(P),\mathfrak{C}^{+,0}_{2}(P),\mathfrak{C}^{+,0}_{G}(P))

as follows. Set

\mathfrak{C}^{+,0}_{1}(P):=\mathfrak{C}^{+}_{1}(P)=\mathfrak{C}_{1}(P)
,

\mathfrak{C}^{+,0}_{G}(P):=\widehat{\mathfrak{C}}_{G}(P)
. We define
\mathfrak{C}^{+,0}_{2}(P)
to consist of all

E\in\mathfrak{C}^{+}_{2}(P)=\mathfrak{C}_{2}(P)
satisfying

\qquad{\begin{pmatrix}{\lambda^{\prime},t^{\prime}}\\
{\lambda,t}\end{pmatrix}}\in E\;\implies\;{t}^{\prime}={t}.

Set

\matheurm{C}^{+,0}_{G}(P):=\mathcal{B}_{G}(\mathfrak{C}^{+,0}(P),\mathcal{Y}(P))\quad\text{and}\quad\overline{\matheurm{C}}^{+,0}_{G}(P):=\overline{\mathcal{B}}_{G}(\mathfrak{C}^{+,0}(P),\mathcal{Y}(P)).

Proposition 14.9
.

There are homotopy pushout diagrams of functors
\matheurm{P}\hskip-2.5pt_{+}\hskip-1.2pt{\mathcal{A}\hskip-0.3pt\mathrm{ll}}(G)\to\matheurm{Spectra}

\textstyle{\mathbf{K}\big(\matheurm{C}^{+,0}_{G}(\--)\big)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathbf{K}\big(\matheurm{C}^{+,0}_{G}(\--)\big)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathbf{K}\big(\overline{\matheurm{C}}^{+,0}_{G}(\--)\big)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathbf{K}\big(\overline{\matheurm{C}}^{+,0}_{G}(\--)\big)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathbf{K}\big(\matheurm{C}^{+,0}_{G}(\--)\big)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathbf{K}\big(\matheurm{C}^{+}_{G}(\--)\big)}
\textstyle{\mathbf{K}\big(\overline{\matheurm{C}}^{+,0}_{G}(\--)\big)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathbf{K}\big(\overline{\matheurm{C}}^{+}_{G}(\--)\big)}

Proof.

For the diagram on the left this is analog to the proof of Proposition
8.9
, more precisely to
the construction of either of the pushout squares from Lemma
8.11
.
For the diagram on the right it follows now from (
14.2
).
∎

Proposition
14.9
implies that it suffices to prove
Theorem
14.7
for
\overline{\matheurm{C}}^{+,0}_{G}(\--)
in place of

\overline{\matheurm{C}}^{+}_{G}(\--)
.
